# Fact-check log

## Events
- 1980-guadalajara-cartel-consolidation - Avilés death (15 Sep 1978, shootout with federal police, Culiacán) confirmed; 'launched 1977' clarified for Operation Condor; source swapped to Avilés Wikipedia entry - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Avilés_Pérez
- 1984-manuel-buendia-murder - place refined to Insurgentes/Reforma intersection at edge of Zona Rosa; 'shot four times in the back'; gunman's full name Juan Rafael Moro Ávila and 'convicted as material author' (identity still disputed); Zorrilla arrested 1989, sentenced 35 years February 1993 (confirmed); source added - es.wikipedia Manuel Buendía; proceso.com.mx 2023/9/20
- 1984-rancho-bufalo-raid - coordinates refined to 27.2994,-105.1736 (Colonia Búfalo, Allende) and municipality confirmed; importance 5->4 (trigger event; the Camarena murder is the defining moment); source added - es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_Búfalo
- 1985-caro-quintero-guadalajara-airport-escape - bribe restated as ~60 million pesos check (about US$275,000-300,000); added 17 March departure to Costa Rica; Pavón Reyes aftermath softened to 'later dismissed and prosecuted' - es.wikipedia Rafael Caro Quintero (17 March 1985 departure)
- 1985-operation-camarena-border-slowdown - removed Ronald Reagan from figures (von Raab ordered the operation); '15 ports' hedged as 'reported'; importance 4->3 - borderlandbeat.com 2013/08; dailycaller 2018/10/29
- 1985-el-mareno-bravo-family-massacre - date 2 March 1985 confirmed; municipality Vista Hermosa confirmed; added two 11-year-old survivors; 'staged to plant bodies' hedged to 'suspected'; coordinates set to La Angostura 20.1964,-102.4417; source added - zetatijuana.com; partidero.com; es.wikipedia La Angostura (Michoacán)
- 1985-camarena-body-found-la-angostura - added 'road toward Zamora' and identification in Guadalajara morgue on 7 March; coordinates aligned to La Angostura 20.1964,-102.4417 - zetatijuana.com; es.wikipedia La Angostura (Michoacán)
- 1985-diaz-parada-arrest-oaxaca - escape timing hedged (Milenio: ~3 months after sentence; CNDH Rec. 15/1990: days later); 'board a flight' detail confirmed; CNDH source added - milenio.com cartel-istmo; cndh.org.mx REC_1990_015
- 1985-caro-quintero-arrest-costa-rica - added Quinta La California, San Rafael de Ojo de Agua, Alajuela; Calderoni's role and Sara Cosío's 2 April phone call; departure from Guadalajara 17 March; importance 5->4; sources updated - es.wikipedia Rafael Caro Quintero; nacion.com; historica/qcostarica summaries
- 1985-fonseca-carrillo-arrest-puerto-vallarta - release date specified as 5 April 2025 after completing 40-year sentence; sources replaced with 2025 coverage - mexiconewsdaily.com; bostonglobe.com 2025/04/09
- 1985-dfs-dissolved - 29 Nov 1985 and successor DISEN confirmed; no substantive change - en.wikipedia DFS
- 1986-matta-ballesteros-escapes-bogota-prison - Cartagena arrest April 1985 'at DEA's request' and March 1986 La Picota escape confirmed - en.wikipedia Juan Matta-Ballesteros
- 1986-ontiveros-el-grenas-arrest-juarez - 'eleven associates' (unverified) changed to 'several associates'; release date 27 May 1988 and rearrest 9 Jan 1989 confirmed; 'served 15 years' corrected to 'remained imprisoned until 2007' (Infobae: freed March 2007 on magistrate's order; El Diario says 15 years/2008); Herald-Post affiliation of Al Gutiérrez added; Infobae source added - eldiariodechihuahua.mx 2025/apr/29; infobae.com 2025/04/30
- 1986-helms-hearings-von-raab-mexico-corruption - 13 May 1986 accusation against Gov. Félix Valdés and US apology confirmed; 'ingrained corruption' testimony was given in hearings earlier that month, wording adjusted - en.wikipedia William von Raab
- 1987-garcia-abrego-gulf-cartel-cali-cocaine - added explicit note that 1987 is approximate ('by the late 1980s') - existing sources
- 1987-acapulco-plaza-division-meeting - added that Félix Gallardo has denied the meeting; dating hedged - existing sources
- 1987-pablo-acosta-killed-santa-elena - 'FBI escort' changed to 'FBI assistance'; hour-long battle hedged as 'reported' - en.wikipedia Pablo Acosta Villarreal
- 1987-javier-caro-payan-arrested-montreal - date set to 6 June 1987 (precision day); wife Yolanda named; extradition 19 April 1988, San Diego prosecution, 25-year sentence added; impact corrected: killed in Guadalajara on Ramón Arellano Félix's orders after trying to reclaim the plaza around 1996 - infobae.com 2024/01/03; upi.com 1988/02/11
- 1987-judge-villafuerte-murdered-cuernavaca - date 20 Sep 1987 confirmed by CNDH Rec. 15/1990; added Colonia Reforma, transfer for safety, killers were June 1987 Morelos prison escapees, 'balazo por año' note; '.380 revolvers' changed to '.380-caliber handguns' (casings recovered); confidence medium->high; CNDH source added - cndh.org.mx REC_1990_015; milenio.com
- 1988-matta-ballesteros-seized-tegucigalpa - 5 April 1988, Dominican Republic route and five deaths confirmed; annex burning rephrased as 'in the following two days'; noted 2017 vacatur of kidnapping conviction; importance 4->3 - insightcrime.org profile; en.wikipedia Juan Matta-Ballesteros
- 1988-first-camarena-trial-los-angeles - date changed from year 1988 to September 1988 (precision month) after an eight-week trial; Félix Gutiérrez convicted 28 Sep 1988; sentences specified (life plus 240 years for Verdugo and López Álvarez, 10 years for Félix Gutiérrez); UPI sources added - upi.com 1988/09/28; upi.com 1990/07/31 chronology (search snippets; pages themselves blocked)
- 1988-amezcua-colima-cartel-founded - DEA 1988 founding and Guadalajara base confirmed; ephedrine sourcing reworded to India/Thailand then Europe per DEA - en.wikipedia Colima Cartel
- 1988-salinas-inauguration-coello-trejo - importance 4->3 (political framing event rather than defining crime event)
- 1989-arellano-felix-take-tijuana-plaza - 'uncle by marriage ties' changed to 'mentor and reputed relative' (kinship disputed); 'moved to Tijuana in the mid-1980s' corrected: Benjamín relocated in the 1970s per Infobae - infobae.com 2024/01/03
- 1989-palma-family-murders-clavel - Clavel described as sent by Félix Gallardo and the Arellano Félix brothers (sources name both); US$2 million figure and hotel detail added; bridge name hedged (La Concordia vs 'Viaducto Viejo'); year hedged (most say 1989, some 1990); es.wikipedia URL corrected to the actual article title - es.wikipedia Héctor «el Güero» Palma; infobae.com 2023/02/11
- 1989-zuno-arce-arrested-san-antonio - 9 Aug 1989 detention at San Antonio airport by INS on arrival from Mexico, held without bail as material witness, confirmed; 'died in US prison' -> 'died in US custody in 2012' - washingtonpost.com 1989/08/13
- 1989-sylmar-record-cocaine-seizure - 29 Sep 1989, 21.4 tons, US$10 million, 12898 Bradley Street confirmed; added ledger detail about hundreds of tons - en.wikipedia Sylmar; latinamericanstudies.org/mexico/munoz.htm
- FLAG: 1988-first-camarena-trial-los-angeles - exact verdict dates unverified: UPI/court pages returned 403; search snippets indicate convictions in September 1988 (Félix Gutiérrez on 28 Sep) but Verdugo/López Álvarez may have been convicted a few days earlier; month precision used
- FLAG: 1985-caro-quintero-guadalajara-airport-escape - bribe amount and Pavón Reyes's later prosecution rest on secondary accounts (Zeta, Shannon's Desperados); exact figure varies between sources
- FLAG: 1985-operation-camarena-border-slowdown - 'all 15 official crossings' comes from Borderland Beat/Daily Caller; the southwest border had more than 15 ports in 1985, the figure may refer to major crossings; start date (about 16 Feb) not pinned
- FLAG: 1985-diaz-parada-arrest-oaxaca - Milenio says Díaz Parada escaped Ixcotel about three months after the April 1985 sentence; CNDH says a few days later; exact arrest day in March 1985 not found
- FLAG: 1986-ontiveros-el-grenas-arrest-juarez - length of second imprisonment inconsistent across sources (Infobae: released March 2007; El Diario: '15 years', released 2008); the number of associates arrested on 25 April 1986 could not be confirmed (UPI article blocked)
- FLAG: 1986-javier-barba-hernandez-killed-mazatlan - date and place rest on Wikipedia only; no primary source retrieved
- FLAG: 1989-palma-family-murders-clavel - year (1989 vs 1990), bridge name, and who dispatched Clavel (Félix Gallardo vs Arellano Félix) vary across Mexican press accounts; the crime spans San Francisco and Venezuela, only the Venezuelan site is geocoded
- FLAG: 1987-acapulco-plaza-division-meeting - existence and date of the meeting are contested (1987 vs 1989, denied by Félix Gallardo); kept at confidence low
- FLAG: 1985-la-langosta-restaurant-killings - the courtlistener opinion returned blank and the UPI June 1985 article was blocked; details rest on prior research and US v. Vasquez-Velasco as remembered
- FLAG: 1987-garcia-abrego-gulf-cartel-cali-cocaine and 1982-matta-ballesteros-medellin-cocaine-link - both are gradual processes assigned approximate years; treat dates as indicative
- FLAG: 1985-caro-quintero-arrest-costa-rica - coordinates are for Alajuela city; the actual house was in San Rafael de Ojo de Agua (a few km south-east), not separately geocoded
- FLAG: 1988-amezcua-colima-cartel-founded - Manzanillo port role is widely reported but was not confirmed in the sources fetched this session
- 1990-agua-prieta-douglas-tunnel - length corrected from ~60 m to ~80-90 m (270 ft per 99% Invisible; 250-300 ft in other accounts); removed claim that a ton of cocaine was found inside the tunnel: the ton was seized from a truck leaving the Douglas warehouse, which prompted the raid, and the tunnel was empty (99percentinvisible.org/episode/the-tunnel); added front man Camarena Macías and hydraulic pool-table detail; date May 17, 1990 confirmed by Mob Museum and Tucson Weekly index (uamr.lib.arizona.edu/item/250919); confidence lowered to medium because ledger/attribution detail is secondary-source
- 1991-cochiloco-killed-zapopan - added time (midday), gunmen count and exact intersection (Obsidiana at Lapislázuli, Residencial Victoria) per Radio Fórmula/Mural; confidence raised to high since date, place and casualties confirmed
- 1992-caro-quintero-sonora-arrest-release - removed unsupported 'within days' for the dismissal; PBS Frontline and Wikipedia say only that the Hermosillo judge dismissed the tax case after threats and bribes
- 1993-aguilar-guajardo-killed-cancun - added restaurant name (Gypsys), submarine-tour circumstance, tourist's name Georgina Knafel and son's age per Wikipedia
- 1993-bali-hai-attack-amado-carrillo - removed hedge 'some say December' (all located sources give Nov 24, 1993); replaced 'bathroom skylight' with 'back of the restaurant' per El Financiero/Infobae; added two killed; coordinates moved from Zócalo centroid to Insurgentes Sur area (19.3719,-99.1798); replaced one 2025 El Financiero URL with es.wikipedia Tijuana Cartel page
- 1993-francisco-rafael-arellano-arrested - clarified he stayed in Mexican custody until September 2006 extradition, pleaded guilty in US and was released March 2008 (Fox News/Wikipedia); confidence lowered to medium because the 'ten-year sentence' is only weakly sourced given ~13 years served
- 1994-colosio-assassination-tijuana - coordinates moved to Lomas Taurinas neighborhood (32.5085,-117.0622) instead of city centroid
- 1994-benitez-lopez-tijuana-police-chief-killed - softened unverifiable specifics (four gunmen, La Mesa district, pickup) to 'gunmen pulled alongside his vehicle' and 'aide'; UPI and Washington Post pages returned 403 so details could not be re-checked; confidence lowered to medium
- 1995-raul-salinas-arrested - named wife Paulina Castañón and noted Switzerland later returned most funds to Mexico (2008); PBS Frontline confirms Swiss drug-money finding and Gulf Cartel tie
- 1995-mario-ruiz-massieu-arrested-newark - added year of Houston civil verdict (1997)
- 1995-colombian-cartels-fall-power-shift - Miguel Rodríguez Orejuela capture date made exact: August 6, 1995
- 1995-guero-palma-arrested-guadalajara - removed unverified crash date 'June 22'; Wikipedia and El Financiero give only arrest date June 23, 1995 after the crash-landing near Tepic; added Ciudad Obregón origin
- 1995-chapo-transferred-puente-grande - added 'early hours' (received at Cefereso 2 at 1:55 a.m., Nov 22, 1995 per Infobae) and Infobae source
- 1996-ibarra-santes-killed-mexico-city - post corrected: he was the PGR/Federal Judicial Police commander (subdelegate) for Baja California, not an INCD commander (afntijuana.info; La Jornada); killing described as in a taxi shortly after leaving the airport en route to PGR HQ rather than 'outside the airport'; title adjusted; AFN source added
- 1996-garcia-abrego-convicted-houston - sentencing date made exact (January 31, 1997); confidence raised to high
- 1997-gutierrez-rebollo-drug-czar-arrested - sentence stated precisely: combined 71 years, reduced to 40 in 2011 (Proceso/La Jornada obituaries)
- 1997-amado-carrillo-fuentes-dies - 'eight hours of surgery' softened to 'hours' (accounts vary); added Polanco location
- 1997-vicente-carrillo-takes-juarez-cartel - named the Max Fim restaurant in the August 1997 attack
- 1997-amado-surgeons-bodies-found - added names of the other two victims (Carlos Ávila; Colombian Ricardo Reyes Rincón) and 'first days of November' per Borderland Beat/Spokesman-Review (found Sunday/Monday, ~Nov 2-3, 1997)
- 1998-carrillo-olea-morelos-resigns - 'resigned' corrected to 'requested indefinite leave (licencia)' on May 15, 1998 under threat of impeachment; added successor Jorge Morales Barud; La Jornada 1998-05-17 source added; title adjusted
- 1998-operation-casablanca - figures corrected per Treasury release rr2452: operation began November 1995 (not exactly three years), 112 prior arrests plus 30 announced (more than 140 total), $35 million seized with $110 million more targeted (not 'over $100 million seized'), 26 bankers charged, three banks named (Bancomer, Serfin, Confía), 12 of 19 largest banks implicated
- 1998-ensenada-el-sauzal-massacre - added time (4:30 a.m.) and 'pulled from their beds' per CBS/AP
- 1999-villanueva-quintana-roo-governor-flees - sources split between March 27 (Infobae, es.wikipedia biography sites) and March 28 (AP via Deseret News); kept March 28 but summary now says night of March 27-28 and 'about a week' before April 5 (not 'days'); capture date made exact (May 24, 2001), extradition May 8, 2010, guilty plea 2012; confidence lowered to medium
- 1999-osiel-cardenas-kills-chava-gomez - added lure-to-meeting and baptism/godfather context per InSight Crime/Wikipedia; replaced Dallas News standoff URL with Arturo Guzmán Decena Wikipedia page as more relevant source
- FLAG: 1990-agua-prieta-douglas-tunnel - exact discovery day: May 17 (Mob Museum, Tucson Weekly index) vs May 18 in some wire reports; tunnel length reported variously as 250, 270 or 300 ft
- FLAG: 1992-caro-quintero-sonora-arrest-release - could not confirm exact arrest date, city of arrest, or that the judge sat in Hermosillo beyond PBS Frontline; year precision retained
- FLAG: 1993-francisco-rafael-arellano-arrested - 'ten-year sentence' rests on tertiary sources; he served roughly 13 years in Mexico before 2006 extradition, so sentence details may be incomplete
- FLAG: 1994-benitez-lopez-tijuana-police-chief-killed - bomb-threat ruse and companion's identity not re-verified (UPI/WaPo archive pages blocked)
- FLAG: 1995-guero-palma-arrested-guadalajara - whether the Learjet crashed on June 22 or June 23, 1995 and whether arrest was same day remains unresolved
- FLAG: 1997-amado-surgeons-bodies-found - exact discovery day (Nov 2, 3 or 7, 1997) differs among reports; month precision kept
- FLAG: 1999-villanueva-quintana-roo-governor-flees - March 27 vs March 28, 1999 disappearance date is contested between Mexican and AP accounts
- FLAG: 1999-osiel-cardenas-kills-chava-gomez - exact date and precise location of Gómez Herrera's killing (Matamoros vs elsewhere in Tamaulipas) not established; July 1999 is the consensus month
- FLAG: 1999-los-zetas-founded - founding year contested (1997 vs 1999); year precision retained with medium confidence
- FLAG: 1994-colosio-assassination-tijuana - cartel attribution 'government' is a placeholder since no cartel role was ever established; consider 'other' if the schema allows a non-cartel political killing
- FLAG: WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-review; remaining checks relied on WebFetch of specific pages and prior knowledge
- 2000-kingpin-act-first-designations - figure corrected from 'Jesús Amezcua Contreras' to 'José de Jesús Amezcua Contreras', Luis Ignacio added to figures, noted 12 designations of which six Mexican; added Clinton White House fact sheet source - clintonwhitehouse3.archives.gov Kingpin implementation fact sheet lists the 12 names
- 2000-fox-wins-presidency-ends-pri-rule - vote shares refined from 'about 43% to 36%' to 42.5% to 36.1% - official IFE 2000 results
- 2001-el-chapo-escapes-puente-grande - guard's full name given as Francisco Javier Camberos Rivera; 'implicated' softened to 'charged or dismissed' - Wikipedia Manhunt article
- 2001-mario-villanueva-arrested-cancun - added fugitive start date March 28, 1999, extradition May 2010, guilty plea to money laundering and June 2013 sentence of 131 months; added DOJ SDNY source - justice.gov/usao-sdny 131-month sentencing release; Deseret News May 25, 2001
- 2001-el-metro-alcides-ramon-magana-arrested-villahermosa - added that he was arrested by soldiers and federal agents after six-month operation, eight-year fugitive with plastic surgery; sentence year 2007 - La Jornada 14 Jun 2001, El Universal, Proceso 21 Jun 2007
- 2001-afi-created-replaces-federal-judicial-police - impact corrected: AFI was not 'dissolved in 2009'; it was replaced by the Policía Federal Ministerial under a 2009 law and ceased operations in 2012; replaced vague 'Osiel Cárdenas support' with Rosales Mendoza and Z-2 captures - es.wikipedia AFI (decree DOF 1 Nov 2001; transition completed 21 Sep 2012)
- 2001-miguel-caro-quintero-arrested-los-mochis - added Denver guilty plea Oct 23, 2009 and 17-year sentence Feb 4, 2010; 'eight-year wait' corrected to seven years (Dec 2001 to Feb 2009); removed State Dept source that could not be fetched - DOJ Colorado release 23 Oct 2009; en.wikipedia Miguel Caro Quintero
- 2002-sinaloa-federation-alianza-de-sangre-formed - softened 'joined the pact in 2002' to 'was part of the pact' since exact year of Juárez adherence is not documented - no primary source gives a 2002 date
- 2002-ramon-arellano-felix-killed-mazatlan - added that death followed a traffic stop by officer Ángel Antonio Arias Torres, who was also killed; noted FBI Ten Most Wanted since 1997; swapped unreachable State Dept source for Wikipedia - en.wikipedia Ramón Arellano Félix; FBI Ten Most Wanted 2000s list (#451, entry 1997)
- 2002-benjamin-arellano-felix-arrested-puebla - added FBI to intelligence partners and 2012 sentencing year; importance lowered 5 to 4 (major but not a defining national moment on the scale of Fox/Chapo/Calderón) - DOJ release March 2002 names PGR, military, DEA, FBI
- 2002-el-chacho-abducted-killed-zetas-nuevo-laredo - 'corrupt police chief' named as Arturo Pedroza Aguirre; operation leader Víctor Manuel Vázquez Mireles added to figures; noted one gunman killed and that El Alvin's death was staged as a suicide in a Matamoros hotel; seized at his home - en.wikipedia Víctor Manuel Vázquez Mireles; Borderland Beat Oct 2019
- 2002-z1-arturo-guzman-decena-killed-matamoros - 'after a night out' replaced with the sourced account (killed in a restaurant after a neighbor reported his gunmen blocking streets) - en.wikipedia Arturo Guzmán Decena
- 2003-osiel-cardenas-captured-matamoros - removed false claim that he was an FBI Ten Most Wanted fugitive; he does not appear on the 2000s list, though a $2 million US reward existed; 'six-month' operation softened to 'months-long'; extradition month January 2007 added - en.wikipedia FBI Ten Most Wanted Fugitives 2000s; InSight Crime profile
- 2003-nuevo-laredo-war-begins-sinaloa-vs-zetas - 'hundreds of gunmen' softened; August 1, 2003 firefight described per source (AFI clashed with Los Negros fighting Zetas, three dead) and labelled the conventional start - en.wikipedia Sinaloa Cartel-Gulf Cartel conflict
- 2003-armando-valencia-milenio-cartel-arrested-guadalajara - place corrected from Guadalajara to Tlajomulco de Zúñiga (Guadalajara metro), coordinates changed to 20.4736,-103.4431; arresting force corrected to army GAFE (not 'military police'); sentence 47.5 years added; title adjusted to 'near Guadalajara'; Milenio source added - Milenio profile of Valencia Cornelio; Infobae
- 2004-apatzingan-prison-raid-gulf-zetas-free-25 - gunmen/vehicle counts given as ranges (30-40 gunmen, 10-15 vehicles) and noted early reports of up to 29 freed with several recaptured; the one inmate was killed, not merely shot - Al Jazeera 6 Jan 2004 (30 men, 15 vehicles, 29 freed) vs Milenio/SEDENA (40 men, 25 escapees)
- 2004-house-of-death-ciudad-juarez-bodies-exhumed - added killing date range Aug 2003-Jan 2004, Santillán's arrest date Jan 15, 2004 in El Paso (before the dig) and 25-year plea; 'recovered a dozen' softened to 'eventually recovered' since bodies were exhumed over weeks - Mail & Guardian 3 Dec 2006
- 2004-francisco-ortiz-franco-zeta-editor-killed-tijuana - added that he was in his car and that investigators named suspects Arturo Villarreal Albarrán and Jorge Briceño - CPJ June 2006
- 2004-rodolfo-carrillo-fuentes-killed-culiacan - wife's full name Giovanna Quevedo Gastélum; escort was state ministerial police whose commander was badly wounded; claim that testimony established Chapo AND Mayo ordered the hit corrected to Jesús 'El Rey' Zambada's testimony that Chapo ordered it; Mayo removed from figures; importance lowered 5 to 4 - Borderland Beat Nov 2018 trial coverage
- 2004-carlos-rosales-el-tisico-captured-morelia - added detail that he trained about 50 gunmen for the La Palma plot; 'south of Morelia' changed to 'in Morelia' - en.wikipedia Carlos Rosales Mendoza (Oct 24), Milenio (Oct 25)
- 2004-z2-rogelio-gonzalez-pizana-captured-matamoros - added casualties (two suspects and one federal agent killed), grenades thrown, Z-2 shot in the torso, and note that some accounts date the raid to Oct 28; confidence lowered to medium - Borderland Beat Sept 2014 (Oct 28, La Covacha) vs en/es Wikipedia (Oct 29)
- 2004-cancun-afi-agents-killed-zetas - date changed from 2004-11-01 (month) to 2004-11-25 (day), when the nine bodies including the three agents were found near the airport; agent's name corrected from 'Roberto Alcántara Valdez' to 'Roberto Alcántara Pérez'; detentions dated Dec 3 and '24' softened to 'about two dozen' (17 formally taken to Mexico City); added El Universal 26 Nov 2004 source - El Universal 'Ejecutan a nueve; tres eran de la AFI' (26 Nov 2004); El Universal 4 Dec 2004
- 2004-arturo-guzman-loera-el-pollo-killed-la-palma - added 9mm pistol detail; removed unverified 'jailed there since 2002' for the killer; Prison Legal News source added - Prison Legal News Dec 2005
- 2005-matamoros-prison-employees-killed-zetas-reprisal - 'stopped at a roadblock' replaced by sourced account (left work around 6 a.m., found half a mile from the prison in a white Ford Explorer) - Prison Legal News Dec 2005
- 2005-operativo-mexico-seguro-launched - '600 Fuerzas Federales de Apoyo' softened to 'hundreds of federal agents' (exact number not confirmed) and added the June 13 friendly-fire clash with municipal police; announcement confirmed as Saturday June 11 - El Universal 12 Jun 2005
- 2005-la-barbie-zetas-interrogation-execution-video - added that the four were part of nine Zetas seized at a Zihuatanejo nightclub and that attribution rested on captured Zetas commander Mateo Díaz López's testimony - Borderland Beat Sept 2010
- 2006-el-manana-newspaper-attacked-nuevo-laredo - impact corrected: the FEADP was created February 15, 2006, nine days after the attack, not 'two days later'; added 8 p.m. time, Orozco hit five times, and that Mora was stabbed in March 2004 - CPJ 7 Feb 2006 and Attacks on the Press 2006; FEADP acuerdo A/031/06 of 15 Feb 2006
- 2006-acapulco-police-heads-left-at-government-office - linked shootout dated precisely to January 27, 2006, and noted the heads were left at the site of that shootout - NBC/AP 20 Apr 2006
- 2006-javier-arellano-felix-captured-at-sea - kept Aug 14 (DEA/DOJ) but noted Wikipedia gives Aug 16 (announcement date); added ten others arrested, leadership since March 2002, Sept 2007 plea, Nov 5, 2007 life sentence later reduced; DOJ transcript cites 'off La Paz' while DEA release says 'south of Cabo San Lucas' - DOJ 16 Aug 2006 briefing; DEA release via LegiStorm
- 2006-uruapan-five-heads-la-familia-debut - sign text corrected to the full message ('no mata por paga ... esto es justicia divina'); unverified 'seized from a mechanic's shop' softened to 'reportedly abducted the previous day'; importance lowered 5 to 4 - El Universal retrospective; Infobae 31 Oct 2024
- 2006-calderon-inaugurated-president - margin given as 0.56 points and vote share 35.9% - IFE/TEPJF 2006 results
- 2006-operativo-conjunto-michoacan-launched - troop figure changed from 'about 7,000' to '6,500-7,000' (es.wikipedia: ~6,500; Excelsior later cites ~10,000 cumulative); unverified '539 executions, a quarter of national total' replaced by 'more than 500 drug-related executions, among the highest in the country'; title shortened - es.wikipedia Operativo Conjunto Michoacán; Excelsior 12 Dec 2016
- FLAG: 2001-miguel-caro-quintero-arrested-los-mochis - exact arrest day (reported as around Dec 20, 2001) not confirmed; State Dept page returned 403; kept month precision
- FLAG: 2002-el-chacho-abducted-killed-zetas-nuevo-laredo - Wikipedia dates the abduction to May 13, 2002, but Borderland Beat says El Alvin's body (he died of untreated wounds afterwards) was found May 13 in Matamoros, implying the raid may have been a day or more earlier; day precision retained with medium confidence
- FLAG: 2002-ramon-arellano-felix-killed-mazatlan - cremation-before-identification and later DNA confirmation are widely reported but were not re-verified in this pass (NPR source unreachable)
- FLAG: 2003-nuevo-laredo-war-begins-sinaloa-vs-zetas - the August 1, 2003 firefight as 'start of the war' rests on Wikipedia; the war's origin is usually given as 2003 generally
- FLAG: 2004-apatzingan-prison-raid-gulf-zetas-free-25 - contemporary reports disagree on gunmen (30 vs 40), vehicles (15 vs 10) and freed inmates (29 vs 25 net); Al Jazeera's Jan 6 is likely the publication date, event kept at Jan 5
- FLAG: 2004-carlos-rosales-el-tisico-captured-morelia - Oct 24 (Wikipedia) vs Oct 25 (Milenio/SEDENA); kept Oct 24
- FLAG: 2004-z2-rogelio-gonzalez-pizana-captured-matamoros - Oct 28 (Borderland Beat, 1 a.m. raid) vs Oct 29 (Wikipedia); the raid may have straddled midnight
- FLAG: 2004-arturo-guzman-loera-el-pollo-killed-la-palma - 42-year sentence taken from Proceso headline slug; article body unreachable (403)
- FLAG: 2005-operativo-mexico-seguro-launched - the '600 agents' figure and the later extension to Sinaloa and Baja California were not re-verified (Proceso source returned 403); extension is well documented elsewhere
- FLAG: 2006-el-manana-newspaper-attacked-nuevo-laredo - FEADP creation date Feb 15, 2006 is from memory of acuerdo A/031/06; sources reached only confirm 'February 2006'
- FLAG: 2006-javier-arellano-felix-captured-at-sea - capture location given variously as off La Paz (DOJ briefing) and south of Cabo San Lucas (DEA release); coordinates kept off Cabo San Lucas

## Territory
- BCN - 2024-26 note rewritten: the mid-2026 rupture was of the CJNG-Chapitos pact, reported by state security secretary Laureano Carrillo on May 28, 2026 after El Mencho's death (February 22, 2026, Tapalpa), not a generic collapse of the anti-Mayiza alliance; the April statement is dated April 21, 2026 and its municipal breakdown (Rusos in Mexicali, Mayos in Rosarito, Chapitos in Ensenada/San Felipe, Tecate and San Quintín disputed) added - evidence: El Financiero 2026-05-28, Zeta Tijuana 2026-04-21, Wikipedia (El Mencho).
- BCN - the two police killings attributed to Los Rusos dated precisely: FESC intelligence officer and wife killed June 26, 2026 in Tijuana, second FESC officer June 29, 2026 in Mexicali - evidence: Borderland Beat June 2026.
- BCN - Tecate plaza controller 2024-2026 changed from mayiza to sinaloa: state officials describe Tecate as contested between Chapitos-CJNG and Los Mayos with no dominant faction - evidence: Zeta Tijuana 2026-04-21.
- BCN - 2021-23 note: unverified '1,844 homicides in 2023 (96 per 100,000)' replaced with the verifiable CCSPJP 2022 figure (2,058, about 105 per 100,000) and 'close to 2,000 a year' - the 2023 municipal figure could not be confirmed.
- BCN - 2024-25 fact: added Tijuana's CCSPJP 2025 rank (18th) and attributed the counts to SSPCM - evidence: Telemundo 20 2026-01-01, Infobae 2026-02-12.
- BCN - Mexico Peace Index fact tightened from 'more than half' to the reported 55% and attributed to IEP rather than Infobae - evidence: Sinaloahoy/Zeta coverage of IPM 2026, May 2026.
- BCN - August 20, 2026 fentanyl seizure: added that it was a federal security cabinet operation with DEA intelligence, 120 kg / about 4 million doses - evidence: El Financiero, Infobae 2026-08-20.
- BCN - Eduardo Arellano Félix arrest date specified as October 25, 2008; Javier's capture dated August 14, 2006; Puerto Vallarta attack identified as the Christine disco, November 1992; 2012 Tijuana low specified as 364 - standard record.
- BCN - Mexicali plaza 'why' corrected: Los Rusos' presence predates 2019 (descended from El Macho Prieto's network, led by Juan José Ponce Félix 'El Ruso'); 2019 is when they split from Los Chapitos - evidence: guillermoortega.com 2026-08-25, InSight Crime.
- BCN - added fact on El Mencho's death and the CJNG-Chapitos break in Baja California; added El Financiero, Infobae ranking and IPM sources.
- BCS - San José del Cabo shooting that killed a US citizen re-dated from June 2026 to May 31, 2026 (second victim, a 65-year-old woman, died June 3) - evidence: Reforma, El Universal, ABC7 headlines May 31-June 3, 2026.
- BCS - removed unverifiable claim that June 2026 homicides were 48% below June 2025.
- BCS - six bodies hung from bridges dated to December 19-20, 2017 and located in Los Cabos and La Paz (not only near Los Cabos) - evidence: El País, BBC, El Universal 2017-12-20/21.
- BCS - 2025 figure stated as 112 victims (SESNSP) / 113 investigations; 2018 figure 185 added; 2017 count qualified as 560 SESNSP victims with the state's higher tally (~788) noted - evidence: El Sudcaliforniano 2026-01-02, Peninsular Digital 2026-05-27, InSight Crime 2018.
- BCS - 2025-26 note: added the Chapitos local leader '00' (René Bastidas Mercado), the April 22, 2025 killing of commander Ulises Cota, and that the conflict spans all five municipalities per the governor - evidence: Zeta Tijuana May 2025, La Razón 2025-04-24.
- BCS - 2018-2019 intensity lowered from 3 to 2: homicides fell to 185 in 2018 (about 23 per 100,000) and roughly 100 in 2019, comparable to periods rated 2 elsewhere in the dataset.
- BCS - 'Mexico's least violent state' softened to 'among Mexico's least violent states' with the 25-50 murders a year figure - evidence: InSight Crime 2018.
- SON - Rodrigo Páez Quintero's arrest place added (Guadalajara metropolitan area, April 30, 2023); Caro Quintero's capture site specified as San Simón, Choix - evidence: Wikipedia Caborca Cartel.
- SON - 2025-26 note: cell map corrected to the February 2026 mapping (Los Rusos also in Sonoyta, Puerto Peñasco and Caborca; Los Memos as the Chapitos cell in Sonoyta; Beltrán Leyva remnants in Cajeme-Guaymas-Empalme; Chapitos-CJNG bloc in 26 municipalities vs 56 for the rival bloc); Hermosillo's entry into the CCSPJP top 50 added - evidence: Grupo Animal 2026-02-09, El Sol de México 2026-02-17.
- SON - CCSPJP fact corrected: Ciudad Obregón's ninth place for 2025 confirmed at 90.81; the 'third in the 2023 list' claim replaced with second for 2021 (about 156) and top three in 2022-2023 - evidence: Infobae 2026-02-12, Wikipedia list of cities by murder rate.
- SON - 2021 record confirmed at 1,765 homicides (+20% on 2020) - evidence: InSight Crime 2022-01-07; 2024 Hermosillo figure (153) and Guaymas 2025 (15) added - evidence: El Imparcial 2026-01-23.
- SON - 2018-21 note: CJNG presence described as 'limited' in San Luis Río Colorado, Empalme and the south rather than a push; Caborca Cartel allies (La Línea, Chapo Isidro) and Los Cazadores added - evidence: InSight Crime 2022-01-07.
- SON - Gente Nueva dated to about 2007 in the 2002-07 note; Tubutama battle dated July 1, 2010; Caro Quintero release dated August 9, 2013.
- SIN - 2024-26 note corrected on territorial control: per Crisis Group (via La Razón 2026-07-07) La Mayiza holds southern and eastern Culiacán and took Navolato in October 2025 (Milenio 2025-10-24), Los Chapitos lost their advantage in the capital and are concentrated in Mazatlán, and Chapo Isidro's network controls the north (Guasave, Sinaloa de Leyva, Ahome, El Fuerte); the original claim that Chapitos kept 'coastal and northern zones' was wrong.
- SIN - 2024-26 secondary changed from [chapitos, mayiza, cjng] to [chapitos, mayiza, beltran_leyva] because Chapo Isidro's Beltrán Leyva remnant controls territory inside the state while CJNG acts only as an outside ally (kept in the note).
- SIN - Los Mochis plaza: 'anchors the Chapitos' northern zone' corrected; controller 2026 set to beltran_leyva (Chapo Isidro consolidated Ahome) - evidence: Crisis Group via La Razón 2026-07-07.
- SIN - Navolato plaza controller 2026 set to mayiza (seized from Los Chapitos October 2025) - evidence: Milenio 2025-10-24.
- SIN - Mazatlán plaza controller 2026 set to chapitos (their main concentration after losing Culiacán ground) - evidence: Crisis Group via La Razón 2026-07-07; El País 2026-08-09 on intensifying violence there.
- SIN - June 2025 (207 killings) re-described as the deadliest month of the war; 'since 2011' could not be verified - evidence: Milenio 2025-09-09, Infobae 2025-07-25.
- SIN - El Mayo's life sentence dated July 20, 2026 - evidence: Wikipedia (Ismael Zambada García), Google News July 20-21, 2026.
- SIN - 2011-2016 intensity raised from 3 to 4: INEGI/SESNSP rates of roughly 30-65 per 100,000 (about 1,000-1,900 killings a year) match the 2017-19 period already rated 4.
- SIN - 2020-2023 intensity lowered from 3 to 2: rates of about 19-27 per 100,000, the lowest in more than a decade, match periods rated 2 in BCN and SON.
- SIN - added IPM 2026 state figures (1,022 homicides in 2024, 1,732 in 2025) and a fact on the May 2025 alliances; Chapo's 1993 arrest placed in Guatemala (June 9); Amado Carrillo's death dated July 4, 1997; added La Razón, Ríodoce and Wikipedia sources.
- SIN - Culiacán 2025 rate given precisely as 103.91 - evidence: Infobae 2026-02-12.
- FLAG: BCN - Tijuana's 2023 municipal homicide total could not be verified (searches exhausted); the CCSPJP 2023-data table lists Tijuana at 1,638 homicides (87.88 per 100,000, 7th) while municipal counts were reported higher. Confirm before quoting a 2023 number.
- FLAG: BCN - Assigning single controllers to Tecate, Rosarito, Ensenada and San Quintín for 2024-26 rests on one official statement (Zeta, April 21, 2026); all four are multi-faction contests and alignments were described as 'still being defined' after the CJNG-Chapitos break.
- FLAG: BCN - Wikipedia states Luis Fernando Sánchez Arellano 'El Ingeniero' was released in 2023 and consolidated power; not verified against a primary source, so not added.
- FLAG: BCN - Los Rusos' leader is given in 2026 reporting as Juan José Ponce Félix 'El Ruso' (aligned with El Mayito Flaco); older reporting names Jesús Alexander Sánchez Félix. Kept to the 2026 attribution.
- FLAG: BCS - 2017 homicide count discrepancy: 560 (SESNSP victims) vs about 788 (El Sudcaliforniano/state tally); InSight Crime's 78.6 per 100,000 does not match 560 divided by the state population (~69). Both figures are reported; check INEGI.
- FLAG: BCS - 2024 total (~55) is inferred from 2025 being 'practically double' 2024 (Peninsular Digital); no direct 2024 figure was retrieved.
- FLAG: BCS - Whether La Paz appeared on the CCSPJP list for 2017 and dropped off in 2018 was not re-verified this session.
- FLAG: SON - Ciudad Obregón's exact CCSPJP positions for 2022 and 2023 data could not be pinned down (sources conflict between 2nd and 3rd, rates 133-138); only 2021 (2nd, ~156) and 2025 (9th, 90.81) are firm.
- FLAG: SON - Nogales 'mayiza from 2022' start year is arbitrary; El Mayo's people held Nogales well before, and the Feb 2026 mapping also lists CJNG probing Nogales.
- FLAG: SON - Sonoyta 2019-26 'caborca' is a simplification: the Feb 2026 mapping places Los Rusos (Mayiza), Los Memos (Chapitos) and the Caborca Cartel in the Sonoyta/Plutarco Elías Calles area.
- FLAG: SON - The Salazar-Chapitos break 'around 2024' and the 'Cártel Independiente de Sonora' label rest on 2025-26 press reporting (Infobae, Milenio); no official document confirms the rebranding date.
- FLAG: SIN - 2026 plaza controllers for Culiacán (mayiza), Mazatlán (chapitos) and Los Mochis (beltran_leyva) rest mainly on a single Crisis Group assessment relayed by La Razón (July 7, 2026); Proceso and Ríodoce declined to assign control, and Infobae reported in August 2025 that the Mayito Flaco-Chapo Isidro alliance might be fracturing.
- FLAG: SIN - Cumulative tolls by July 2026 vary by source (Revista Espejo 3,045 dead / 4,635 disappeared; Proceso ~3,000 / ~4,000; La Prensa 4,000 dead / 3,400 disappeared); the dataset uses the conservative Proceso figures.
- FLAG: SIN - The 2017 state total 'about 1,500 homicides' was not re-verified this session.
- FLAG: SIN - The January 5, 2023 police fatality (10 soldiers, 1 police officer, 19 gunmen) follows Wikipedia's account; SEDENA's contemporaneous statement counted 29 dead (10 military, 19 gunmen).
- FLAG: GENERAL - Web search budget was exhausted mid-review; 2024-2026 items were verified via direct fetches of the cited articles and Google News feeds, but some secondary details (e.g., BCS monthly 2026 trends, exact Sonora 2022-2023 totals) remain unverified.
- CHH - 2020-2023 note and Cereso 3 fact: death toll corrected from '19 dead' to 10 guards and 7 inmates killed inside the prison plus two attackers (17 + 2), about 30 escaped, El Neto killed 5 January 2023 - Infobae 2 Jan 2023, N+, en.wikipedia 2023 Ciudad Juárez prison attack.
- CHH - 2024-2026 period: primary changed from sinaloa to juarez with secondary [mayiza, chapitos, sinaloa] because the June 2026 Lantia/Noroeste survey rates La Línea dominant in 26 of 67 municipalities including the Juárez valley, the state capital and the Jiménez-Camargo-Delicias corridor, while the Sinaloa bloc is split three ways (Mayiza 17, Chapitos 14, Salazar 10) - noroeste.com.mx Lantia column, 7 June 2026.
- CHH - plazas Ciudad Juárez, Chihuahua and Ojinaga: controller 2024-2026 changed from sinaloa to juarez to match the Lantia mid-2026 assessment (La Línea/NCDJ dominant in the Juárez valley, capital and Ojinaga-Aldama fighting zone) - noroeste.com.mx; Infobae 3 Dec 2025 on Ojinaga.
- CHH - 2024-2026 note: removed unverified claim of 'at least 23 dead in July 2025 clashes' at Ojinaga-Coyame; replaced with the documented dispute from September 2024 (Los Cabrera vs La Línea in Aldama, Coyame del Sotol, Manuel Benavides, Ojinaga) and the 27 May 2025 fighting - Infobae 3 Dec 2025, La Opción 27 May 2025, Wikipedia Sinaloa infighting timeline; El Heraldo de Chihuahua reported four dead in one Ojinaga-Coyame clash.
- CHH - 2024-2026 note: Guadalupe y Calvo fight relabelled as La Línea versus Los Salgueiro/Gente Nueva del Tigre (reported as Chapitos-aligned) instead of 'Los Salgueiro/Mayiza' - Infobae 5 Apr 2025 map (Chapitos in Chihuahua via Gente Nueva del Tigre and Los Salgueiro).
- CHH - 2024-2026 note: 'La Línea, now allied with CJNG' softened to 'reportedly supplied by CJNG' since Lantia describes CJNG as a drug supplier with sporadic presence in four municipalities - noroeste.com.mx.
- CHH - fact 6 and note: Ciudad Juárez 2025 homicides corrected from 920 to 926; unverified claim that November 2025 (49) was the lowest month since February 2018 removed - El Diario de Chihuahua 3 Jan 2026 (926), diario.mx 31 Dec 2025 (862 Jan-Nov).
- CHH - fact 7: removed unverified displacement figure (500 in 2021 to 1,500 in 2025), which does not appear in the cited Noroeste/Lantia article; added the presence counts (45/53/30/26 municipalities) and CJNG's four municipalities that the article does give.
- CHH - facts/notes: added dated detail (Aguilar Guajardo 12 April 1993, Rodolfo Carrillo 11 September 2004, Vicente Carrillo 9 October 2014, Creel 13 dead, Villas de Salvárcar 15 dead, 2010 state rate ~111 per 100,000) - standard references; Wikipedia list of Mexican states by homicides (2010 Chihuahua 110.7).
- CHH - Madera fact: gunmen count given as about 40 (survivor account) with the National Guard estimate of about 60 - en.wikipedia Madera ambush, La Jornada 5 Apr 2020.
- DUR - 2008-2012 secondary: added gulf alongside zetas (Zetas were the Gulf Cartel's armed wing until February 2010) to respect the rule that Los Zetas were not an independent organization before 2010.
- DUR - 2024-2026 note and fact 6: Vascogil bombing attribution softened to 'reportedly linked to Los Chapitos'; added the second aerial bombing on Tamazula on 2 November 2024 and the seizure of two aircraft; removed unverified 'ACLED counted at least 15 drone attacks' - Infobae 30 Oct and 9 Nov 2024, La Jornada 31 Oct 2024, Wikipedia Sinaloa infighting timeline.
- DUR - fact 7: El Durazno displacement anchored to documented events (Gente del Guano blockade 15 August 2025; displacement still reported 13 May 2026) - unotv.com 13 May 2026, Wikipedia Sinaloa infighting timeline.
- DUR - fact 2 and 1990-2007 note: homicide-rate figures aligned with the state series (2007 15.6, 2010 61.3, 2012 36.2, 2020 8.0 per 100,000) - Wikipedia list of Mexican states by homicides.
- COA - 2005-2009 period: primary changed from zetas to gulf with secondary [zetas, sinaloa, juarez], because Los Zetas were the Gulf Cartel's armed wing until the February 2010 split (rule: Zetas not independent before 2010).
- COA - plazas Piedras Negras, Ciudad Acuña, Saltillo, Monclova: controller 2005-2014 'zetas' split into gulf 2005-2009 and zetas 2010-2014; Torreón 2007-2012 'zetas' split into gulf 2007-2009 and zetas 2010-2012 (same reason).
- COA - 2010-2012 intensity lowered from 4 to 3: the state's peak rate (about 27 per 100,000 in 2012) was near the national average, far below Chihuahua's or Durango's peaks - Wikipedia list of Mexican states by homicides.
- COA - fact 3 and 2010-2012 note: homicide rates corrected from '6.8 (2008) to roughly 27-34 (2011-2012)' to 6.6 (2008), 23 (2011) and 27 (2012) with a fall below 8 by 2018 - Wikipedia list of Mexican states by homicides.
- COA - Villa Unión (2016-2019 note and fact 5): death toll corrected from 22 (16 gunmen, 4 police, 2 civilians) to the state's figure of 23 (17 gunmen, 4 police, 2 civilians), with more gunmen killed in the 1 December pursuit - El Universal, es/en Wikipedia Villa Unión shootout.
- COA - 2020-2026 note and fact 7: removed unverified 'INEGI preliminary 2025 count of 109, third lowest'; replaced with the verified prosecutor figure (84 in 2025 vs 122 in 2024, -31 percent, lowest in 30 years) and the second-lowest state rate after Yucatán - Vanguardia, Milenio, Info7.
- COA - fact 6: Treasury 6 August 2025 action specified (Antonio Romero Sánchez 'Romeo', Piedras Negras operations) - home.treasury.gov sb0219.
- COA - fact 1: Allende victim figures reworded: state investigation confirmed 28 deaths, researchers/National Security Archive estimate up to 300, bodies incinerated in fuel drums - nsarchive.gwu.edu briefing book 18 Mar 2021.
- COA - Piedras Negras tunnel escape dated 17 September 2012 (about 130 inmates) - standard reporting.
- ZAC - 2008-2012 period split into 2008-2009 (primary gulf, secondary zetas, sinaloa) and 2010-2012 (primary zetas, secondary gulf, sinaloa) to respect the Zetas-independent-only-from-2010 rule; intensities 1 and 2 match rates of about 5 (2008) and 13 (2012) per 100,000 - Wikipedia list of Mexican states by homicides.
- ZAC - all six plazas: controller 2008-2014 'zetas' split into gulf 2008-2009 and zetas 2010-2014 (same reason).
- ZAC - note: 'late-2011 revolt of El Talibán' corrected to the 2012 split, with grievances aired via banners from late 2011 - en.wikipedia Los Talibanes.
- ZAC - 2024-2026 note: Pánuco event corrected to the full incident - ten men killed 16-18 July 2026, five hung from a bridge at Pozo de Gamboa (Pánuco), four left in Morelos and one in Saín Alto; added CJNG's 24 August 2026 declaration of war on the Mayiza and the Talibanes' SLP corridor - Infobae 18 Jul 2026, Proceso, InSight Crime 7 Sep 2026, Expansión 2 Sep 2026.
- ZAC - car-bomb fact: damage figures reconciled (about 200 kg industrial explosives, 11 injured, 10 homes severely and about 53 lightly damaged, 13-15 vehicles) - Small Wars Journal note 55, El Universal, InSight Crime 7 Sep 2026.
- ZAC - Urzúa fact: attribution to 'CJNG gunmen' softened to 'gunmen during an operation in Pinos' (perpetrator group not confirmed in sources checked); full name Urzúa Padilla added.
- ZAC - fact 1: removed unverified '(up from 1,244 in 2020)' INEGI comparison; 1,776 and 109 per 100,000 confirmed - Expansión 26 Jul 2022, Animal Político, LJZ 31 Oct 2022.
- ZAC - 2020-2023 note: 2022 figure (981 SESNSP cases) confirmed via Expansión 2 Sep 2026; 2023 figure presented as 'roughly 1,000' pending verification.
- ZAC - fact 7: SESNSP 4 September 2026 presentation details confirmed (Marcela Figueroa, Guadalupe; 4.5 to 0.2 daily, -96 percent; 1.13 to 0.17 Sep 2024-Aug 2026) - Infobae 4 Sep 2026.
- ALL - periods and plaza controller sequences re-checked: contiguous 1980-2026, inclusive 'to', no overlaps; coordinates for all 22 plazas match the named cities.
- FLAG: CHH - Making La Línea (juarez) the 2024-2026 primary and switching the Juárez, Chihuahua and Ojinaga plazas to juarez rests mainly on Lantia's mid-2026 municipal survey; other analysts still describe Ciudad Juárez as a split retail market (Mexicles/Artistas Asesinos vs Aztecas/La Línea). Ojinaga town itself is actively disputed with Los Cabrera (Mayiza), who hold Aldama and the Coyame desert.
- FLAG: CHH - The 2012-2023 attribution of Ciudad Juárez to 'sinaloa' is a simplification; La Línea/Aztecas never left the city and several sources treat it as contested throughout.
- FLAG: CHH - Cuauhtémoc plaza shown as juarez 2017-2026 (El 80's stronghold); Lantia's 2026 map places the Mayiza in the central zone near Cuauhtémoc, so 2024-2026 may deserve 'mayiza' or contested status.
- FLAG: CHH - Statewide counts differ by source: SESNSP gives about 2,300 cases in 2020, 2,040 victims in 2024 and 1,791 in 2025, while El Diario de Chihuahua's own tally is 3,468 (2020), 2,305 (2024) and 1,778 (2025). The notes use SESNSP.
- FLAG: CHH - Juárez 2010: 3,111 (media/federal) vs 3,622 (state) both circulate; not re-verified online this session.
- FLAG: CHH - The 'Juárez above 1,100 homicides a year' claim for 2020-2023 fits 2023 (1,172) and 2024 (1,104) but 2021-2022 were around 1,200 and 1,000; treat as approximate.
- FLAG: DUR - Vascogil bombing attribution to Los Chapitos is media/analyst attribution (village was a Mayo/Cabrera stronghold); the municipality of Vascogil is given as Santiago Papasquiaro by Infobae but Tamazula in other reports.
- FLAG: DUR - Scale of El Durazno displacement (number of families) is not quantified in the sources checked.
- FLAG: DUR - 383 exhumed bodies and 124 identified by 2019 rest on the Infobae summary; Durango prosecutor totals vary between 300 and 400.
- FLAG: COA - Allende official victim count (28) and the 300 upper bound could not be re-verified beyond the NSArchive briefing; FIDH and Aguayo's Colmex report give varying tallies (28 confirmed, 42 disappeared, up to 300 estimated).
- FLAG: COA - Moreira's '1,600 disappeared' acknowledgement not re-verified this session.
- FLAG: COA - CDN 'cells in Monclova and Saltillo per Lantia' not re-verified; CDN's Coahuila presence beyond the border strip is thin in public reporting.
- FLAG: ZAC - SESNSP investigation series for 2018-2021 and 2023 (561, 510, 789, 1,134, ~1,050) could not be re-verified because the web-search budget was exhausted; 981 (2022), 369 (2024) and 114 (2025) are consistent with checked sources.
- FLAG: ZAC - General José Silvestre Urzúa Padilla's exact title (head of state public security) and the group responsible for his 24 November 2022 death in Pinos (CJNG vs Talibanes) were not confirmed online this session.
- FLAG: ZAC - The 'pax narca' truce between the Mayiza and state actors is reported by InSight Crime and Zeta on the basis of narcomantas; treat the truce and its July 2026 collapse as reported, not established.
- FLAG: ZAC - Ojocaliente is described by InSight Crime as Mayiza-leaning southeastern ground while the plaza list shows CJNG control 2020-2026; the town is best read as contested.
- FLAG: ZAC - 2019 secondary 'cdn' (Cártel del Noreste in mining districts) relies on Lantia/Expansión analyst statements; CDN's Zacatecas footprint is small.
- FLAG: GENERAL - Web search budget ran out midway; verification for 2024-2026 relied on direct fetches of cited articles (Noroeste/Lantia, Expansión, InSight Crime, Small Wars Journal, Infobae, El Diario, Vanguardia, Treasury) and pre-2024 rate figures on the Wikipedia state-homicide table.
- TAM - 2003-2006 period: removed beltran_leyva from secondary (BLO was not an independent organization before its January 2008 break with Sinaloa); note now attributes La Barbie's Nuevo Laredo push to the Sinaloa Federation's Beltrán Leyva wing - canonical-ID rule plus standard chronology of the BLO split.
- TAM - 2003-2006 intensity lowered 4 to 3 and 2007-2009 lowered 3 to 2: statewide homicide rates were about 11 per 100,000 in 2006 and 8-10 in 2007-09 versus roughly 30 (SESNSP) / 45 (INEGI) in 2012 - homicide-rate table (Wikipedia list compiled from SESNSP/INEGI).
- TAM - 2015-2016 intensity lowered 4 to 3: rates of about 15-17 per 100,000 were below the 2013-14 (16-18) and 2017-18 (22-23) levels - same rate series.
- TAM - 2024-2026 intensity lowered 3 to 2 and note updated: SESNSP recorded 331 homicides in 2024 (rate 9.3), down from 680 INEGI in 2023 - es.wikipedia homicide-rate annex (INEGI 2023 / SESNSP 2024).

- TAM - 2013-2014 note and NLE 2013-2016 note: Z-40 arrest specified as 15 July 2013 near Anáhuac, Nuevo León (outside Nuevo Laredo) rather than simply 'near Nuevo Laredo' - Wikipedia Los Zetas article.

- TAM - facts: added a Grupo Fugitivo fact and added 2023 (680 INEGI) and 2024 (331 SESNSP) homicide figures to the homicide fact.
- NLE - 2021-2023 note and facts: 2022 homicide total corrected from 1,328 to about 1,430 (Fiscalía figures reported to SESNSP); 2011 (2,003) and 2012 (about 1,459) remain the only higher years - El Universal 28 Dec 2023 and Verificado.

- NLE - 2024-2026 note: Rayones clash re-dated to the night of 5 May 2025 with 'reportedly five dead' - bodies were removed and the state prosecutor could not confirm deaths (El Universal, Milenio 6 May 2025).
- NLE - 2024-2026 note: Los Aldama clash dated to late October 2025 (El Financiero, 28 Oct 2025), which also confirms the name Operativo Muralla.
- NLE - Don Zefe fact and note: removed the unsourced claim that he ran Díaz Ordaz, Camargo, Miguel Alemán, Mier and Guerrero 'for decades'; replaced with his documented profile (former Miguel Alemán police chief, early Zetas recruiter, killed 23 December 2025 by AEI agents in Los Rodríguez, Santiago, while allegedly building an armed cell) - Infobae and La Razón 23-24 Dec 2025.
- NLE - 1997-2004 and 2005-2009 notes: Beltrán Leyva arrivals in 2003-04 described as part of the Sinaloa Federation, with BLO independent only from 2008 (kept as secondary in 2005-2009 because that period runs past 2008).
- NLE - 2010-2012 note: added INEGI 2012 figure (about 1,459).

- SLP - periods re-cut: 2014-2016 (intensity 2) and 2017-2023 (intensity 3) instead of 2014-2017 and 2018-2023, because CJNG arrived in 2017 and the homicide rate jumped to about 16.7 per 100,000 that year, matching the 2011 level - homicide-rate table.

- SLP - 2024-2026 note and fact: electricians were CFE subcontractors abducted on Highway 57 near Matehuala on 21 March 2026 and found alive on 23 March; Matehuala's municipal police director was detained (Proceso 25 Mar 2026, La Jornada 23 Mar 2026).
- SLP - 2014-2016 note: CDN presence dated from 2015 (not 2014) consistent with its founding after Z-42's March 2015 capture.

- VER - 2024-2026 note: Tuxpan prison riot dated 2 August 2025 (seven inmates dead, 11 injured, protest against Grupo Sombra extortion) - El Financiero/La Jornada 3 Aug 2025; Poza Rica-Cazones body dump softened to 'three dismembered bodies with a Grupo Sombra message on a highway near Poza Rica in early August 2025' (Expansión 6 Aug 2025 does not name the road).
- VER - 2024-2026 note: Roxana Guzmán case corrected - abducted 2 June 2026 in Nanchital, remains identified 3 July 2026 in Moloacán, eight arrested including four Ixhuatlán del Sureste municipal police and Grupo Sombra operative 'Delta 7' - CPJ and Infobae 3-4 July 2026.
- VER - 2024-2026 note: added killing of journalist Carlos Castro in Poza Rica (8 January 2026) and of retired teacher Irma Hernández (Álamo, July 2025), and Nahle's inauguration dated 1 December 2024.
- VER - Grupo Sombra fact: founding phrased as 'began operating in 2017' and the open war with CJNG dated to January 2024 (Expansión); leader name omitted because Expansión (Martín Martínez) and Infobae (Francisco Mendiola Cisneros) disagree on the identity of 'Comandante Miranda'.

- FLAG: INEGI absolute counts for Tamaulipas 2012 (about 1,550) and 2018 (1,437) and for Nuevo León 2012 (about 1,459) and 2015 (about 450) could not be re-verified live: inegi.org.mx refused connections and the web-search budget was exhausted; they match the compiler's recollection and the SESNSP-based rate curve (2012 and 2018 are the two peaks) but should be checked against INEGI's Mortalidad_08 tabulado.
- FLAG: Veracruz 2017 total: the timeline's 1,722 comes from a state-government release that could not be fetched (DNS failure); a La Jornada summary of INEGI's 2017 release put Veracruz at 1,555 deaths, and SESNSP victim counts for 2017 are usually cited near 1,640. The 20.1 per 100,000 rate in the Wikipedia table appears to be SESNSP-based rather than INEGI. Treat the 1,722/854 pair as state-reported SESNSP figures pending confirmation.
- FLAG: Killing of the FGR delegate in Reynosa (August 2025) is retained from the original timeline but was not independently confirmed within this session's remaining tool budget.
- FLAG: Z-42 arrest: most reporting says 4 March 2015 in San Pedro Garza García; the English Wikipedia Los Zetas article says 3 March 2015 in Monterrey. Kept 4 March / San Pedro.
- FLAG: Tampico plaza controller sequence (Zetas 2010-2013, Gulf factions from 2014) and Ciudad Victoria (Zetas Vieja Escuela 2016-2019, CDN from 2020) are plausible but rest on analyst reporting rather than a single authoritative source; InSight Crime's Matamoros piece and the CDN/ZVE Wikipedia entries do not date those shifts. Ciudad Victoria in particular remains disputed between CDN, ZVE remnants and Gulf-aligned cells.
- FLAG: NLE 2017-2026 'primary: cdn' is a judgment call: Infobae (Oct 2023) and state officials describe a four-way contest (CDN, Gulf, Sinaloa, CJNG) for the Monterrey metro with no group in full control; contested=true reflects that.
- FLAG: SLP 2023 INEGI count: La Orquesta (Aug 2026) cites 560 while the es.wikipedia annex cites 555; kept 'about 560'.
- FLAG: Nuevo León 2025 homicide total not established; Verificado reports a sharp fall (roughly half) through October 2025 but no clean annual figure was obtained.
- FLAG: Grupo Sombra's leader identity is inconsistent across sources (Expansión: Martín Martínez; Infobae: Francisco Mendiola Cisneros), and Infobae's claim that it now controls 52 municipalities is likely an overstatement; not used.
- FLAG: Duarte-era journalist toll ('at least twelve killed and three disappeared by 2015') follows Article 19-style counts that vary between 12 and 17 depending on inclusion criteria.
- FLAG: Veracruz 2024-2026 intensity kept at 3 despite a SESNSP rate under 10 per 100,000 because of the documented Grupo Sombra-CJNG escalation in the north; a reviewer preferring a strict rate-based scale could set it to 2.

- GUA - Celaya, Salamanca and Villagrán plaza controllers changed from zetas 2008-2012 to sinaloa through 2009 and zetas from 2010; same rule.

- GUA - 'Salamanca 4 Oct 2024, 12 dead' reworded as a wave of attacks that day (Milenio, 13 Nov 2024: '12 ejecuciones en Salamanca' on 4 October), not a single massacre.

- GUA - added 2025 Irapuato mass-grave discoveries (17 bodies 26 May, 32 bodies 30 July; Wikipedia 2025 in Mexico).

Proceso (Aguascalientes, 3 Jul 2026), La Silla Rota (2019 Zetas list; 2025 Treasury), emeequis (Hidalgo 2025) and the Google-indexed La Jornada/El Financiero/effeta articles could not be opened directly; their content is accepted from headlines and secondary summaries.
- FLAG: GUA - SESNSP 2025 figures conflict: am.com.mx cites 2,557 cases (carpetas) for Guanajuato in 2025, while Informador (8 Jan 2026) cites 2,388 victims (11% of the national total); both may be correct under different metrics but should be reconciled with the SESNSP victim series.
- FLAG: GUA - Celaya plaza controller 'santa_rosa_lima 2017-2026' is a simplification; Celaya has been continuously disputed with CJNG since 2018 and CJNG holds much of the urban retail market.
- FLAG: GUA - Guanajuato's 2020 INEGI count (5,370) is as reported by am.com.mx; the state share of the national total depends on whether INEGI's original (36,773) or revised (36,579) 2020 national figure is used.
- FLAG: QUE - The 'October 2023 CJNG video announcing arrival' is reported by Seasons of Crime; state authorities at the time questioned its authenticity, and Infobae's Nov 2023 review cites a May 2023 'Comandante Castro' video instead. Kept with hedged wording.
- FLAG: QUE - Zetas as primary organization 2010-2013 is based on their documented rear-base and extortion cells; no source quantifies market dominance in the state.
- FLAG: AGU - The original claim that 2024 was a decade high (112 homicide files) is plausible given Seasons of Crime's '20% rise' but could not be verified; the 2026 figure of 65 by April was inconsistent with the reported 2025 decline and was dropped.
- FLAG: AGU - Los Talibanes as a map-level secondary for 2018-2026 rests on Seasons of Crime (May 2024) and Zacatecas reporting; their footprint inside Aguascalientes is thin.
- FLAG: HID - The 2011 Federal Police list of Zetas-controlled municipalities comes from the La Silla Rota 2019 article cited in the original, which returned HTTP 403 and could not be re-read.
- FLAG: HID - Remigio Morfín Morfín 'overseeing fuel theft in Hidalgo' is from La Silla Rota's reading of the Treasury release; OFAC's listing only gives a Hidalgo address. Proceso (16 May 2025) headlined that the DEA shows three cartels in Hidalgo; the third could not be identified from the sources fetched, so CDN was removed rather than confirmed.
- FLAG: HID - Huejutla controller sequence after 2016 (Zetas Vieja Escuela then Gulf Cartel from 2019) and the 'fragmented' label for Pachuca/Tula 2013-2022 are analytical judgments; CJNG's Grupo Pantera (Dec 2022) and Los Hades are the only named actors in recent reporting.
- FLAG: ALL - 'El Mencho' death on 22 Feb 2026 and the succession by Juan Carlos Valencia González are taken from the English Wikipedia biography; the atlas should cross-check with Reuters/AP before publication.
## Organizations
- guadalajara - Matta-Ballesteros fate changed from 'remains in US federal prison' to died 30 Oct 2025 at MCFP Springfield, Missouri, aged 80; added May 2025 compassionate release reversed by Ninth Circuit 8 Oct 2025 and Dec 2018 dropping of Camarena charges - evidence: en.wikipedia Matta article citing La Prensa (Honduras) 30 Oct 2025; laprensa.hn article
