- gulf - 'Delta 1' arrest dated precisely to July 2, 2026 in Coatzacoalcos and tied to the abduction and murder of journalist Roxana Berenice Guzmán Ramírez - La Razón, Reforma, Zeta and N+ (2-3 Jul 2026); Guzmán was seized in Nanchital on June 2, 2026 and found dead 24 days later in Moloacán.
- gulf - Tuxpan prison riot redated from Aug 4, 2025 to Aug 2, 2025 (control regained Aug 3) and reframed as inmates rioting against Grupo Sombra's control; casualty figure kept at 7 dead, 11 injured added - La Jornada 3-4 Aug 2025, El Financiero, SSP Veracruz.
- gulf - FGR delegate killing dated Aug 4, 2025 and the victim named Ernesto Cuitláhuac Vásquez Reyna; a Los Metros suspect (Jaret Roberto 'H') was arrested Aug 8 - La Jornada 5 Aug 2025, Proceso 4 Aug 2025, N+; ODNI CT Guide also lists it as a Los Metros 'notable attack'.
- gulf - OFAC May 1, 2025 designation clarified: the three Morfín Morfín brothers and two companies were sanctioned as a CJNG fuel-theft network in Tamaulipas, not as Gulf Cartel members - Treasury/OFAC 1 May 2025 and Proceso/SinEmbargo coverage naming César 'Primito' Morfín Morfín 'líder de la célula del CJNG en Tamaulipas'.
- gulf - Matamoros huachicol raid redated from 'April 24-25' to April 25, 2026, with location (ejido Progreso Agrario) and two arrests added - Infobae and La Jornada, 25 Apr 2026 (FGR with Semar; 8.3 million pesos in damage).
- gulf - Ally 'other/Los Marros' reassigned to the canonical id santa_rosa_lima, period changed to 2026-present - La Jornada, 24 May 2026 and Noticieros en Línea, 3 Sep 2026: FGR Guanajuato delegate Juan Francisco Vera Ayala names the Cártel de Santa Rosa de Lima (Los Marros) as allied with Los Escorpiones.
- gulf - CJNG alliance period changed from 2025-2026 to 2023-2026 - the Metros cell went over with 'El Primito' in 2023 (SDP/Milenio 'La Primiza'), and ODNI's April 2026 CT Guide states Los Metros is allied with the CJNG.
- gulf - 'Metro 9' identified as Alexander Benavides Flores, arrest attributed to federal ministerial police in colonia Vicente Guerrero, and his May 8, 2026 arraignment on military-grade weapons charges added - El Financiero and Excélsior, 27 Apr 2026; Infobae, 8 May 2026.
- gulf - García Ábrego conviction and sentencing dates separated (convicted Oct 1996, sentenced Jan 31, 1997) - Wikipedia/NDSN and Texas Monthly; the $350M figure is the jury forfeiture, with total fines and forfeitures reported elsewhere at $478M.
- gulf - Juan N. Guerra's Prohibition framing softened ('final years of U.S. Prohibition and under Texas' dry laws') and his age at death (85) added - born 18 Jul 1915, died 12 Jul 2001; U.S. Prohibition was repealed in Dec 1933, so a 1930s whisky-running career spans only its last years.
- gulf - 'Concord 3' described as the Zetas' finance chief and the killing attributed to Gulf gunmen rather than a kidnapping-then-killing - InSight Crime 'Battle for Monterrey' and DEA-sourced accounts of the Jan 18, 2010 killing in Reynosa.
- gulf - 'Comandante Toro' alias variant corrected from 'Juan Manuel Loza Salinas' to 'Juan Manuel Loisa Salinas' - El Universal, Excélsior and Crónica (Apr 2017) use Loisa Salinas in both variants.
- gulf - Homero Cárdenas Guillén's death flagged as never officially confirmed - Wikipedia and Mexican reporting note the March 28, 2014 liposuction death was never verified.
- gulf - X-20's fate expanded: he was a cooperating witness (including against 'El Menchito') and died before his case concluded - Latin Times, Mar 2025; DOJ confirmed the death on Mar 21, 2025.
- gulf - El Vaquero's fate updated with his March 31, 2026 guilty plea in Brownsville (up to 10 years, sentencing set July 7, 2026) - Infobae, 31 Mar 2026; reporter Dave Hendricks confirmed the Brownsville hearing.
- gulf - FTO designation nuance added: Rubio signed the determination Feb 6, 2025 under E.O. 14157 and it took effect on Federal Register publication Feb 20, 2025 - Federal Register 2025-02873; CRS IN11205.
- gulf - Grupo Fugitivo item dated precisely (kidnapped May 25, 2025, five musicians, bodies found May 28) and attribution firmed to a Los Metros cell - Milenio and La Silla Rota (Jun 2025): 12 detainees, all Metros, with Santos 'N' 'M-47' named as the mastermind.
- gulf - Added the Jan 20, 2026 transfer of 37 prisoners including former Gulf Cartel member Ricardo Cortez-Mateos 'Billeton' - DOJ press release, 21 Jan 2026.
- gulf - Added Los Rojos gunman Serapio 'El Sapo / R-105', captured in Aldama, TAM on Feb 4, 2026 - Infobae, 5 Feb 2026.
- gulf - Added ODNI operating-area data (Tamaulipas, Nuevo León, Coahuila core; limited Jalisco and Tabasco; at least 16 U.S. states) to peak note and facts - ODNI Counterterrorism Guide, Gulf Cartel, as of April 2026.
- gulf - Added assassination of civilians and government officials plus bribery of officials to the activities list - ODNI Counterterrorism Guide, April 2026.
- gulf - Sources list rebuilt: added ODNI CT Guide, both DOJ transfer releases, the ICE sentencing release, DHS/HSI removal release and the primary 2025-2026 Mexican reporting; removed the Spanish Wikipedia page on the 2025 transfers as a sole citation and corrected the Federal Register link to the full document URL.
- FLAG: 'La Kena' arrest date: Wikipedia and most outlets give January 19, 2024 in Monterrey; Debate and some Semar-sourced accounts give January 17 or 18, 2024. Kept Jan 19 but the exact day is contested.
- FLAG: Rafael Cárdenas Vela: ICE headlines say a South Texas jury convicted him, but he is also widely described as a cooperating witness who testified for the government, which usually follows a plea. I could not obtain the DOJ/DEA release text (403) to settle whether he pleaded guilty in March 2012 or was convicted at trial; the sentence (240 months, $1M fine, Nov 17, 2014) is solid.
- FLAG: 'Concord 3': sources disagree on his first name — Víctor Peña Mendoza (DEA-sourced accounts) vs Sergio Peña Mendoza / Sergio Peña Solís. Used Víctor.
- FLAG: Armando López Garcés 'Escorpión 7' as current Escorpiones leader rests on InSight Crime naming him a possible successor and on Borderland Beat / social-media OSINT; no government confirmation of his leadership or of his being at large as of September 2026.
- FLAG: Los Panteras 'from 2015' is not documented in any source I could verify; the cell is real and Metros-linked but the start year should be treated as an estimate.
- FLAG: Los Escorpiones 'from 2009' as Tony Tormenta's bodyguard unit is a plausible and commonly repeated origin but I found no primary confirmation of the year; the faction is only clearly attested from the 2010 split.
- FLAG: Grupo Sombra founding: 2017 per 2026 Infobae/Milenio/Yucatán profiles, but some earlier reporting places its emergence in 2016. Also, ODNI's April 2026 guide lists only three Gulf factions (Escorpiones, Metros, Ciclones) and does not treat Grupo Sombra or Los Rojos as Gulf factions, so faction taxonomy varies by source.
- FLAG: A February 2025 truce between Grupo Escorpión and Los Metros was widely reported (Infobae, 13 Feb 2025) but never confirmed by any authority; it is not asserted in the profile.
- FLAG: Osiel Cárdenas's Mexican charges: AP and Reuters reported drug, organized-crime and money-laundering charges; other outlets mention homicide among the reactivated cases. I removed 'homicide' as unverified and used the charges the wire services named.
- FLAG: 'Founded 1984' follows InSight Crime and ODNI, but the leader list begins with Juan N. Guerra in 1930; the 1930s network is the cartel's predecessor rather than the cartel itself, which is why the two dates differ.
- FLAG: The peak-period state list (15 states, 1999-2010) reflects Zetas-era reach and is an editorial judgement, not a sourced enumeration; no single source lists exactly these states.
- FLAG: Whether 'El Vaquero' was actually sentenced on July 7, 2026 could not be confirmed — the web-search budget was exhausted before I could check for a sentencing report; the profile says only that sentencing was set for that date.
- FLAG: El Contador's earlier arrest dates (Feb 19, 2018 and Oct 29, 2019) come from the original profile and Mexican summaries; I verified the 2022 arrest and the Nov 2024 release but not the exact days of the first two.
- zetas / zetas_vieja_escuela - MAJOR: removed the false claim that Z-43 José María Guízar Valencia was transferred to the US on 12 Aug 2025 among 26 prisoners. He is a US-born dual citizen (Tulare, CA) who waived extradition after his 2018 arrest, pleaded guilty in the Eastern District of Texas on 20 Dec 2021 and was sentenced to 480 months (40 years) on 15 Nov 2022 by Judge Richard A. Schell - DOJ/USAO-EDTX press release 'Two High-Ranking Los Zetas Cartel Members Latest Sentenced to Federal Prison'; Infobae 17 Nov 2022; Univision San Antonio. He was NOT on the 12 Aug 2025 list of 26 (that group included Abigael González Valencia, 'La Tuta', 'El Chavo Félix') - El Financiero, 12 Aug 2025.
- zetas / zetas_vieja_escuela - Z-43's arrest date corrected from 8 Feb 2018 to 9 Feb 2018 - USAO-EDTX release ('arrested in Mexico on Feb. 9, 2018'); English Wikipedia, Zetas Vieja Escuela.
- zetas - Omar Treviño Morales (Z-42): removed 'held at MDC Brooklyn'. He was held at the Alexandria, Virginia, Adult Detention Center, threatened a guard, and was moved to FCI Lewisburg, Pennsylvania, on 18 Nov 2025 under Special Administrative Measures - Infobae 5 Mar 2026; adn40 5 Mar 2026; El Financiero 4 Mar 2026.
- zetas - Miguel Ángel Treviño Morales (Z-40): added his place of detention, the Northern Neck Regional Jail in Virginia, and the 45-page motion filed 19 Jan 2026 alleging 275+ days of solitary confinement - El Imparcial 19 Jan 2026; Infobae 19 Jan 2026; Milenio.
- zetas - Jaime González Durán 'El Hummer': removed the unsupported '35-year Mexican sentence' framing and added his US disposition - pleaded guilty 28 Feb 2025 and sentenced in Washington, DC, on 20 Jun 2025 to 35 years with a forfeiture reported at about US$792 million - Infobae, Proceso, La Jornada and El Universal, 20 Jun 2025. Extradition date 20 Oct 2022 confirmed.
- zetas - Jesús Enrique Rejón Aguilar 'El Mamito': guilty-plea date pinned to 1 Feb 2013 before Judge Barbara J. Rothstein, extradition given as Sept 2012 (the 11 Sep 2012 day-precision was not corroborated), and his role as a government witness at the Austin quarter-horse trial added; the profile's implicit closure was replaced with 'sentence not publicly reported' - DOJ/DEA press release 1 Feb 2013; Austin American-Statesman 30 Apr 2013.
- zetas - Rogelio González Pizaña (Z-2): death date softened from a flat '6 Dec 2015' to 'early Dec 2015 (reported 5-6 Dec)' because SinEmbargo published the killing on 5 Dec 2015 while Wikipedia gives 6 Dec; added that the body was never found and that his release followed a sentence reduction - SinEmbargo 5 Dec 2015; English Wikipedia.
- zetas - Efraín Teodoro Torres (Z-14): corrected 'died 3 Mar 2007 of wounds from a horse-race shootout' to killed in the shootout at the Villarín horse-race meeting on 3 Mar 2007, with the body stolen from the cemetery the same night - La Silla Rota (Veracruz), Expediente, English Wikipedia.
- zetas / zetas_vieja_escuela - Luis Reyes Enríquez 'El Rex' (Z-12): death date corrected from 20 Nov 2018 to Saturday 17 Nov 2018 (stabbed hours after entering the Centro de Ejecución de Sanciones of Nuevo Laredo; reported 20 Nov). The 'remains left outside three prisons on 27 Nov' detail was replaced with the sourced version: body handed to the family, stolen from the funeral home, dismembered and scattered near prisons in the Monterrey metropolitan area with the head left at Ciudad Victoria - Infobae 20 Nov 2018; La Prensa; La Silla Rota 30 Nov 2018.
- zetas - Raúl Lucio Hernández Lechuga 'El Lucky': added alias Z-100 and his US destination, McKinney, Texas (Eastern District of Texas), and extended his territory to Oaxaca - N+ list of the 29 transferred on 27 Feb 2025; La Silla Rota 27-28 Feb 2025.
- zetas - status_2026 and the judicial paragraph rewritten: the 2028 trial date is the court's tentative date, not a firm 'September 2028' setting; the 10 Sept 2026 status hearing is retained as verified - Infobae 10 Jul 2026; SDP Noticias; Infobae 14 Oct 2025.
- zetas - the fact about the US$5M rewards was extended to note Z-43 also carried one; the San Fernando survivor fact dropped the unverified '22 km' figure.
- cdn / zetas - 'El Kiko' Juan Francisco Treviño Chávez: arrest details and disposition added - arrested 28 Sept 2016 in Baytown, near Houston (he is a US citizen), convicted on all seven counts by a Waco jury on 24 Jul 2018, sentenced 8 Nov 2018 by Judge Alia Moses to two consecutive life terms plus 20 years and US$4M in fines/money judgments - DEA press release 8 Nov 2018; ICE/HSI release; ATF release.
- cdn - Villa Unión assault date corrected from 1 Dec 2019 to 30 Nov 2019, and the toll restated as about 23-25 dead including the following days' pursuit rather than 'at least 22'; the '60 gunmen' figure was replaced with 'dozens' because sources conflict (Spanish Wikipedia ~50 in 14 vehicles, English Wikipedia ~150 in 25 vehicles) - English and Spanish Wikipedia; Excélsior; La Jornada 1 Dec 2019.
- cdn - 'El Ricky' seizure figures corrected from '300 g of methamphetamine and 1,500 fentanyl pills' to 434 g of methamphetamine and 1,509 fentanyl pills - La Jornada 4 Feb 2025; UnoTV; El Universal.
- cdn - 'El Tartas': full name corrected to César Alejandro Silva Delgado; the seizure corrected from 2,500 to 2,000 fentanyl pills; and his death added - he killed himself in a Nuevo Laredo prison on 18 Jul 2024 while awaiting extradition - La Jornada 29 Nov 2023; N+; Infobae 21 Jul 2024; Reforma.
- cdn - the claim that eight members were arrested in Feb 2025 for the 17 Aug 2024 attack could not be verified and was replaced with the verified fact that El Ricky was charged over that attack - La Jornada 4 Feb 2025.
- cdn - 'El Juanito' Juan Cisneros Treviño: added his second alias 'La Sombra' - Milenio profile of the CDN leadership.
- cdn - 'El Makabelico' identified by his real name, Ricardo Hernández Medrano, and the Treasury finding that half his streaming royalties went to the CDN was added - OFAC/Treasury action 6 Aug 2025; Al Jazeera 6 Aug 2025; UPI.
- cdn - Miguel Ángel de Anda Ledezma given his alias 'El Miguelón' - Infobae and Semanario ZETA, 21 May 2025.
- cdn - Antonio Romero Sánchez's description softened from 'Piedras Negras boss' to 'high-ranking member', the wording OFAC actually used - Treasury action 6 Aug 2025.
- cdn - Francisco Daniel Esqueda Nieto's role sharpened: OFAC says he took over tactical operations in Nuevo Laredo after El Ricky's arrest and commanded an attack on a SEDENA helicopter during it - Treasury action 6 Aug 2025.
- cdn - 14 Apr 2026 OFAC action detailed and verified: six targets, namely Casino Centenario, Diamante Casino (Tampico plus its gambling site), operator CAMSA, Eduardo Javier Islas Valdez 'Crosty', lawyer Juan Pablo Penilla Rodríguez and Jesús Reymundo Ramos Vázquez - Treasury press release sb0440, 14 Apr 2026.
- cdn - Rivera Parrazales arrest restated: 19 Jul 2026 in Mérida, with searches in Mérida and Komchén, 15 luxury vehicles and assets valued at 145.2 million pesos (about US$7.7M), and his August 2026 binding over for money laundering and drug dealing; his reported links to La Barredora noted in the history - El Imparcial 19 Jul 2026; La Jornada 19 Jul and 16 Aug 2026; Expansión 20 Jul 2026.
- cdn - the Milenio report on El Juanito's continued command was dated to 'August 2026' rather than the unverifiable '12 August 2026' - Milenio CDN leadership coverage.
- cdn - the '8,992 members in 2022' figure could not be traced to a primary source; it was removed from the facts list and downgraded to a hedged note in the peak field - InSight Crime's CDN profile carries no membership estimate.
- cdn - added El Negrolo (Heriberto Rodríguez Hernández, arrested 28 Nov 2022) to the Tropa del Infierno succession in the history and noted that El Tartas replaced him - N+ and La Jornada, Nov 2023.
- cdn - 'Operativa Texas' faction note stripped of the 'Crashout Media, 2024' citation, which is not a source this project can stand behind.
- zetas_vieja_escuela - founding year changed from 2015 to 2016 to match the source the profile itself cites (English Wikipedia gives 2016) and the March 2016 anti-CDN pact that is the group's first documented act; Antonio Salas Perea's leader dates moved to 2016-2018 accordingly.
- zetas_vieja_escuela - 'El Pata de Queso' Martiniano de Jesús Jaramillo Silva: arrest corrected from 17 Nov 2017 to 14 Nov 2017 (at the general hospital in Ciudad Victoria) and his death from 'hours later' to three days later, on 17 Nov 2017, in a Mexico City hospital, aged 56, of kidney failure and peritonitis - Excélsior 17 Nov 2017; Aristegui Noticias; El Informador.
- zetas_vieja_escuela - 'La Barbie' José Roberto Stolberg Becerra: arrest located at Tlajomulco de Zúñiga, Jalisco, by the FGR on a 2018 kidnapping and criminal-association warrant, with a 1.5-million-peso Tamaulipas reward; date 9 Apr 2019 confirmed - Proceso 9 Apr 2019; El Financiero; Milenio.
- zetas_vieja_escuela - 'InSight Crime calls northern Veracruz the group's headquarters' softened to an unattributed description, since that specific attribution could not be verified in InSight Crime's own text.
- zetas_vieja_escuela - status_2026 and history rewritten to remove the Z-43 transfer claim and replace it with his 2022 US sentence; the reference to 'InSight Crime's August 2026 Gulf Cartel profile' was generalised to 'InSight Crime's 2026 Gulf Cartel profile' because the month could not be confirmed.
- talibanes - Iván Velázquez Caballero's leader dates changed from 2012-2012 to 2007-2012, matching the profile's own account (and English Wikipedia's) that he ran the cell from about 2007; the founding-year ambiguity is now stated explicitly in the history.
- talibanes - 'El Talibancito' Raúl Velázquez Caballero: the 'some outlets say brother' hedge was dropped in favour of the sourced reading that he is El Talibán's son - Infobae 8 Oct 2020; English Wikipedia.
- talibanes - the Expansión analyst is named in full and given his affiliation, Víctor Manuel Sánchez of the Universidad Autónoma de Coahuila - Expansión 2 Sept 2026.
- talibanes - Ojocaliente car bomb detailed: Sunday 30 Aug 2026, ten wounded including two municipal police officers, attributed by authorities to the CJNG in reprisal for the arrest of an 18-year-old gunman; added as a fact - Expansión 2 Sept 2026.
- talibanes - the five hanged bodies located precisely at Pozo de Gamboa, in the municipality of Pánuco, about 75 km from Ojocaliente - Expansión 2 Sept 2026.
- talibanes - the Politico.mx 17 Feb 2026 map description corrected: the Sinaloa Cartel is placed in Fresnillo, the north and the capital, and the CDN in the east and north including Pinos; the map carries no homicide figures - Politico.mx 17 Feb 2026.
- talibanes - the claim that Sheinbaum's Zacatecas visit was on 5 Sept 2026 was removed, as the Expansión piece dates only the article (2 Sept 2026) and the upcoming visit.
- talibanes - 114 Zacatecas homicides in 2025 confirmed as intentional homicides (homicidios dolosos) - Expansión 2 Sept 2026.
- FLAG: CDN founding year: the profile uses 1975-style precision (2015) but sources disagree. InSight Crime's own profile dates El Kiko's leadership of the splinter to 2012; Mexican reporting dates the first 'Cártel del Noreste' banners to 2014-2015. I kept founded=2015 and noted InSight Crime's earlier dating in the history, but this field is genuinely contested and a curator may prefer 2014.
- FLAG: Los Talibanes founding year: kept at 2012 (the independent break with Z-40), but English Wikipedia and Infobae date the cell's formation to 2007 under Z-50. The Zetas profile's own 'talibanes_cell' faction runs from 2007, so the two records now describe the same body with different start years by design. Flagged for a curatorial decision.
- FLAG: Zetas Vieja Escuela founding year: changed 2015 to 2016 on the strength of English Wikipedia and the March 2016 pact, but the group's antecedents (Grupo Operativo Zetas, the split after Z-42's March 2015 arrest) genuinely run from 2014-2015. Low confidence in any single year.
- FLAG: El Huevo's sentencing: he pleaded guilty on 19 Nov 2024 with sentencing scheduled for May 2025, but no reporting confirming a sentence was found. His fate now reads 'sentencing not publicly confirmed'. Worth re-checking PACER or a WDTX release.
- FLAG: El Mamito (Z-7) has no publicly reported sentence. He pleaded guilty in Feb 2013 and cooperated as a government witness; whether he was ever sentenced, and to what, is unresolved.
- FLAG: El Hummer's forfeiture figure of about US$792 million is reported consistently by Proceso, La Jornada, El Universal and Infobae but is implausibly large relative to the 450 kg of cocaine and 90,000 kg of marijuana attributed to him in the same reporting. Treated as 'reported at' rather than asserted.
- FLAG: Villa Unión attacker count: English Wikipedia says roughly 150 gunmen in 25 vehicles; Spanish Wikipedia and Excélsior say roughly 50 in 14 vehicles. The profile now says 'dozens'. Death toll also drifts across sources between 19, 22, 23 and 25 depending on the cut-off date.
- FLAG: CDN membership near 9,000 (or 8,992) in 2022: widely repeated in Mexican media but not traceable to a named government document; removed from facts and hedged in the peak note. Needs a primary SEDENA or SSPC source before reinstating.
- FLAG: Antonio Salas Perea (ZVE co-founder): no reporting on his fate, arrest or death was found in any of the preferred sources. The entry remains 'not publicly reported'.
- FLAG: 'Comandante Bravo 1' of Grupo Bravo: identity, dates and fate rest on Spanish Wikipedia and Tamaulipas local reporting only; treat as low confidence.
- FLAG: ZVE membership of about 3,000 across seven states is a Wikipedia figure with no independent corroboration; retained but explicitly attributed to Wikipedia in both the fact and the peak note.
- FLAG: Los Talibanes cell leaders 'Martín R' (5 Nov 2020), 'El Destripador' (24 Aug 2021) and 'El Gary' (12 May 2022): these rest on regional SLP/Zacatecas reporting that the session's remaining search budget could not re-verify. Dates carried forward unchanged; verify before publication.
- FLAG: The Zetas' 2007 Guatemala cell dates and 'Z-200' Daniel Pérez Rojas's April 2008 capture were carried forward unverified in this pass.
- FLAG: Coahuila prosecutors' estimate of about 150 people killed and dissolved at the Piedras Negras prison, and the 'cells in some 37 US cities' figure, were carried forward from the original profile without re-verification.
- FLAG: The claim that ZVE gunmen killed three alleged CJNG members in José Azueta, Veracruz, on 2 April 2019 was not re-verified in this pass.
- FLAG: Sangre Nueva Zeta details ('El Bukanas' 19 Oct 2019 threat, 'El Viejón' arrested 17 Dec 2021) rest on English Wikipedia and Puebla regional press; not independently re-verified.
- FLAG: This session exhausted its web-search budget (200 calls) before the 17 Aug 2024 Nuevo Laredo attack, the 38 attacks on military bases in March 2022, the 2020 SLP Navy municipality counts, the 255% Zacatecas homicide rise, and the 'El Mijis' shooting could be independently re-verified. All were carried forward as written.
- beltran_leyva - Pedro Inzunza Noriega was listed as a fugitive with to=0; he was captured by the military in Culiacán on 31 Dec 2025 and transferred to the United States on 21 Jan 2026. Evidence: DOJ, '37 Mexican Nationals Wanted for Serious Crimes Transferred to the United States from Mexico' (21 Jan 2026), which lists him as BLO and captured 31 Dec 2025; Mexico News Daily, 'Pedro Inzunza, a US-indicted narcoterrorist, is arrested in Sinaloa'.
- beltran_leyva - Added leader Pedro Inzunza Coronel 'El Pichón', indicted with his father in May 2025 and killed in a clash with the Mexican military on 30 Nov 2025. Evidence: DOJ/SDCA narcoterrorism indictment coverage; Mexico News Daily.
- beltran_leyva - Added leader Juan Pablo Bastidas Erenas 'Payo', high-ranking Beltrán Leyva lieutenant captured 28 May 2025 and transferred to the US on 21 Jan 2026. Evidence: DOJ 37-transfer press release.
- beltran_leyva - Added that Inzunza Noriega and his son were charged in May 2025 with narcoterrorism and material support of terrorism, the first application of US terrorism statutes to cartel figures; the BLO is prosecuted as a faction of the FTO-designated Sinaloa Cartel. Evidence: DOJ, 'Sinaloa Cartel Leaders Charged with Narco-Terrorism, Material Support of Terrorism and Drug Trafficking'.
- beltran_leyva - The Dec 2023 OFAC action was said to name Meza Flores as one of three leaders; the 6 Dec 2023 release names Gastélum Iribe and Inzunza Noriega as BLO leaders, while Treasury's 9 Apr 2025 release is the one that calls Meza Flores the organization's current leader. Evidence: Treasury jy1952 (6 Dec 2023) and sb0076 (9 Apr 2025).
- beltran_leyva - Marine Melquisedet/Melquisedec Angulo's family was said to have been murdered 'six days later'; they were killed on the night of 21 Dec 2009, hours after his funeral, five days after the 16 Dec raid, and the victims were his mother, two siblings and an aunt. Evidence: NBC News/AP, 'Gunmen kill family of Mexican drug war hero' (22 Dec 2009); CSMonitor 22 Dec 2009.
- beltran_leyva - Carlos Beltrán Leyva's arrest lacked the arresting agency and misstated the seizure; he was captured by the Federal Police in Culiacán on 30 Dec 2009 with a forged licence, a .45 pistol and a .308 rifle, not cocaine, and faced drug-trafficking and money-laundering charges. Evidence: La Jornada, 3 Jan 2010; Wikipedia/Federal Police account.
- beltran_leyva - Alfredo Beltrán Leyva's arrest attributed only to the Army; it was carried out by Army special forces together with the PGR in the Burócrata district of Culiacán on 21 Jan 2008, with ~US$900,000 seized. Evidence: SEDENA press release; Noroeste, 'La caída de El Mochomo'.
- beltran_leyva - La Barbie's arrest lacked the agency; he was taken by an elite federal police squad at a house in Lerma, State of Mexico, on 30 Aug 2010. Evidence: AP photo caption/CSMonitor, 31 Aug 2010.
- beltran_leyva - Alfredo's forfeiture was given as US$529 million; the court-ordered figure is US$529,200,000. Evidence: DOJ/DEA press release, 5 Apr 2017.
- beltran_leyva - H2's death was described as 'night of 9-10 Feb 2017' with 'his bodyguards'; sources give 9 Feb 2017 in Tepic with seven other gunmen killed in a Navy operation. The unverified claim that successor 'H9' Daniel Silva Gárate was killed in Feb 2017 was removed from the faction note. Evidence: es.wikipedia Cártel de los Beltrán Leyva; no corroborating source found for H9.
- beltran_leyva - InSight Crime's BLO profile was cited as updated 15 June 2026 'after the arrest of El 24'; it was last updated 5 June 2026, before that arrest, and calls the BLO defunct with splinters operating 'mostly without conflict' with the Sinaloa Cartel. The El 24 material belongs to InSight's separate Chapo Isidro profile, updated 16 June 2026. Evidence: both InSight Crime pages.
- beltran_leyva - 'El 24' was dated only to June 2026; he is Iván 'N', arrested 15 June 2026 in Sinaloa operations. Evidence: InSight Crime Chapo Isidro profile.
- beltran_leyva - The May 2025 alliance with La Mayiza was stated as fact; it is reported by Milenio and Infobae (announced c. 7 May 2025, violence from 5 May), reported to be fracturing by Infobae in August 2025, and not corroborated by InSight Crime, which in June 2026 said there were no reports of Guasave Cartel intervention. Text now attributes and hedges it. Evidence: Milenio 'La Mayiza y el Cártel de Guasave se unen contra Los Chapitos'; Infobae 7 May and 11 Aug 2025; InSight Crime Chapo Isidro profile.
- beltran_leyva - Jesús Alfredo Beltrán Guzmán was described as running a BLO cell without qualification; his 2016 arrest was in Zapopan, Jalisco, a reported 29 Nov 2024 detention at Mojolo, Culiacán, was denied by Sinaloa authorities, and Mexican reporting places him fighting for Los Chapitos against La Mayiza, i.e. opposite Chapo Isidro. Evidence: Semanario ZETA Nov 2024; Infobae 30 Nov and 1 Dec 2024; Treasury sb0076.
- beltran_leyva - The FTO fact was too flat; the BLO was not designated on 20 Feb 2025, but DOJ prosecutes it as a faction of the designated Sinaloa Cartel, which is what enabled the May 2025 terrorism charges. Evidence: Federal Register FTO designations (effective 20 Feb 2025); DOJ SDCA indictment release.
- beltran_leyva - The Édgar Guzmán López killing was attributed flatly to the BLO; the attribution is contested, with Vicente Zambada's account describing a mistaken hit. Hedge added. Evidence: Infobae, 6 May 2025, 'Quién mató a Édgar Guzmán, según la versión de El Vicentillo'.
- beltran_leyva - The record fentanyl seizure entry lacked detail; it was split across two Guasave/Ahome sites (about 800 kg and 300 kg) and valued at an estimated US$400 million loss. Evidence: Globe and Mail/Reuters and Al Jazeera, 5 Dec 2024.
- beltran_leyva - Los Rojos faction start year changed from 2009 to 2010 to match the post-Arturo fragmentation and the pacifico_sur profile; Mazari's 105-year sentence (July 2026) added to the note. Evidence: Proceso, 25 Jul 2026.
- beltran_leyva - Peak note contradicted its own state list (eleven states plus CDMX vs fifteen ISO codes); note reworded to distinguish InSight's peak count from the wider documented presence.
- beltran_leyva - Added a us_government rival entry and an inzunza_network faction to capture the 2023-2026 US enforcement track and the production arm dismantled in 2025-2026.
- pacifico_sur - Radilla's arrest was given only as 'May 2011'; he was detained by the Federal Police on 25 May 2011. Evidence: El Universal, 'Cronología Caso Sicilia'.
- pacifico_sur - The Sicilia killing was described as gunmen murdering the victims on 28 Mar 2011; the seven bodies were found bound and asphyxiated in a car in Temixco on 28 Mar 2011, and a judge freed six accused in August 2023. Evidence: La Jornada, 29 Mar 2011; Proceso, 22 Aug 2023.
- pacifico_sur - El Ponchis entry lacked detail and gave a vague release; he was detained with a sister at Cuernavaca airport trying to fly to Tijuana, received the three-year juvenile maximum, and was released on 26 Nov 2013 and sent to the United States. Evidence: CBS News; KPBS, 26 Nov 2013.
- pacifico_sur - Los Rojos note said El Carrete was arrested in 2019 without location; he was captured on 1 Aug 2019 in Leonardo Bravo, Guerrero (not Morelos), sentenced to 20 years in July 2023 and to 105 years in July 2026. Evidence: Infobae, 11 Aug 2019 and 12 Jul 2023; Proceso, 25 Jul 2026.
- pacifico_sur - Héctor's arrest now notes that his financier Germán Goyenechea was captured with him. Evidence: Dallas Morning News/AP and NBC News, 1-2 Oct 2014.
- pacifico_sur - La Barbie's arrest attributed to 'the Federal Police' without location detail; corrected to federal police at a house in Lerma. Evidence: AP/CSMonitor, 31 Aug 2010.
- pacifico_sur - Cuernavaca coordinates corrected from 18.9242,-99.2216 to 18.9186,-99.2342 (city centre).
- pacifico_sur - status_2026 updated with Mazari's 105-year sentence of July 2026. Evidence: Proceso, 25 Jul 2026.
- cida - El Koreano's arrest was credited to the Army on 2 Aug 2011; he was detained by the Federal Police on 1 August 2011, announced the following day. Evidence: Animal Político and El Informador, 2 Aug 2011.
- cida - The Plaza Sendero messages were said to be 'signed by CIDA'; the three narcomessages were signed in the name of 'El Chapo' Guzmán as a warning to groups extorting businesses, and CIDA's involvement rests on the later arrest and confession of gunman José Lozano, 21, with six others. Evidence: InSight Crime, 'Beheadings in Acapulco Point to Sinaloa Cartel'; UPI and AP, Jan 2011.
- cida - The Acatianguis entry wrongly placed the murder of merchants' leader Antonio Valdés on 7 Apr 2011; the arson was 7 Apr 2011 (about 110 stalls destroyed, four dead), while Antonio Valdés Andrade, the ex-councilman who created the market, was killed in October 2010. Evidence: Proceso and La Razón, 4-5 Apr 2011; Federal Police statement of 2 Aug 2011.
- cida - The 2010 kidnap victims were described as 'tourists from Michoacán'; they were 20 men from Morelia, and the grave at Tuncingo, beside Tres Palos, was located from 3 Nov 2010 after a video posted online named the site. Evidence: CNN, 3 Nov 2010; Al Jazeera, 4-5 Nov 2010.
- cida - El Negro Pipas's sentence was given as 27 years on 30 Oct 2025; it is 27 years and 6 months, reported 29-30 Oct 2025, for the 5 Feb 2018 killing of Pamela Montenegro del Real. Evidence: Infobae, 30 Oct 2025; Tribuna, 29 Oct 2025.
- cida - El Panadero's fate said 'on drug charges'; he was 59 and held on drug, homicide, kidnapping, vehicle-theft, extortion and weapons charges. Evidence: Infobae, 28 Dec 2024.
- cida - El Traumado's entry lacked identity and place; he is Abner Noé Cervantes, 38, arrested in the Venustiano Carranza borough of Mexico City on 15 Feb 2025, and Mexican press describes him as a founding figure of CIDA, so his 'from' year was moved to 2010. Evidence: Infobae, El Heraldo and El Informador, 15 Feb 2025.
- cida - El Chambitas's extortion was undated; the 5,000-peso demand at the Central de Abasto was made on 25 Mar 2025, with arrest on 26 Apr 2025. Evidence: Infobae and El Heraldo de México, 27 Apr 2025.
- cida - Los Rusos were said to have been 'founded by Juan Carlos Rodríguez'; the group is a Beltrán Leyva splinter led by Carlos Alberto Navarrete Soriano 'El Ruso'/'El Colorado' and Jesús Orlando Rodríguez Soriano. Evidence: El Sol de Chilpancingo, 6 Aug 2022; El Financiero, 3 Sep 2026.
- cida - status_2026 updated: Los Rusos, not CIDA, are Acapulco's dominant group as of September 2026, holding the Zona Diamante in a reported alliance with the Cártel de Caborca, and their enforcement wing Los Amarillos lost chief 'El Gobén' to a capture in Puebla in early September 2026. A caborca rival entry was added. Evidence: El Financiero, 3 Sep 2026.
- cida - El Tucán's arrest place refined to Toluca de Lerdo, State of Mexico, 9 Jul 2026, and the transport figure confirmed at about 20,000 units across six groups. Evidence: Infobae, 9 Jul 2026.
- cida - Acapulco coordinates corrected from 16.8634,-99.8901 to 16.8531,-99.8237 (Acapulco de Juárez centre).
- cida - Summary softened from 'the 2011 Plaza Sendero beheadings' as a CIDA signature to 'implicated in', reflecting the contested attribution.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - The May 2025 Guasave-Mayiza alliance is a direct source conflict: Milenio and Infobae report it and Anabel Hernández built a 'quiet winner' thesis on it, while InSight Crime's Chapo Isidro profile (16 Jun 2026) states there are no reports the Guasave Cartel intervened in the Sinaloa infighting, and Infobae itself reported the pact fracturing in Aug 2025. The profile now presents both; an atlas editor should decide which to privilege.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - Jesús Alfredo Beltrán Guzmán is simultaneously an OFAC-designated 'key leader of the BLO' (9 Apr 2025) and, per Mexican reporting, a fighter for Los Chapitos against La Mayiza. If the Guasave faction is on the Mayiza side, the two BLO-labelled cells are on opposite sides of the war. The taxonomy of 'BLO' in 2025-26 is therefore US-legal rather than operational.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - founded=2008 treats the BLO as dating from the January 2008 split. English Wikipedia's infobox gives 2003-2012 and Spanish Wikipedia 2008-2017; InSight Crime dates the brothers' rise within the Federation to 2002-2005. The 2008 date is defensible for the independent cartel but is a modelling choice, not a sourced fact.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - Mario Alberto Beltrán Leyva's alias 'El General' is also attributed to Héctor in several sources, and no reliable account of Mario Alberto's fate after 2009 was found. Both the alias and the 'never captured' claim are weakly sourced.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - La Barbie's specific facility (USP Coleman II) could not be re-verified; reporting confirms only that he left BOP records in Nov 2022 and returned in early 2023, with a listed release date of 27 Jul 2056. The facility claim was removed rather than asserted.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - Sergio Villarreal 'El Grande': the extradition date of 23 May 2012 and the c. Dec 2019 release as a protected witness could not be re-verified (the Justice in Mexico page returned 403). The year 2012 and his Jan 2023 García Luna testimony are solid; the exact day and release date are not.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - Carlos Beltrán Leyva's status after his 2010 prosecution is genuinely undocumented in accessible sources; no release, sentence or death was found.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - The caborca alliance entry rests on Mexican press reports of a Sept 2024 Badiraguato meeting and on a claim that José Gil Caro Quintero was sanctioned as a BLO cocaine logistician in Dec 2023, which could not be confirmed against the OFAC list. Treated as reported, not established.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - The cdn alliance from 2016 remains sourced only to Wikipedia and should be considered unreliable.
- FLAG: beltran_leyva - The claim that 'H9' Daniel Silva Gárate succeeded H2 and was killed in Feb 2017 could not be verified in any accessible source and was dropped from the faction note.
- FLAG: pacifico_sur - The 'more than 150 dead in Morelos within months' figure in the 2010 succession war is repeated across secondary sources but no primary tally was located.
- FLAG: pacifico_sur - The claim that Héctor's reward was US$5 million (State Department) and 30 million pesos (Mexico) was carried over unverified; the figures are widely repeated but were not re-confirmed.
- FLAG: cida - The founding year is contested: Spanish Wikipedia dates CIDA to 2012, while contemporaneous reporting and the sequence of events (La Barbie's arrest Aug 2010, CIDA-signed killings from Sept 2010) support 2010, which is retained.
- FLAG: cida - Abner Noé Cervantes 'El Traumado' is called a 'líder fundador' of CIDA by El Informador and others, which sits awkwardly with El Koreano's status as founder; his 'from' year was set to 2010 on that basis but the press label may be loose.
- FLAG: cida - Several mid-period leaders (Javier Muñoz 'El Cholo', Ulises Laredo 'El Virus', Víctor Hugo Aguirre Garzón) have no traceable outcome in accessible sources; their date ranges are approximate.
- FLAG: cida - The exact date of the Plaza Sendero discovery is inconsistent across sources: UPI, El Universal, La Jornada and Spanish-language wires place it on 8 Jan 2011, while InSight Crime describes bodies found 'Sunday morning' (9 Jan) and gives a weekend toll of 28 killed and 16 decapitated. 8 Jan 2011 is retained for the mall discovery with the 28-death weekend figure.
- FLAG: cida - The 2011 Acapulco homicide figures (over 1,000 murders, rate above 100 per 100,000) are widely cited in secondary literature but were not verified against SESNSP data in this pass.
- FLAG: cida - 'Los Granados' and 'Los Maldonado' appear only in the July 2026 Infobae listing of six groups and have no independent profile in the sources consulted.
- guerreros_unidos - Adán Casarrubias 'El Tomate' sentencing date changed from 6 March 2025 to 5 March 2025 - Chicago Sun-Times story dated 5 March 2025 says he was sentenced 'Wednesday', which was 5 March 2025 (the Spanish edition ran a day later, which is likely the source of the error)
- guerreros_unidos - 'El Pajarraco' arrest date changed from 3 June 2025 to 4 June 2025 and located in Almoloya, Hidalgo; name added as Juan Miguel Pantoja Miranda - Infobae, N+ and El Financiero, 4-5 June 2025
- guerreros_unidos - Pablo Vega Cuevas's fate rewritten: sentenced to 10 years on 19 August 2025 and released the same day on time served, with $1.75 million forfeited, after testifying against the cartel and helping the DEA interpret wiretaps - Chicago Sun-Times, 19 August 2025; the profile's vague 'roughly 10-year sentence; released in 2025' understated the documented facts
- guerreros_unidos - Abarca's 92-year sentence specified as 92 years and six months and its later fate added: an amparo suspension of its execution obtained in September 2025 - Infobae/Proceso 17 May 2023; La Jornada and El Universal, September 2025
- guerreros_unidos - Added that an appeals tribunal in Tamaulipas confirmed Abarca's acquittal for the kidnapping of the 43 students on 3 June 2025 - Infobae, 4 June 2025; La Crónica, 3 June 2025
- guerreros_unidos - Six-member sentence fact corrected: sentences of 25 to 119 years and three months were reiterated/confirmed by a judge in Iguala on 16 May 2025 for kidnappings in Taxco, reinstating a 2022 conviction, not for 'crimes committed in 2013–2017' - La Jornada and Proceso, 16 May 2025; Reforma
- guerreros_unidos - Ángel Aguirre entry corrected: detained 6 August 2026 in the State of Mexico and interned in Altiplano on 7 August; the charge is ordering the disappearance of Palacio de Justicia security-camera footage in Iguala that recorded bus 1531 - El Imparcial, Infobae and UnoTV, 7 August 2026
- guerreros_unidos - 'El Gama' entry corrected: Rolando Salgado sentenced on 2 July 2026 to 54 years and 10 months for organized crime, kidnapping (April 2015, Iguala) and drug possession; he was arrested in July 2015 - La Silla Rota, Infobae, La Jornada, 2 July 2026
- guerreros_unidos - Crematorium claim reattributed: the 31 August 2026 account of more than 20 bags of calcined remains and an unregistered crematorium at the El Ángel funeral home was a La Jornada report, not a prosecutors' announcement, and the families publicly rejected it days later as a mixture of old evidence - La Jornada 31 Aug 2026; Infobae 2 Sept 2026
- guerreros_unidos - Mario Casarrubias entry expanded: captured in Toluca in April 2014 and sentenced in 2017 to 10 years for weapons and drug offences before dying of COVID-19 on 25 July 2021 - Excélsior 23 April 2014; La Jornada 26 July 2021
- guerreros_unidos - 'El Gil' entry expanded: freed in September 2019 by a judge in Tamaulipas who discarded more than forty items of evidence, became an FGR protected witness in 2020, fled in May 2024 and was re-arrested at an address in Santa Martha Acatitla, Mexico City - CNN Español, Excélsior and Proceso, 6-8 September 2024
- guerreros_unidos - NYT publication date specified as 2 September 2023 and Vega Cuevas's warehouses located in Aurora, Batavia and Chicago - Proceso 2 Sept 2023; Chicago Sun-Times
- guerreros_unidos - '158 arrests, 120 imprisoned' attributed precisely to figures given to the families at their 4 September 2025 meeting with President Sheinbaum - A dónde van los desaparecidos, 25 September 2025
- guerreros_unidos - Mondragón's death specified as occurring when surrounded by federal police, and the Tilde arrest month softened to '2012' pending confirmation - El Economista and Milenio, October 2014
- rojos - Leonor Nava Romero corrected from 'sister of El Rojo' to brother (male); added alias 'El Tío', arrest at a checkpoint in Nuxco, Tecpan de Galeana, and a 15-year-and-three-day sentence in November 2025 - Excélsior 18 May 2014, Milenio profile, La Jornada 24-25 November 2025
- rojos - El Carrete's 20-year sentence moved from 2020 to 12 July 2023, imposed by the Eighth District Court in Uruapan, Michoacán - Infobae, 12 July 2023
- rojos - El Carrete's capture located in Leonardo Bravo, Guerrero, wounded and extracted by army helicopter, rather than left unlocated - reporting on the 1 August 2019 operation
- rojos - Tehuitzingo massacre attribution removed: the 17 May 2026 killing of ten of the Torres family at Texcalapa was ruled out as organized crime by Puebla prosecutors on ballistic grounds (handguns, not rifles); 'El Pony' (Juan Manuel N.), arrested 19 May 2026, is a nephew of victim Cecilio Torres Gervacio and the presumed motive is a family dispute - El Financiero, La Silla Rota, Quadratín Puebla, 19 May 2026
- rojos - Calep N. arrest date changed from 15 to 14 August 2026 and located in Tilapa, Puebla - La Silla Rota and Angulo 7, 14 August 2026
- rojos - R13's killing relocated from 'Avenida Gobernadores' to the El Tomatal neighbourhood of Chilpancingo - Infobae, 10 October 2024
- rojos - Omar Cuenca Marino's extradition softened to 'late February 2025' with the December 2016 Georgia indictment added, because sources give both 23 and 25 February - Infobae and Univision Atlanta, 25 February 2025
- rojos - Added the 2026 detention of 'El Piojo', named as the Acatlán cell's second in command, and the Mazari son's alias 'El Carretito' with the 3 April 2022 date confirmed - Diario Cambio; Infobae 6 April 2022
- ardillos - parent changed from 'beltran_leyva' to empty: the group was founded around 2000 as an independent family clan that later operated under the Beltrán Leyva umbrella, not a splinter of an organization that only became autonomous in 2008 - Infobae 12 May 2026; es.wikipedia
- ardillos - The July 2023 Chilpancingo hostage crisis was added to this profile (it had been assigned to Los Tlacos): on 10 July 2023 protesters seized an armoured vehicle, entered the state palace and held 13 public servants (five state police, four National Guard, three state and one federal official) at Petaquillas, demanding the release of Jesús Echeverría Peñafiel 'El Topo' and Bernardo Chávez Cruz, detained on 5 July; the hostages were freed on 11 July and López Obrador publicly blamed Los Ardillos - Zeta Tijuana and El Financiero, 11 July 2023
- ardillos - May 2026 casualty figures replaced: the unverified '76 killed, 25 disappeared, more than 1,300 displaced, state acknowledged about 120' becomes the documented 96 displaced, four bodies on the Chilapa–Tlapa highway and six wounded officially, about 120 displaced per state figures, and more than 800 families or over a thousand people per CIPOG-EZ and Tlachinollan; 118 people returned to Xicotlán by 14 May - Infobae and Reforma 12 May 2026; Expansión 17 May 2026; ContrapuntoNews
- ardillos - San Pedro Huitzapula relocated from Chilapa to the municipality of Atlixtac and identified as a Me'phaa community besieged for 17 days - Proceso 16 May 2026; Tlachinollan via EDUCA
- ardillos - Federal deployment detail expanded to 690 soldiers, 400 National Guard and 200 state police plus 164 vehicles and five helicopters, confirmed by Sheinbaum on 13 May 2026 - Tribuna, 13 May 2026
- ardillos - Membership figure reattributed from 'investigators estimate' to an expert's estimate of no fewer than 2,000 members, with the 29-of-85-municipalities presence retained - Milenio/El Universal profiles
- ardillos - Germán Reyes Reyes described as arrested on 12 November 2024 for qualified homicide while interim head of the municipal Secretaría de Seguridad Pública, rather than flatly as 'intellectual author' - El Financiero, 12 November 2024
- ardillos - Bustos Pérez episode corrected in both profiles: the eight-minute video was recorded while he was visibly under coercion during a criminal interrogation, naming his superior and a Guerrero prosecution official as working for Los Tlacos against Los Ardillos and 'Los Rusos'; bodies were found on the Tierra Colorada–Plan de Lima road - Infobae and Latinus, 6-7 September 2026
- ardillos - Added detail confirmed from sources: Ortega Rosas founded the group with three of his seven sons; the 26 Nov 2015 CRAC ambush took place in Tixtla; Ayahualtempa's armed-children protest was repeated in April 2021 - es.wikipedia; Infobae 12 May 2026
- ardillos - Bernardo Ortega's federal deputy term (2018-21) marked as 'reported' rather than asserted, since the Infobae and Wikipedia profiles list only the 2002-05 mayoralty and the 2005-08 and 2012-15 state deputy terms
- tlacos - parent changed from 'rojos' to empty: sources describe the group as growing out of community-police and self-defence structures in Heliodoro Castillo after the Beltrán Leyva fragmentation, with El Necho having had ties to Rojos and Guerreros Unidos leaders, not as a Rojos splinter - Infobae 12 May 2026; Expansión 17 May 2026
- tlacos - The claim that Tlacos-mobilized transport operators seized a National Guard armoured vehicle, stormed the state palace and held 13 hostages until two leaders were released was removed: the federal government attributed the July 2023 Chilpancingo events to Los Ardillos, the two detainees were Ardillos-linked, and the government never confirmed they were released - Zeta Tijuana and El Financiero, 11 July 2023
- tlacos - R13's death relocated to the El Tomatal neighbourhood of Chilpancingo - Infobae, 10 October 2024
- tlacos - Added verified events from es.wikipedia: the 2 February 2020 attack on a rodeo at Zacacoyuca, the 22 December 2021 rescue of two detainees by about 100 gunmen at Chichihualco, the November 2024 dismantling of the group's Autopista del Sol car-theft ring, the 28 October 2025 Chilpancingo arrests, and the Los Filos–El Bermejal gold mine as a strategic prize
- tlacos - The 30 September 2021 Iguala video annotated with the competing account that the 20 La Bandera captives were executed and displayed outside the mayor-elect's campaign office - es.wikipedia
- tlacos - San Miguel Totolapan attack of 27 August 2026 given its confirmed detail: about 4 a.m., seven houses, six of one family killed including a disabled 18-year-old woman, attributed by residents to 'La Changa' over a refusal to sell land for logging - El Financiero and La Jornada, 27-28 August 2026
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - Cleotilde Toribio Rentería 'El Tilde' arrest: could not confirm the month (July 2012) or the Mexico City location; the year alone is retained in the text
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - Pablo Vega Cuevas's arrest is given as 2014 by the Chicago Sun-Times but Mexican outlets describe 'almost 12 years of judicial proceedings', implying 2013; the specific 'December 2014 in Aurora' claim was softened to 'in the Chicago area in 2014'
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - 'El Gama': Mexican outlets split between Rolando Salgado Lea (La Silla Rota) and Rolando Salgado Olea (Azteca Noticias); the surname is uncertain
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - The deportation of witness Édgar Damián Sandoval Albarrán 'La Rana' in June 2024 could not be verified and was dropped from the history rather than asserted
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - Salomón Pineda Villa 'El Molón' being still at large rests on InSight Crime's profile and was not independently confirmed for 2026
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - The reported CJNG alliance (2016-2022) rests solely on InSight Crime and was not independently confirmed
- FLAG: guerreros_unidos - Whether the government's public Ayotzinapa report is formally due before 26 September 2026 is not confirmed; La Jornada (27 Aug 2026) reports only that Sheinbaum is preparing one after Aguirre's arrest
- FLAG: rojos - The DEA 2020 National Drug Threat Assessment listing of Los Rojos among 'nine' Mexican organizations was not re-verified; the numeral was removed and the substance retained
- FLAG: rojos - Jesús Nava Marino 'R2' being found dead in Iguala's dump in 2010, and the 23 February 2015 Tetela del Río clash with 15 Rojos dead, could not be re-verified within the search budget
- FLAG: rojos - The alleged army linkage of detained CIPOG-EZ leader Jesús Plácido Galindo to Los Rojos and Los Tlacos in July 2026 could not be verified; it is stated as an accusation his supporters reject
- FLAG: rojos - Alexis Oswaldo Mazari's August 2018 arrest and Alfonso Miranda Gallegos's imprisonment were not re-verified
- FLAG: ardillos - The reported federal deputy term of Bernardo Ortega Jiménez (2018-2021) is unverified; the Infobae and Wikipedia profiles list only his mayoralty and state deputy terms
- FLAG: ardillos - Reported early Ortega links to Los Zetas rest on a single Infobae account and are contested
- FLAG: ardillos - The claim that 'in August 2026 more than 300 families had fled and 52 remained displaced' could not be verified and was removed; Mongabay (June 2026) documents only that returns were incomplete
- FLAG: ardillos - The seizure of 12 guns and 36,700 cartridges in the 5 September 2026 Acapulco operation was not confirmed in the sources retrieved; the arrest of 16 people including a financial operator is confirmed
- FLAG: ardillos - es.wikipedia lists Guerreros Unidos as an Ardillos ally, which contradicts every other source consulted; not incorporated
- FLAG: ardillos - The 3 October 2024 killing of incoming security chief Ulises Hernández Martínez and the June 2023 severed-heads message were not independently re-verified
- FLAG: tlacos - The founding month (May 2017) comes from es.wikipedia; Infobae and Expansión say only that the group consolidated 'around 2017'
- FLAG: tlacos - 'El Marras' is named Pedro Guovani Hernández by some outlets and Pedro Geovanny Gutiérrez Hernández by others; the full name is uncertain
- FLAG: tlacos - Javier Marquina 'El Barbas' dying in June 2021 in a Chilpancingo hospital after a Tetela del Río clash, the November 2018 Filo de Caballos displacement of 1,200, the February-March 2024 truces, the 28 July 2026 El Fresno drone attack, the August 2026 arrest of 'El Traka' and the six members over a 300,000-peso extortion demand, and the December 2025 Tlacotepec armed caravan were all carried over unverified after the web-search budget was exhausted
- FLAG: tlacos - The reported CJNG alliance and the 'Federación Guerrerense' rebranding rest on es.wikipedia and were not independently confirmed
- templarios - La Tuta's second Mexican sentence dated Dec 19, 2022 corrected to Dec 9, 2022 (FGR announcement; Cambio de Michoacán 9 Dec 2022; Zeta Tijuana profile Aug 2025). Also corrected in familia_michoacana leader entry and facts.
- templarios - 55-year sentence dated only 'June 2019' now specified as June 17, 2019, and identified as for the 2011 kidnapping of a Uruapan businessman (Infobae, 17 Jun 2019; Zeta Tijuana).
- familia_michoacana / templarios - 'La Tuta pleaded not guilty in SDNY early 2026' made precise: he pleaded not guilty on March 17, 2026 before Judge John G. Koeltl (El Informador, Excélsior, El Universal, 17 Mar 2026).
- familia_michoacana / templarios - added the hearing chronology behind the Sept 16, 2026 date: hearing moved from Dec 8, 2025 to Mar 17, 2026 (order of Dec 9, 2025), then set for June 24, 2026 and postponed on joint request of prosecutors and defense counsel Thomas Ambrosio (Infobae, 25 Jun 2026; Parriva).
- templarios - Channel 4 News interview dated Oct 30, 2014 corrected: the Channel 4 jungle interview by Guillermo Galdos was published Feb 25, 2014; the October 2014 item was a 24-minute self-recorded video/audio vowing to fight to the end (channel4.com; InSight Crime).
- templarios - iron ore figure corrected and expanded: about 119,000 tons plus 124 machines seized at Lázaro Cárdenas in March 2014, 75,800 tons intercepted aboard the ship Jian Hua at Manzanillo (announced May 2014), over 220,000 tons seized in Michoacán and Guerrero in H1 2014 (Mining.com; InSight Crime, 2 May 2014).
- templarios - Pope Benedict XVI banners: the 14 banners of Mar 17, 2012 were hung on bridges in Guanajuato municipalities, not across Michoacán, ahead of the Mar 23-26 visit (Excélsior 18 Mar 2012; Wikipedia; Justice in Mexico).
- templarios - Ignacio Rentería Andrade 'El Cenizo': arrest date made precise (June 21, 2017, in Parácuaro, after a shootout with soldiers) and 20-year sentence added, reported Nov 29, 2025; faction area changed from 'La Huacana' to Uruapan/Parácuaro (Proceso, El Universal, Infobae).
- templarios - Pablo Toscano Padilla 'El 500': death narrowed from '2017' to September 2017 (Wikipedia, Knights Templar Cartel).
- templarios - Los Viagras faction note: 'allied with CJNG in August 2024' softened to a reported accommodation in 2024, as the claim could not be confirmed with a primary source.
- familia_michoacana - Carlos Rosales Mendoza's fate corrected: released May 22, 2014, briefly re-arrested at a Morelia restaurant on Aug 5, 2014 and freed without charge, killed Dec 27, 2015 with the body found Dec 28, 2015 in Gabriel Zamora; birth data (Feb 12, 1963, La Unión, Guerrero) and the Puente Grande transfer of Oct 28, 2004 added (Wikipedia; Proceso, 28 Dec 2015).
- familia_michoacana - Feb 25, 2010 OFAC action corrected: seven leaders (Moreno, Méndez Vargas, Gómez Martínez, Enrique Plancarte, Loya Plancarte, Rueda Medina, Barrera Medrano) plus Transportadora Purepecha S.A. de C.V. were designated, not four (OFAC recent actions 20100225).
- familia_michoacana - Kingpin Act designation dated precisely to April 15, 2009 (OFAC recent actions 20090415).
- familia_michoacana - Apatzingán battle re-dated: over 2,000 federal police surrounded El Alcalde on Dec 9, 2010, with two days of fighting, rather than 'Dec 8-9' (Wikipedia, Nazario Moreno González).
- familia_michoacana - Rafael Cedeño Hernández arrest detail corrected: a Federal Police raid of about 400 agents on a family baptism at a Morelia social hall in which 44 people were detained; exact day varies by source (La Jornada 20 Apr 2009 says Apr 18; Wikipedia says Apr 20).
- familia_michoacana - José María Chávez Magaña 'El Pony': arrest given a date and agency — captured by the Navy on July 2, 2014, reported in Pénjamo, Guanajuato (some accounts say Tejupilco); native of Tepalcatepec, Michoacán (El Universal, SinEmbargo, 2014).
- familia_michoacana - Chango Méndez sentence detail added: handed down in December 2022 with a 751,218-peso fine, upheld by the Tribunal Colegiado de Apelación in Tepic, Nayarit on May 26, 2024; held at CEFERESO 13 in Oaxaca before transfer (El Financiero, MVS, Zeta Tijuana).
- familia_michoacana - Nazario Moreno's first book title corrected to 'Pensamientos del Más Loco' (Wikipedia).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - origin coordinates corrected from 18.283/-100.267 to 18.315/-100.284 for Arcelia, Guerrero (es.wikipedia infobox: 18°18'54"N 100°17'02"W).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - Johnny Hurtado's birth data confirmed and sourced: Mar 1, 1973, Arcelia, Guerrero, per his OFAC listing; alias corrected from 'Don Gabino' to the OFAC/DOJ aliases El Pez, El Mojarra/Mojarro and El Pescado.
- nueva_familia_michoacana - Taxco abduction re-dated: the mayor's father Juan Vega Arredondo was seized Apr 11, 2026 and Mayor Juan Andrés Vega Carranza on Apr 12; both were freed Apr 13 in a State of México community, reportedly to force the release of plaza boss 'El Gokú' detained Apr 8 (Infobae, Proceso, Excélsior, 14 Apr 2026).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - July 8, 2026 attack relocated: the assault was on the ejido of Guajes de Ayala, with residents sheltering in a clinic in the neighboring community of El Pescado; an estimated 100-150 drone bombs were dropped over a day-long siege (La Jornada 9 Jul 2026; Excélsior).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - Aug 4, 2026 camp inventory expanded: about 120 bomb cylinders, 140 kg of gunpowder, 500 tubes of adhesive and gallons of sulfuric acid (Yahoo Noticias/El Universal, Aug 2026).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - Franco Tabares Martínez's role corrected: DOJ lists him as of Guerrero, Mexico, charged with methamphetamine conspiracy; the Atlanta-based money launderer is a separate defendant, Guadalupe Tabares Martínez of Mableton, Georgia, indicted Apr 8, 2025 (DOJ press release, 15 Apr 2025).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - NDGA indictment described precisely: a three-count indictment returned Sept 24, 2024 and unsealed Apr 15, 2025 (DOJ NDGA/OPA).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - 'El Orejón' identified and dated: Miguel Ángel Reyna Román, arrested May 15, 2025 in Teloloapan by the National Guard and Army with three others; added as a leader entry (Infobae, El Universal, 20 May 2025).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - Tuzantla shootout detail corrected: fighting began in La Yerbabuena and spread to the town square; the eight dead included three minors, and the bodies were found at a butterfly sanctuary near Tuzantla (Michoacán FGE via El Financiero/Infobae; Wikipedia).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - San Miguel Totolapan: mayor's surname corrected to Conrado Mendoza Almeda, his father named as former mayor Juan Mendoza Acosta, and the attack described as hitting both a private house and the town hall (Wikipedia, San Miguel Totolapan massacre).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - Aculco sentences dated and detailed: five men sentenced to 70 years each (plus 678,840-peso fines) reported Aug 20-21, 2026 by a judge in Jilotepec for an express kidnapping in San Lucas, Aculco, on Aug 28, 2025, in which the attackers identified themselves as La Familia Michoacana (Infobae, FGJEM).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - 'El Queso de Puerco' identified: Mario Iván Mendoza Martínez, arrested July 13, 2026 in the Central de Abasto area of Iztapalapa after a speeding stop, carrying cocaine, marijuana, crystal and a firearm (Crónica, Excélsior, Infobae).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - reward framing clarified: US$5m plus US$3m equals the US$8 million total cited in Mexican coverage, not separate additional sums (State Dept via La Jornada, 15 Apr 2025).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - added the September 2025 killing of Colombian musicians B-King and Regio Clown, whose bodies were found in Cocotitlán with a narcomessage signed in the group's name, with the attribution dispute (Unión Tepito suspects) noted (Infobae, El Financiero, Sept 2025).
- nueva_familia_michoacana - US footprint expanded beyond Atlanta to Houston, New Mexico, North Carolina and Washington, DC, per Mexican reporting on DEA files (Infobae/El Universal, Apr 2025).
- FLAG: familia_michoacana parent='zetas' is defensible but contested: La Empresa was built by Carlos Rosales with Gulf Cartel backing and Zetas trainers after he broke from the Valencias/Milenio. A case exists for parent='gulf' or even 'milenio'. Left as 'zetas' because the Zetas supplied the armed structure, but this is an editorial call, not a sourced fact.
- FLAG: Michoacanazo counts: '10 mayors and roughly 20 other officials' could not be pinned to a primary source in this session (no Wikipedia article; search budget exhausted). Commonly cited figures range from 27 to about 35 detained in total, including 10 or 11 mayors. Verify before publication.
- FLAG: Project Delirium '221 US arrests in July 2011' was not re-verified. DOJ described 1,985 arrests over the 20-month investigation with a final takedown of about 221; confirm which number the fact intends.
- FLAG: The super-lab figure of 'about 45 kg of methamphetamine in eight hours' is widely repeated in secondary reporting but was not traced to a DEA or Mexican primary source.
- FLAG: The '85 percent of Michoacán businesses paid extortion' figure is a widely circulated estimate from 2013-2014 press coverage; no primary study was located.
- FLAG: Templarios dissolved=2017 rests mainly on English Wikipedia's 'as of September 2017' framing plus the Rentería Andrade and Toscano deaths/arrests. Some Michoacán reporting treats residual Templar-branded cells as persisting into 2018-2019; treat 2017 as approximate.
- FLAG: Los Viagras' reported 2024 alliance or accommodation with the CJNG could not be confirmed with a primary or tier-1 source in this session; the faction note has been hedged accordingly.
- FLAG: 'El Cenizo' name order varies across Mexican outlets: Ignacio Rentería Andrade (Proceso, El Universal, Milenio) vs Ignacio Andrade Rentería (some wire copy). Retained Rentería Andrade.
- FLAG: Pablo Toscano Padilla's lieutenant 'El Cheques' (Ezequiel) is carried over from the original profile and was not independently verified.
- FLAG: Rafael Cedeño Hernández's exact arrest day is disputed: La Jornada places the Morelia baptism raid on April 18, 2009; English Wikipedia says April 20; other outlets say April 19. Text now says 'April 2009'.
- FLAG: Dionisio Loya Plancarte's current status: no post-2014 sentence, release or death was found. A December 2016 rumor of his death at Altiplano circulates online and appears unfounded; a 2013 report of his death in a gunfight was demonstrably false. Confirm before stating he is alive and imprisoned in 2026.
- FLAG: nueva_familia_michoacana founded=2011 follows English Wikipedia and the continuity of the Guerrero/Edomex wing, but US agencies effectively date the Hurtado-run LNFM from 2014, and El Universal describes the brothers as having operated for about 20 years. Founding year is a framing choice.
- FLAG: The CJNG–LNFM drone-supply relationship from 2023 rests on InSight Crime reporting and Mexican press naming 'El Fresa' as liaison; it coexists with continued CJNG–LNFM hostility in Guerrero. The simultaneous ally (2023-) and rival (2019-2022) entries are an imperfect fit for a fluid relationship.
- FLAG: The May 2023 displacement of 'about 600 people' from Nuevo Poblado El Caracol, Atoyac, and the March 2023 failed Petatlán operation are carried over from the original profile and were not re-verified in this session.
- FLAG: Attribution of the September 2025 B-King / Regio Clown killings to La Nueva Familia Michoacana rests on a narcomessage left at the scene; Mexico City investigators also identified Unión Tepito operators and specialists called the message a possible decoy. Presented as disputed.
- FLAG: The Mexican Navy's 56-municipality tally (20 MIC / 18 GRO / 14 MEX / 4 MOR) is from 2025 Mexican press citing Navy documents; no primary Semar release was located.
- FLAG: 'El Zarco's' 2019 public threat against 'El Mencho' over the Balsas River line is carried over from the original profile and was not independently verified.
- FLAG: Ubaldo Hurtado Olascoaga's Estado de México arrest warrant 'since June 2024' was not independently verified; note that a June 2024 OFAC action on LNFM does exist, which may be the source of the date.
- milenio - Armando Valencia Cornelio's fate rewritten: profile said he was 'never extradited' and 'reported released on health grounds (cancer) by 2020'. He WAS extradited to the US, sentenced there to 19 years 7 months (after a 47-year-6-month Mexican sentence at the Altiplano), and released 21 April 2020 from the Federal Medical Center, Lexington, Kentucky, with terminal lymphatic cancer - evidence: Borderland Beat (Jun 2020) 'Armando Valencia Cornelio, Founder of the Milenio Cartel, Is Released From Prison After 17 Years'; Infobae 9 Apr 2026 'Cuál fue la enfermedad terminal...'; Milenio 'Quién es Armando Valencia Cornelio'.
- milenio - Arresting agency and place corrected: profile said the AFI captured him 'in the Guadalajara area'; sources say Army special forces (GAFE) with the PGR, at Tlajomulco de Zúñiga - evidence: Wikipedia Milenio Cartel citing PGR; Milenio.com profile.
- milenio - Rosalinda González Valencia: 'served five years' corrected to sentenced to five years (Dec 2023) and released early on 27 Feb 2025 after more than three years, from CEFERESO 16 in Morelos - evidence: Infobae/ground.news reporting on her 30 Jan 2025 judicial release order and 27 Feb 2025 release.
- milenio - El Lobo's US sentencing date made exact: 7 January 2014 (S.D. Texas, Houston), 25 years cut to 200 months - evidence: Borderland Beat/Infobae Dec 2023 releases coverage.
- milenio - Added that El Lobo put his total García Luna payments at about $10 million, alongside the verified $2.5M (2006, via Arturo Beltrán Leyva) and 3 million pesos (2008, Guadalajara car wash) - evidence: Borderland Beat Jan 2023 trial coverage.
- milenio - El Tigre's capture detailed: 6 May 2010 in Guadalajara's Providencia district, together with his sister Jaqueline Patricia Nava Valencia; later held at the federal prison in Matamoros - evidence: El Informador, 10-11 May 2010 and 28 Jul 2010.
- milenio - El Molca's arrest attributed to the Federal Police and La Resistencia's founding dated to June 2010 - evidence: InSight Crime 'Mexico Captures Leader, Founder of Resistencia Gang'; Justice in Mexico.
- milenio - Arcos del Milenio dump described precisely (26 bodies in three trucks on a Guadalajara avenue, 24 Nov 2011) - evidence: Wikipedia CJNG article summary of the incident.
- milenio - Marriage chronology corrected: Rosalinda González Valencia was previously married to Armando Valencia Cornelio and married Nemesio Oseguera in 1996 after their separation; the history had implied the marriage occurred during the post-2003 Guadalajara phase - evidence: Infobae 19 Mar 2026 'No es El Mencho? Quién es el verdadero padre...'.
- milenio - El 85 entry: release date made exact (29 Dec 2017, on due-process grounds), and his US case updated (guilty plea 7 Apr 2026, sentencing set 31 Jul 2026) - evidence: Wikipedia Érick Valencia Salazar; JURIST 7 Apr 2026.
- milenio - Fact #8 replaced: the fact citing 'Wikipedia's territorial listing' was swapped for a verified fact on Armando Valencia's Mexican and US sentences and his 21 April 2020 release.
- milenio - Fact on El 85's 2012 arrest changed from '9 March 2012' to 'March 2012' in the facts list because English Wikipedia gives 3 March and Spanish sources give 9 March (day flagged, not asserted).
- milenio - status_2026 updated for Rosalinda's early release, Gerardo's July 2023 life sentence, and El 85's April 2026 guilty plea.
- cjng - San Sebastián del Oeste ambush redated from 7 April 2015 to 6 April 2015 (history and facts) - evidence: Wikipedia '2015 San Sebastián del Oeste ambush'; Borderland Beat.
- cjng - Martín Arzola 'El 53' fate completed: arrested 13 Jul 2011 in Tlajomulco de Zúñiga by the Federal Police, sentenced to 24 years in Dec 2015 but freed after an amparo, and shot dead 31 July 2019 at Plaza Galerías, Zapopan; profile recorded only the arrest - evidence: Infobae 2 Aug 2019; Excélsior; Borderland Beat.
- cjng - Hugo Gonzalo Mendoza Gaytán 'El Sapo' role corrected from 'precursor-chemical logistics chief' to overseer of weapons procurement and sicario training, with a $15M reward - evidence: DOJ press release, 5 Aug 2026.
- cjng - Julio Alberto Castillo Rodríguez 'El Chorro' role corrected to operations coordinator in the Colima region (son-in-law relationship retained), $15M reward - evidence: DOJ press release, 5 Aug 2026.
- cjng - Ricardo Ruiz Velasco's reward specified as $10 million (profile gave none) - evidence: DOJ press release, 5 Aug 2026.
- cjng - Antonio 'Tony Montana' Oseguera fate updated: he pleaded guilty in 2026 to trafficking 450+ kg of cocaine and 45+ kg of methamphetamine plus a firearms count, sentencing set for 13 November 2026 - evidence: ABC News/voz.us/Heraldo USA coverage of the plea.
- cjng - Carlos Enrique Sánchez's full name corrected to Carlos Enrique Sánchez Martínez and his death described accurately (gunshot to the head, body left on a bench in Jardín Hidalgo, Tlaquepaque, 18 Mar 2021) rather than 'tortured and killed' - evidence: Infobae 19 Mar 2021; Vallarta Independiente 23 Mar 2021.
- cjng - Audias Flores Silva capture detailed: El Mirador, Nayarit, ~20 km north of Puerto Vallarta, by more than 500 Navy troops, found in a drainage ditch, no shots fired; reward raised from $5M to $15M on 5 Aug 2026 - evidence: Al Jazeera 28 Apr 2026; Infobae 27 Apr 2026; DOJ 5 Aug 2026. The unverified '29 Apr preventive detention' claim was dropped.
- cjng - Los Rs leadership corrected and dated: leader is Ramón Ángel Álvarez Ayala 'R1' (not 'Ramón Álvarez Ayala'), captured 30 July 2026 as alleged intellectual author of the Manzo killing and the 31st arrest in the case; his brother Rafael Álvarez Ayala 'R2' identified as successor; operating area expanded to Uruapan, Apatzingán and Morelia - evidence: Proceso, La Jornada and El Financiero, 30 Jul 2026; TV Azteca.
- cjng - Misattributed statistic fixed: the '~30,000 homicides over a decade, 55% in Baja California' figure is not from the Mexico Peace Index 2026 but from Baja California state security authorities as reported by El Financiero on 28 May 2026; the MPI 2026 instead reports a 22.7% drop in the 2025 homicide rate and ranks Colima least peaceful - evidence: El Financiero 28 May 2026; Mexico Peace Index 2026 (IEP).
- cjng - NCTC recognition date softened from '8 July 2026' to 'July 2026' (no source gives a day); added the Wall Street Journal's 18 March 2026 report of the takeover - evidence: InSight Crime profile of 'El 03'; Wikipedia Juan Carlos Valencia González.
- cjng - El Mencho bounty increase to $15 million dated to 4 December 2024 - evidence: DEA Administrator Milgram statement, 4 Dec 2024; State Department release.
- cjng - Tapalpa raid geography and casualties corrected: about 100 km southwest of Guadalajara (not '90 km south'); four gunmen died at the scene and his two bodyguards died with him in transit; the raid was by Army and National Guard special forces - evidence: AP via PBS NewsHour and NBC News, 22-23 Feb 2026; Wikipedia '2026 Jalisco operation'.
- cjng - Car-bomb victim named (Army captain Leonel Cardoso Gómez, San Juan de los Lagos) - evidence: Wikipedia '2026 Mexico cartel unrest'.
- cjng - Helicopter shoot-down toll specified as nine (eight soldiers, one federal police officer) - evidence: Wikipedia '1 May 2015 Jalisco attacks'.
- cjng - Membership fact revised from '18,800-32,000 (c.2020)' to '18,800-33,100 (2020s tallies)'; the 15,000-20,000 2026 figure verified - evidence: Wikipedia CJNG infobox; Infobae 22 Aug 2026.
- cjng - '27 of 32 states' claim softened to 'a large majority of Mexico's 32 states' in the history and peak note, since no primary source confirms the exact count.

- cjng - El 03 biography completed with exact birth date (12 September 1984, Santa Ana, California), the 8 October 2020 D.C. indictment, and the pre-existing $5M reward from December 2021 - evidence: State Department Narcotics Rewards page; Wikipedia; InSight Crime.
- cjng - Bermúdez Requena dates added to the Barredora alliance note: arrested in Paraguay 12 September 2025, expelled to Mexico 17 September 2025 - evidence: Mexico News Daily; Wikipedia Hernán Bermúdez Requena.
- cjng - Abigael González Valencia's US status refined: pleaded not guilty and, by late 2025, reported to be negotiating a plea agreement and cooperating; the 12 Aug 2025 transfer confirmed as one of 26 fugitives - evidence: DOJ 12 Aug 2025 press release; Latin Times reporting.
- cjng - Rosalinda's 2018 arrest date '26 May 2018' removed (unverified); retained the fact that she was arrested in 2018 and released for lack of evidence, rearrested Nov 2021, sentenced Dec 2023, released 27 Feb 2025.
- cjng - Nueva Plaza faction note adjusted to say the remnant reorganized after El 85's September 2022 arrest rather than implying the group simply ended in 2022.
- cjng - Menchito's forfeiture stated as 'more than $6 billion' and sentencing judge named (Beryl Howell) - evidence: The Intercept 7 Mar 2025; CNN en Español.
- cjng - Erick Valencia's transfer described as one of 29 prisoners moved on 27 February 2025, and his plea specified as a single cocaine-importation conspiracy count before Chief Judge James Boasberg - evidence: JURIST 7 Apr 2026; Wikipedia.
- cjng - Rancho Izaguirre fact softened: 'well over a thousand items of clothing' replaced with 'large quantities of discarded clothing' since no source verifies the precise count.
- cjng - García Harfuch described as Mexico City security secretary rather than 'police chief' (his title was Secretario de Seguridad Ciudadana).
- cjng - Colima note reworded: instead of the unverifiable 'most violent state per capita', it now cites the Mexico Peace Index 2026 ranking Colima Mexico's least peaceful state - evidence: Mexico Peace Index 2026.

Kept as 'March 2012' in the facts and 'reported 9 Mar' in the milenio leader entry; needs a primary source (Sedena/PGR bulletin) to settle.
- FLAG: Erick Valencia's sentencing was scheduled for 31 July 2026 before Chief Judge James Boasberg, but no source found confirms whether the sentence was imposed or continued. Left as 'sentencing set for 31 Jul 2026'; should be re-checked.
- FLAG: Antonio 'Tony Montana' Oseguera's guilty-plea date is inconsistent across secondary outlets (reports appear in June and again in late July 2026). The plea itself and the 13 November 2026 sentencing date are corroborated; the plea date is deliberately left unstated.
- FLAG: The year Armando Valencia Cornelio was extradited to the US is disputed: Borderland Beat implies 2012, Milenio says 'twelve years after his 2003 arrest' (i.e. 2015). The fact of extradition, the 19-year-7-month US sentence and the 21 April 2020 release are solid; the extradition year is omitted.
- FLAG: CJNG founding year: this dataset uses 2010 (the post-Coronel split), but English Wikipedia's infobox dates the group to 31 August 2009 and some Mexican outlets trace 'Los Torcidos' to 2009. Kept at 2010 for consistency with the Milenio-split lineage.
- FLAG: Casualty counts at Tapalpa vary by source: AP (via PBS/NBC) reports four killed at the scene plus three wounded who died and three soldiers wounded, while Wikipedia's article on the operation reports 7-8 gunmen killed and three soldiers killed. The AP figures were used.
- FLAG: Ricardo Ruiz Velasco 'Doble R': the claim that a Mexicali man arrested in March 2026 with the same nickname was a different person could not be verified with the sources available; the exact arrest date (11 March 2026) was removed and the statement softened.
- FLAG: Julio Alberto Castillo Rodríguez's role: DOJ places him as operations coordinator in Colima, while Infobae describes him as a lieutenant to El 03 in the succession line. Both are reflected; his 'from 2026' start year is a dataset convention rather than a sourced fact, as he was active well before 2026.
- FLAG: The precise number of Mexican states with CJNG presence (the widely repeated '27 of 32') could not be traced to a primary federal assessment; the text now says 'a large majority' while the peak state list is retained unchanged.
- FLAG: The Los Cuinis sibling count of 18 is the standard federal figure but sources disagree internally (one report says 10 men and 9 women, which totals 19) and others give 12. Retained as 18.
- FLAG: Rosalinda González Valencia's stated separation from El Mencho in 2018 (in the original role field) could not be verified and was replaced with the sourced 1996 marriage date.
- FLAG: Whether Los Rs remains active under 'R2' after R1's 30 July 2026 arrest is based on a single TV Azteca report; the faction's 'to' value is left at 0 (active).
- FLAG: Web-search budget for this session was exhausted before the 'Arcos del Milenio' incident attribution (Zetas vs La Resistencia) and the CJNG's 2026 membership figure could be cross-checked against a second independent primary source; both rest on one corroborating source each.

- santa_rosa_lima - Los Marros faction start moved from 2024 to 2020 and the alternate name 'La Marriza' added - Infobae (29 Jan 2026) says Los Marros arose as a principal CSRL faction after El Marro's 2020 capture and uses both names for it; Milenio (27 Jan 2026) headlines 'La Marriza' as the CSRL group blamed for ~20 deaths in a week.
