- santa_rosa_lima - gulf alliance period changed from 2024-present to 2025-present - the earliest evidence cited is the Treasury designation of 17 Dec 2025 and the FGR's May 2026 court filing; nothing supports a 2024 start.
- santa_rosa_lima - 'held at CEFERESO 1 Altiplano' softened to 'federal maximum-security prison' in the status text - the Treasury release confirms he is imprisoned and directing operations from prison but does not name the facility, and no 2025-26 source confirming his current prison was located.
- carteles_unidos - origin coordinates changed from 19.183,-102.85 to 19.19206,-102.8454 - OpenStreetMap/Nominatim places the town of Tepalcatepec at 19.1920558, -102.8453959 (the original point was ~1 km off, closer to the municipal centroid).
- carteles_unidos - founding date kept at 2019 but the history now credits InSight Crime's dating of the alliance's consolidation to El Abuelo's May 2020 call for four municipalities' vigilante groups to unite - InSight Crime's current CU profile calls 2020 the year the latest incarnation formed, so the single-year founding is a judgement call now made explicit.
- carteles_unidos - removed El Abuelo's unverified birth date and birthplace (10 August 1970, Tepalcatepec) - no source located; the Tepalcatepec origin is retained descriptively as it is uncontested in reporting.
- carteles_unidos - 2018 arrest given exact dates and a corrected motive: detained 27 May 2018 over alleged CJNG links and freed 30 May 2018 for irregularities in the detention - La Jornada 28 May 2018, Milenio and Animal Político 30 May 2018. The profile's 'freed days later' was right but omitted that he was then described as an ally of 'El Mencho', which is material to the later CU-CJNG war.
- carteles_unidos - 30 August 2019 Tepalcatepec battle toll changed from a flat 'nine dead and eleven wounded' to 'nine dead and more than ten wounded, with authorities disputing the count' - Infobae 30 Aug 2019 ('9 muertos y más de 10 heridos'); Sin Embargo 3 Sep 2019 reports local and federal authorities disagreed even on the number of dead.
- carteles_unidos - the September 2020 'tank' location clarified as Bonifacio Moreno, known as El Aguaje, in Aguililla - InSight Crime.
- carteles_unidos - Los Viagras founding changed from 'December 2014' to '2013-2014' and the description of the founders corrected - es.wikipedia and InSight Crime date the group's emergence to 2013-2014 out of dissident Templars and Familia members; no source supports a December 2014 founding.
- carteles_unidos - Los Viagras leadership corrected to name Carlos Sierra Santana alongside Nicolás 'El Gordo' - InSight Crime's current profile states 'Carlos Sierra Santana, and his brother Nicolás, alias El Gordo, are the known leaders of Los Viagras'; the profile attributed sole leadership to Nicolás from 2014, and Nicolás is only documented as jefe from around March 2018 (changoonga, 19 Mar 2018).
- carteles_unidos - the 6 January 2015 Apatzingán clash recast from 'federal police killed ten of their followers' to '10 civilians killed and 21 wounded' after Los Viagras seized the town hall - es.wikipedia citing the official/press toll ('10 civiles muertos y 21 heridos'); the victims were civilians, not simply Viagras followers, which is the crux of the Apatzingán massacre controversy.
- carteles_unidos - Milenio's 'volatile alliance' between Los Viagras and the CJNG redated from 2024 to 8 October 2025, and the March 2026 rupture added - Milenio, 'Los Viagras y el CJNG: la volátil alianza que siembra terror en Michoacán', 8 Oct 2025; El Financiero and Animal, 9 Mar 2026, on Los Viagras betraying the CJNG.
- carteles_unidos - 'El Fruto' capture redated from 31 March 2021 to 30 March 2021 - El Universal, 'Capturan en Guatemala a exalcalde de Michoacán requerido por narcotráfico en EU', 30 Mar 2021.
- carteles_unidos - 'El Fruto' sentence dated to February 2023 and stated as 10 years - Infobae and Diario Libre, 10 Feb 2023; Noroeste, 11 Feb 2023. 'Extradited' changed to 'sent to the United States' because the mechanism (extradition from Guatemala vs expulsion) is not established in those reports.
- carteles_unidos - the 27 May 2025 Los Reyes landmine attribution corrected: most reporting blames the CJNG, not Cárteles Unidos, and the Guard unit was returning from a CJNG camp in Santa María del Oro, Jalisco - Mexico News Daily and La Jornada, 28 May 2025. The original text placed the attack in a CU-framing paragraph without noting the attribution.
- carteles_unidos - the same landmine: added that the toll was initially reported as six and later confirmed at eight FERI National Guard members, and the 'deadliest IED attack in Mexico's history' claim narrowed to 'deadliest IED strike on Mexican security forces on record' - Infobae 28 May 2025 (six) vs La Jornada and El Horizonte 28 May 2025 (eight); Mexico News Daily frames it as the deadliest since criminal IED use became known.
- carteles_unidos - removed the unverified IED-deactivation figures '736 in 2024 and 1,645 in 2025' and replaced them with a sourced cumulative figure: at least 18 military personnel killed by mines and explosives in Michoacán since 2024 - Infobae and Semanario ZETA, 1-2 Sep 2026.
- carteles_unidos - removed the unverified claim that 'in May 2024 mines, gunfire and drones killed four soldiers near Aguililla' - no source located for that incident or toll.
- carteles_unidos - the Aguililla siege redated from a precise '6 to 28 April 2021' window and 'about ten months' of CJNG control to 'through April 2021' and 'for months', and the road reopening from 10 February to 9-10 February 2022 - El Economista, 9 Feb 2022, 'Ejército ingresa a Aguililla... y repliega al CJNG y los cárteles unidos'. The '43 communities' figure could not be verified and was dropped.
- carteles_unidos - the April 2021 drone attack that wounded two police officers dated to 'around 20 April 2021' - Infobae, 21 and 25 Apr 2021, on Sedena's acknowledgement of CJNG drone-bomb attacks during the Aguililla dispute.
- carteles_unidos - Tepalcatepec mayor's name corrected from 'Martha Laura Mendoza' to 'Martha Mendoza Mendoza' and the circumstances added (shot dead with her husband outside her home) - El País, La Jornada, Infobae and El Financiero, 17-18 Jun 2025.
- carteles_unidos - Bernardo Bravo's death described as found tortured on 20 October 2025 and El Bótox named as the alleged intellectual author - Semanario ZETA, Infobae and Proceso, 20-21 Oct 2025 and Jan 2026.
- carteles_unidos - Los Blancos de Troya faction period closed at 2026 and the note rewritten - Infobae, 22 Jan 2026, 'Por qué acabó la alianza entre Los Blancos de Troya y El Abuelo Farías'; Infobae, 21 Oct 2025, on a CJNG-Viagras-Blancos de Troya extortion arrangement. Treating them as an uninterrupted CU faction through 2026 was wrong.
- carteles_unidos - the 16 July 2026 FTO action corrected to name both designees, Los Viagras and the Juárez Cartel, and dated to Federal Register publication - PBS NewsHour.
- carteles_unidos - García Harfuch's confirmation of Mexican warrants for El Abuelo and his son redated from 2 July to 3 July 2026 - La Jornada, El Heraldo de México and Reforma, 3 Jul 2026; the DC grand jury charges against the son and nephew are the 2 July item (El Financiero).
- carteles_unidos - added the nephew's alias 'Papo' and the three specific counts (meth conspiracy, material support to an FTO, firearms including machine guns and destructive devices) - El Financiero, 2 Jul 2026.
- carteles_unidos - the 18 April 2026 Tepalcatepec raid detainee's relationship to El Abuelo flagged as disputed in reporting (son per El Financiero and Quadratín, brother per La Jornada), rather than stated flatly as 'a relative' with no note - Infobae, El Financiero, Quadratín and La Jornada, 18-19 Apr 2026.
- carteles_unidos - added the arrest of 'El Repollo', operator for El Abuelo's son, on 29 May 2026 and his arraignment on 15 June 2026 - UnoTV 29 May 2026; Latinus and Infobae 15 Jun 2026.
- carteles_unidos - R5's death confirmed as Monday 31 August 2026 at Rodeo del Pinal, Tocumbo, announced 1 September - Sedena/García Harfuch confirmations reported by La Jornada, El Imparcial, Vanguardia and Reporte Indigo on 1 Sep 2026, all describing an operation 'el pasado lunes' (31 Aug; 1 Sep 2026 was a Tuesday). The cited Infobae piece of 2 Sep 2026 says 30 August, which is inconsistent with its own 'Monday' framing and with Sedena's account.
- carteles_unidos - added that a 15-year-old bodyguard nicknamed 'Chuchito Nini' was killed alongside R5 - Expansión Política 2 Sep 2026, El Financiero 2 Sep 2026, Diario del Yaqui 2 Sep 2026.
- carteles_unidos - added Jesús Ochoa Chávez 'La Venada' as the reported new head of the Cártel de Los Reyes - Infobae, El Imparcial and El Congresista, 2 Sep 2026; Contramuro, 3 Sep 2026. The profile stopped at R5's death and missed the succession.
- carteles_unidos - added that Poncho la Quiringua was moved to the Altiplano prison amid blockades and is accused of drone attacks on troops and recruiting ex-Colombian military - Mexican press coverage of the 1 Aug 2026 capture.
- carteles_unidos - Hipólito Mora's murder kept at 29 June 2023 in La Ruana but the unverified '25 gunmen firing more than 1,000 rounds' detail removed from the history and the facts list, and the later arrests of 'El Betillo' (Jan 2026) and 'El Conejillo' (detained in the United States, Jul 2026) added - La Jornada 2 Jan 2026; Latinus 24 Jul 2026; Infobae 18 Aug 2026.
- carteles_unidos - the August 2024 lime strike figures confirmed and made precise: begun 12 August 2024, more than 23,000 hectares and 34 packing plants idled for eight days, quotas of one peso per kilo from grower and packer doubled, lime at 60 pesos per kilo - Mexico News Daily. Blame is shared among Los Viagras, Cárteles Unidos and the CJNG, not CU alone.
- carteles_unidos - Plan Michoacán results confirmed and expanded with the firearms figure and the reporting window (10 Nov 2025 - 20 Jan 2026) - UnoTV; the 57-billion-peso budget, 12 axes, 9 Nov 2025 launch and Carlos Manzo's 1 Nov 2025 assassination all check out.
- carteles_unidos - El Bótox entry expanded with his birth date (26 Nov 1982), the seven outstanding arrest warrants and the 24 properties searched after his capture - Milenio (OFAC coverage); Infobae and La Jornada, 22-23 Jan 2026.
- carteles_unidos - La Sirena and El Gordo entries given the birth dates and specific charges in the OFAC/Milenio reporting (Cisneros Flores b. 21 Feb 1986, plaza chief in La Ruana since 2018; Sierra Santana b. 10 Sep 1977) - Milenio.
- carteles_unidos - added the 2010 origin of the 'Cárteles Unidos' label under Ignacio Coronel Villarreal to the history's opening rather than as an aside, and noted the member groups of that earlier alliance - InSight Crime; en.wikipedia's La Resistencia entry.
- carteles_unidos - CJNG rivalry note expanded to record that the rivalry is complicated by Los Viagras' 2024-2025 alliance with the CJNG and its 2026 rupture - Milenio 8 Oct 2025 and 19 May 2026; El Financiero 9 Mar 2026.
- carteles_unidos - added 'recruitment of former Colombian soldiers as explosives specialists' to the activities list - reported by Mexican authorities in the Poncho la Quiringua and Cártel de Los Reyes coverage, Aug 2026.
- carteles_unidos - U.S. indictment scope in the activities list expanded to note alleged distribution in the U.S., Europe and Australia - El Financiero, 2 Jul 2026.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - Founding year 2014: es.wikipedia and most Mexican reporting date the CSRL's consolidation to 2014, but InSight Crime's analysis gives no founding year and some analysts date the group only to its ~2017 break with the CJNG. The 2014 date is the best-supported but is not from a first-rank source.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - El Marro's biography (exact birth date, birthplace, the 2010 move from cargo theft into huachicol) rests on Mexican press profiles I could not access in this pass. Treat the 1980 birth year as the only reliable element.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - 'Pemex counted 1,696 illegal taps in Guanajuato in 2017' could not be re-verified against a Pemex or SENER document. The figure is widely repeated in Mexican reporting but should be checked against Pemex's tomas clandestinas series before publication.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - The INEGI series for 2021-2024 (4,333 / 4,329 / 3,862 / 4,035) was verified only for 2024 (4,035, via Periódico AM). The three earlier years are plausible and internally consistent but unconfirmed here.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - The SESNSP 2025 total of 2,539 victims (and 3,151 for 2024) could not be confirmed; Animal Político's 22 Jan 2026 fact-check puts Guanajuato's 2025 reduction at about 20 percent, consistent with those numbers, but the absolute figures are unsourced. I removed them; restore only against the SESNSP monthly report.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - 'El Kin / El Ranchero' as a distinct 2023 arrest is thinly sourced; es.wikipedia mentions an 'El Ranchero' among post-2020 cell chiefs but no arrest report was located. Consider dropping the leader entry if no source surfaces.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - Mario Eleazar Lara Belman's 'from' year is set to 2024, but the only documentation found dates his identification as Los Marros' leader to January 2026 (Infobae, El Financiero, N+). The 2024 start is an inference from the cell's continuity, not a sourced fact.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - Which prison holds El Marro in 2026 is unconfirmed. Treasury (17 Dec 2025) says only that he directs the CSRL from prison. Do not assert CEFERESO 1 Altiplano without a 2025-26 source.
- FLAG: santa_rosa_lima - Whether the CSRL originated as a CJNG cell that broke away around 2017, or as an independent village network, remains genuinely contested in the literature; the profile presents both readings and should keep doing so.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - Founding year: I kept 2019, but InSight Crime's current profile effectively dates the latest CU incarnation to 2020 (El Abuelo's May 2020 unity call). If the atlas needs a single year, 2019 vs 2020 is a defensible editorial choice, not a settled fact.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - Carlos Sierra Santana's status is unresolved. InSight Crime lists him as a current co-leader of Los Viagras; es.wikipedia instead reports a Juan Carlos Sierra Santana 'La Sopa'/'El Greñas' killed 6 March 2017 in Aguililla, plus brothers Daniel 'El Chaco' (variously reported arrested Aug 2020 and killed by a mine in Tumbiscatío in Jan 2024) and Gabino 'El Ingeniero'. Whether Carlos is alive and leading is unverified.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - The 3 December 2020 CJNG raids on Los Reyes, Cotija, Tocumbo and Tingüindín (seven dead) and the November 2020 trench-digging around Tepalcatepec and Coalcomán are carried over from the original profile and were not re-verified in this pass.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - The 16 August 2023 lime work stoppage was not re-verified; only the August 2024 strike was confirmed.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - Edgar Orozco Cabadas 'El Kamoni' is confirmed as a $3 million reward target and fugitive, but his described role as 'chief of sicarios under El Abuelo' rests on Infobae's summary of the U.S. announcement rather than on the indictment itself.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - The 27 May 2025 landmine is attributed by most reporting to the CJNG, yet the profile's peak/faction framing still places it in Cártel de Los Reyes territory. If the atlas maps attacks to perpetrators, this one should be attributed to the CJNG, not to Cárteles Unidos.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - Los Blancos de Troya's alignment is unstable across sources: reported inside CU, then in a triple arrangement with the CJNG and Los Viagras (Oct 2025), then broken with El Abuelo (Jan 2026). The faction end-year of 2026 is a judgement call.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - Los Viagras' status as a CU faction after its FTO designation (16 Jul 2026) is ambiguous; U.S. reporting treats it as a separate organisation operating alongside CU, and Mexican reporting since 2023 describes Viagras-versus-CU friction. The atlas may want a separate org id for Los Viagras rather than a faction entry.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - 'La Venada' (Jesús Ochoa Chávez) as the new Cártel de Los Reyes leader comes from reporting only days old as of 8 Sep 2026 and is described as presumed, not confirmed by authorities.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - The DOJ charges announced 14 August 2025 are described in secondary reporting as District of Columbia charges against five leaders; I could not open the DOJ or State Department releases (both returned 403), so the exact court, count structure and unsealing date should be checked against the primary documents.
- FLAG: carteles_unidos - The peak window 2020-2022 and its territorial list are analytic judgements carried over from the original profile and were not independently sourced.

- union_tepito - Founding meeting dated to May 2010 rather than a generic '2010 meeting' - Wikipedia 'Unión Tepito' (founded 2009 by Édgar Valdez Villarreal, formalized in a May 2010 meeting in Tepito).

- union_tepito - '54 arrests ... (4 August)' corrected to reported 5 August 2026, and the war's death toll of 24 added - Milenio topic page, headline dated 05.08.2026: '54 detenidos en guerra entre Unión Tepito y Anti Unión que deja 24 muertos'.
- union_tepito - The 4 September 2026 Peralvillo killing reattributed: removed the 'internal dispute' framing, since El Universal reports motorcycle gunmen killed a member who had been sought since March, matching the Anti-Unión pattern - El Universal tag 'la-union-tepito', 4 September 2026.
- union_tepito - 'Los Gregory' faction: leader identified as 'El Gregory', arrested 15 June 2026 in Coyoacán as a priority target linked to homicides and extortion, and the faction's area extended from Miguel Hidalgo to include Coyoacán - El Universal tag 'la-union-tepito', 15 June 2026.

- union_tepito - El Huguito timeline made precise: captured 27 July 2024 in Cancún, moved to Mexico City 28 July, linked to trial 6 August 2024; added the 3 December 2025 hearing postponed after a shooting at Plaza Garibaldi - Milenio topic page 'el-huguito'.

- FLAG: union_tepito - The January 2026 Tren de Aragua link is described by InSight Crime as an alleged agreement over control of sexual exploitation; it is retained as an ally entry but is an allegation, not an established relationship.
- FLAG: anti_union - Conflicting arrest dates for El Tortas: 28 May 2019 (El Financiero, February 2026; InSight Crime says only 'May 2019') versus 18 May 2019 (Milenio's June 2020 sentencing report). Retained 28 May with the discrepancy stated in the fate field and history.
- FLAG: anti_union - The claim that El Betito had one of El Tortas's brothers killed and another kidnapped appears in press accounts but was not confirmed by any source reached this session.
- FLAG: anti_union - The CJNG envoy 'Luis Eusebio Duque Reyes, El Duque' brokering the 2019 pact, and the October 2019 transfer of 140 high-risk inmates to Chiapas and Oaxaca, could not be verified this session and are retained on the strength of the original profile.
- FLAG: anti_union - The January 2026 charging of two members over a Venustiano Carranza murder, and the SSC arrests of four members on 4 July 2026 (three minors) and four more on 11 July 2026, could not be independently verified this session.
- FLAG: anti_union - El Tortas's release date from the Altiplano ('about 2027') is an inference from an eight-year sentence dated to a May 2019 arrest; time-served credits and the exact sentencing date could shift it.
- FLAG: tlahuac - Conflicting detention counts for the 20 July 2017 narcobloqueos: 23 (Wikipedia, and the figure in most official recaps) versus 16 (Infobae, 2022). Both are now shown.
- FLAG: tlahuac - Conflicting mototaxi figures: Milenio documents about 1,000 units directly organized into twelve coordinations, while Infobae reports SEMAR identified 10,000 of the city's ~30,000 registered mototaxis as working for the cartel. Both are recorded; they measure different things.
- FLAG: tlahuac - The relationship of Diana Karen Pérez Ramírez is given as daughter by Milenio's family profile but as 'cuñada' (sister-in-law) in one El Financiero passage. Retained as daughter, per the more detailed Milenio genealogy.
- FLAG: tlahuac - The status of María de los Ángeles Ramírez Arvizu as widow versus ex-partner of El Ojos varies by outlet (El Financiero and Grupo Animal say 'viuda'; Milenio says ex-wife). Both renderings are noted in the leader record.
- FLAG: tlahuac - 'Plaza boss César Medrano jailed in June 2026' could not be verified in any source reached this session and has been removed from the history and status_2026 pending confirmation.
- FLAG: tlahuac - The 2017-2018 arrests of sons Kevin (at 16, April 2018) and Alexis (at 14, January 2020), Kevin's 17 May 2024 recapture, the 'El Chato Venegas' arrest with 13 others in October 2020, and Bertha Alicia Flores's June 2022 arrest are retained from the original profile; only the April 2018 and January 2020 dates were independently corroborated by Milenio's family profile.
- FLAG: tlahuac - The 29 July 2026 shootings at La Turba and Delfín in Tláhuac and the August 2026 prosecutors' listing of Tláhuac, La Unión and Los Rodolfos as despojo actors could not be independently verified this session.
- FLAG: tlahuac - The founding year of 2012 is given by Expansión and matches the prosecutorial dating, but El Ojos's group grew continuously out of 'La Mano con Ojos' from about 2010-2011, so the date marks consolidation rather than a clean origin.
- FLAG: ALL - The WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted before verification began, so every check above was performed by direct WebFetch of the profiles' own cited URLs plus a small number of publisher topic pages. Several 2026 claims therefore rest on a single outlet, and La Jornada, Proceso and two Wikipedia URLs returned HTTP 403/404. A second pass with search access is advisable before publication.

- autodefensas - added the December 2014 Los Viagras occupation of Apatzingán's municipal palace and the 6 January 2015 federal police eviction that left 10 civilians dead and 21 wounded, a major omission in the 2014-15 narrative - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Viagras
- autodefensas - Cárteles Unidos FTO designation date changed from the specific '20 February 2025' to 'February 2025' since sources confirm only the month for this group within the six-cartel action - es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cárteles_Unidos, es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_José_Farías_Álvarez
- autodefensas - Los Viagras FTO designation date changed from the specific '16 July 2026' to 'July 2026'; the source confirms only that the designation was in place as of July 2026 - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Viagras
- autodefensas - the seven leaders sanctioned on 14 August 2025 are now named: Farías Álvarez 'El Abuelo', Barragán Chávez 'Wicho', Fernández Magallón 'Poncho', Orozco Cabadas 'El Kamoni' (Cárteles Unidos) and Sierra Santana 'El Gordo', Cisneros Flores 'La Sirena', Sepúlveda Arellano 'El Botox' (Los Viagras) - home.treasury.gov/news/press-releases/sb0224
- autodefensas - the 2 July 2026 indictment detailed: District of Columbia grand jury, defendants aged 31 and 37, three counts (meth manufacture/distribution for import, material support to an FTO, machine guns and destructive devices), maximum life - justice.gov press release 2026-07-02
- autodefensas - Nicolás Sierra Santana's alias expanded to 'El Gordo / El Coruco'; Treasury uses El Gordo, Spanish Wikipedia uses El Coruco - home.treasury.gov/news/press-releases/sb0224 and es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cárteles_Unidos
- autodefensas - El Abuelo's record expanded with his birth date (10 August 1970), his 2009 detention in Buenavista Tomatlán, and the judicial ground for his 2018 release (procedural irregularities) - es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_José_Farías_Álvarez
- autodefensas - Nestora Salgado's Senate term made explicit (1 September 2018 - 31 August 2024, out of office since) and the UN arbitrary-detention finding added - es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestora_Salgado
- autodefensas - Texcaltitlán crops corrected from generic 'crops' to bean (haba) and oat crops, and the killed gang leader 'El Payaso' added; the 10 gunmen / 4 villagers split was confirmed against the article body - infobae.com 2023-12-09
- autodefensas - Carlos Manzo aftermath expanded and dated: shot at the Festival de las Velas on 1 November 2025, Morelia march 2 November, nationwide protests in over 50 cities 15 November, widow Grecia Quiroz sworn in 5 November, Plan Michoacán para la Paz y la Justicia, alleged mastermind Ramón Álvarez Ayala 'R1' arrested by July 2026 - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Manzo
- autodefensas - added a 'government' rivals entry covering Antúnez (13 January 2014), the Mireles arrest, the 6 January 2015 Apatzingán eviction and the 18 April 2026 Tepalcatepec raid, which the profile described in the history but omitted from rivals - multiple sources above
- autodefensas - activities expanded with synthetic-opioid trafficking, extortion of cattle ranchers and whole towns, IED use and foreign-mercenary recruitment, all attributed to Cárteles Unidos and Los Viagras - home.treasury.gov/news/press-releases/sb0224
- autodefensas - Alfredo Castillo's leader entry now records his 15 January 2014 appointment date, which appeared only in the history - unchanged sourcing
- autodefensas - el_machete faction note now includes the 5 July 2021 killing of Simón Pedro Pérez López that triggered its formation - internal consistency with the history
- autodefensas - templarios rivals note now names the three decapitation dates already in the history (Nazario Moreno 9 March 2014, Enrique Plancarte 31 March 2014, La Tuta 27 February 2015) - internal consistency
- FLAG: Web search quota for this session was exhausted before verification began (200/200 calls used), so all checking was done with direct page fetches. Several 2025-2026 claims could not be reached because search engines returned CAPTCHAs (DuckDuckGo) or 403s (Brave, state.gov, Federal Register, CourtListener HTML, borderreport), and Reuters, AP and azcentral are not fetchable from this environment. The items below remain unconfirmed.
- FLAG: caborca - VICTIM NAME: the 18 October 2024 killing is attributed by the cited El Financiero piece to 'Nicholas Douglas', an American ex-Marine. Other reporting I could not reach appears to identify the victim as Nicholas Quets of Tucson (possibly the same man, Nicholas Douglas Quets). English Wikipedia has no article under either name. The name in the profile has been left as the cited source has it, but it needs a second source before publication.
- FLAG: caborca - The 8 March 2027 trial date, the anonymous-jury request, the 1 October 2026 status conference and the extension of special confinement measures could not be independently verified; only the fact of plea negotiations (19 March 2026) is supported by the cited headline. Treat the trial and hearing dates as provisional.
- FLAG: caborca - Whether Caro Quintero has actually entered a plea between March and September 2026 could not be checked. The profile now says no plea is on the public record as of September 2026; if a plea was entered in mid-2026 this is the single most likely error in the object.
- FLAG: caborca - FOUNDING YEAR is disputed: InSight Crime says 2017 (retained), Spanish Wikipedia says 2015, English Wikipedia says 2019 and names Miguel Caro Quintero as a co-founder. 2017 was kept as the best-sourced figure, but the range should be footnoted in the atlas.
- FLAG: caborca - Spanish Wikipedia also lists the Tijuana Cartel, CJNG and a 'Cártel Independiente de Sonora' among Caborca's allies 'until 2024'. These were not added because no better source corroborates them, and a CJNG alliance would sit oddly with the group's other alignments.
- FLAG: caborca - Unverified 2026 operational claims retained from the original profile: the 21 January 2026 dismantling of a Caborca cell in Sonora, the 12 June 2026 arrest of three alleged members in the State of Mexico, and the continued arrests of Los Salazar cell leaders in June-July 2026.
- FLAG: caborca - Unverified 2025 claims retained: the fall of the Caborca Deltas boss 'Keka' on 23-24 February 2025 and the Sonora prosecutor's 24 May 2025 declaration that Los Deltas were dismantled.
- FLAG: caborca - Sajid Emilio Quintero Navidad's 2017 detention is asserted in the original profile but I found no source for it; if he was detained in 2017 the faction leadership dates for La Plaza (from 2017 to present) are internally inconsistent and should be revisited.
- FLAG: caborca - The status of Jesús Darío Murrieta Navarro 'La Cara de Cochi' and the 21 July 2020 killing of his predecessor 'El Barbas' rest on 2021 reporting only.
- FLAG: caborca - The claim that the DEA's 20-million-dollar reward was 'the largest ever posted for a Mexican trafficker at the time' is plausible but unverified.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The Mora prosecution timeline for 2026 is entirely unverified: the 3 January 2026 arrest, the 24 July 2026 capture of 'El Conejillo' in California and the 11 August 2026 extradition request, as is the killing of Mora's nephew in La Ruana on 1 November 2025.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The 18 April 2026 joint army-navy raid on Tepalcatepec, including the seizure of a son of El Abuelo, is unverified; note the tension with the 2 July 2026 DOJ indictment, which charges the son Juan José Farías Mendoza as a defendant without indicating he is in custody.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The July 2026 killing of El Machete commander Fernando Ruiz in Chenalhó and the group's subsequent re-arming are unverified, as is the February 2025 entry of federal forces into Pantelhó.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The France 24 profile of Guajes de Ayala in March 2026, the group's 2020 founding and its size of about 50 men are unverified.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The exact date of the 10-million-dollar US reward for El Abuelo (July 2025) is unverified; only the amount is confirmed. Likewise the exact FTO designation dates of 20 February 2025 (Cárteles Unidos) and 16 July 2026 (Los Viagras) have been reduced to month-level precision.
- FLAG: autodefensas - CÁRTELES UNIDOS FOUNDING is disputed: the profile dates the ex-militia alliance to 2019 (retained), but Spanish Wikipedia traces Cárteles Unidos to 2010 as 'La Resistencia', an anti-Zetas front founded by Ramiro Pozos González, Elpidio Mojarro Ramírez and Víctor Manuel Torres García. The atlas should distinguish the name's lineage from the 2019 militia-derived alliance.
- FLAG: autodefensas - EL ABUELO'S BIOGRAPHY is contested: Spanish Wikipedia places him in the Milenio Cartel in the 2000s and inside the CJNG from 2010 to 2019, which conflicts with the framing of him as a militia founder who only later turned to organized crime. The profile keeps the militia-founder framing with his 2009 arrest noted, but the CJNG-membership account should be weighed.
- FLAG: autodefensas - TEXCALTITLÁN CASUALTY SPLIT: the cited Infobae headline says eleven of the fourteen dead were La Familia Michoacana, while the article body says ten criminals and four residents. The 10/4 split from the body was retained; the discrepancy is unresolved.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The claim that 8 Texcapilla residents were still missing a year after 8 December 2023 is unverified.
- FLAG: autodefensas - Estanislao Beltrán's current status ('alive, out of public office, still making occasional statements (2025)') and Luis Antonio Torres 'El Americano's post-2015 whereabouts are both unverified.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The Fuerza Rural's dissolution year is given as 2016 in the factions array while the history says it was wound down after Castillo left in January 2015; no source was found fixing a formal end date, so the two should be reconciled.
- FLAG: autodefensas - The killing of UPOEG co-founder Juan Carlos González two days before Bruno Plácido (i.e. 15 October 2023) is unverified, as is Plácido's August 2022 arrest and same-day release.
- FLAG: autodefensas - CRAC-PC's figures (about 2,500 police, 210 communities, 29 municipalities) and the 2018 weakening of Guerrero's Law 701 recognition were carried over from the original profile without independent confirmation.
## Figures
- juan_ramon_matta_ballesteros - MAJOR: profile said he was 'arrested in the United States in 1985' and 'escaped a US prison in 1986'. Two separate events were conflated. He was convicted in 1970 of entering the US on a false passport and escaped the federal prison camp at Eglin, Florida, in 1971; the March 1986 escape was from Colombian custody, allegedly by bribery. Corrected in bio and notable. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Ramón_Matta-Ballesteros (1970 conviction, 'he escaped from the camp the following year'; Colombia escape March 1986).
- juan_ramon_matta_ballesteros - MAJOR: profile presented his Camarena kidnapping conviction as standing. Judge John Kronstadt vacated the kidnapping convictions in 2017 and prosecutors dropped those charges in December 2018; only his life sentence for drug smuggling remained until his death. Corrected in bio and notable. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Ramón_Matta-Ballesteros.
- juan_ramon_matta_ballesteros - Removed the claim that he 'spent years at ADX Florence'. Article text places him at USP Canaan, Pennsylvania, then the US Medical Center for Federal Prisoners, Springfield, Missouri, where he died. status_2026 now names Springfield. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Ramón_Matta-Ballesteros.
- juan_ramon_matta_ballesteros - Refined the 1988 rendition: he was flown to the Dominican Republic and US marshals took custody in Puerto Rico, not in the Dominican Republic. Also removed the unsupported exact date '5 April 1988' in favour of 'April 1988'. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Ramón_Matta-Ballesteros.
- ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - House arrest date corrected from 'November 2016' to July 2016. He left Puente Grande prison for house arrest in July 2016, with about nine years of his 40-year sentence remaining. Evidence: Excélsior, 'Don Neto va a casa; deja penal de Puente Grande', 28 July 2016, cited in en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Fonseca_Carrillo.
- ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - Softened the exact release date. Profile asserted 9 April 2025; the Wikipedia infobox gives 10 April 2025. status_2026 and bio now say 'April 2025' pending a primary source. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Fonseca_Carrillo (footnote: 'Released on April 10, 2025').
- ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - Age in status_2026 corrected from 94 to 96. Born 1 August 1930, he turned 96 in August 2026, before today's date of 8 September 2026. The bio correctly notes he was 94 at release in April 2025.
- ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - Removed the unverifiable claim that he 'filed suit in late 2025 to recover seized assets'; no source found. Also softened arrest wording: the army surrounded his Puerto Vallarta villa and he surrendered on 7 April 1985. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Fonseca_Carrillo.
- ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - Birthplace narrowed to 'Badiraguato, Sinaloa' and added that the DEA long listed his birth year as 1942; the profile's specific 'Santiago de los Caballeros' is not supported by the source consulted. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Fonseca_Carrillo.
- miguel_angel_felix_gallardo - Sentence was incomplete. He received 40 years originally and a further 37-year term at a 2017 retrial. Added to bio, notable and role context. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Ángel_Félix_Gallardo, citing Business Insider, 30 August 2017.
- miguel_angel_felix_gallardo - Corrected 'held in maximum-security prisons ever since'. He was moved from Altiplano to a medium-security federal prison in Jalisco in December 2014 on health grounds. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Ángel_Félix_Gallardo (Proceso citation).
- miguel_angel_felix_gallardo - status_2026 rewritten. Profile asserted the 2022 house-arrest order was 'repeatedly suspended and never executed'. Reuters (12 September 2022) reports the grant and a planned 13 September 2022 transfer; no source consulted confirms the suspension. Now stated as granted, reportedly suspended, outcome not independently confirmed. Evidence: reuters.com/world/americas/mexican-drug-lord-who-was-jailed-killing-us-agent-granted-house-arrest-2022-09-12/.
- miguel_angel_felix_gallardo - Birthplace narrowed to Culiacán, Sinaloa; the profile's 'Bellavista' neighbourhood is not in the source consulted. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Ángel_Félix_Gallardo.
- enedina_arellano_felix - MAJOR: Luis Fernando Sánchez Arellano is her NEPHEW, the son of her sister Alicia María Arellano Félix, not her son as the profile stated twice. This is a widely repeated error. Corrected in bio and notable. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Fernando_Sánchez_Arellano ('He is the son of Alicia María Arellano Félix and not Enedina Arellano Félix as is often reported').
- enedina_arellano_felix - Added his exact arrest date and place: 23 June 2014 at a restaurant in the Mesa de Otay borough of Tijuana. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Fernando_Sánchez_Arellano.
- benjamin_arellano_felix - MAJOR: prison corrected from 'USP Lee, Virginia' to USP Coleman I, Florida (BOP register 00678-748), with release scheduled 28 April 2032. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamín_Arellano_Félix (as of September 2025).
- benjamin_arellano_felix - Added precise dates: transferred to the US 29 April 2011; pleaded guilty 4 January 2012; sentenced 2 April 2012. Removed the unverified January 2023 compassionate-release denial and the 'ruthless and inhumane' quotation. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamín_Arellano_Félix.
- jesus_gutierrez_rebollo - Arrest date corrected from 18 February 1997 to 6 February 1997; 18 February is when the arrest was made public, days before the US certification vote. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesús_Gutiérrez_Rebollo.
- jesus_gutierrez_rebollo - Sentence detail sharpened: the first term was 31 years, 10 months and 15 days for misuse of army-restricted weapons; the 2007 federal conviction added 40 years plus a fine of 24,716,829 pesos. Added his age at death (79). Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesús_Gutiérrez_Rebollo.
- carlos_salinas_de_gortari - Raúl Salinas's conviction year corrected from 1996 to 1999. He was arrested February 1995, convicted in 1999 (sentence later reduced to 27.5 years), and the conviction was overturned in June 2005, when he was released. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raúl_Salinas_de_Gortari.
- carlos_salinas_de_gortari - Softened the Swiss money figure from 'more than 100 million dollars' to the sourced 'more than 90 million dollars' moved abroad 1992-1994 via Citibank (a GAO finding), with $84 million frozen in Geneva. Also added Salinas's age (78). Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raúl_Salinas_de_Gortari.
- hector_palma_salazar - Added exact birth date 29 April 1960 and corrected the account of the 1989 murders: Rafael Enrique Clavel decapitated his wife and sent the head, while the two children, Jesús and Nataly, were taken to Venezuela and thrown from a bridge near San Cristóbal. The profile implied all three were killed together. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Héctor_Luis_Palma_Salazar.
- hector_palma_salazar - status_2026 corrected. The profile said prosecutors 'blocked his release' after the 2023 acquittal; the operative reason he remains at Altiplano is a pending prosecution for the 1995 murder of two Nayarit police officers. Also added that he served his US sentence at Atwater, California. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Héctor_Luis_Palma_Salazar.
- rafael_aguilar_guajardo - Role corrected. Profile called him the 'first boss' and 'first recognised head' of the Juárez Cartel, but he took over only after Pablo Acosta Villarreal's death in April 1987 and led it 1987-1993. Reworded to co-founder and leader from 1987. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Aguilar_Guajardo.
- rafael_aguilar_guajardo - Added birthplace (Ciudad Juárez, Chihuahua), the name of the killing site (outside the Gypsys restaurant) and the detail that he was returning from a submarine excursion; corrected 'days after' to 'two days after' his threat to expose government contacts. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Aguilar_Guajardo.
- ignacio_coronel_villarreal - Corrected the 2010 raid: Coronel killed one soldier and wounded another before being shot dead; the profile said only that he wounded a soldier. Added the $5 million US reward and his 1980s start in Nayarit. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacio_Coronel_Villarreal.
- miguel_caro_quintero - Birthplace corrected from 'La Noria, Badiraguato, Sinaloa' to Caborca, Sonora, with the family's Sinaloan origin retained as context. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Caro_Quintero.
- miguel_caro_quintero - Added precise dates the profile omitted: extradited to the US on 25 February 2009, sentenced in Denver on 4 February 2010 to 17 years, deported through the El Chaparral crossing at Tijuana on 20 July 2019. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Caro_Quintero.
- miguel_caro_quintero - Removed the claim that 'Mexican reporting places him back in Sonora'; the source consulted states Mexican authorities issued no statement and his whereabouts are undisclosed. Capture month left as December 2001 without the unsupported day. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Caro_Quintero.
- amezcua_contreras_brothers - Corrected which brother went to Almoloya. Luis Ignacio received the 49-year sentence and was held at Almoloya; the profile attributed the conviction to José de Jesús. The May 2002 blocked extradition did concern José de Jesús. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colima_Cartel.
- amezcua_contreras_brothers - Corrected 'the organization did not survive their imprisonment'. By 2008 it had no single leader and survived as precursor-diversion cells absorbed into larger structures in the Sinaloa orbit. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colima_Cartel.
- juan_garcia_abrego - Resolved an internal contradiction. The bio said he was born in Matamoros yet was 'deemed a US citizen by birth'. US authorities placed his birth in La Paloma, Texas, which is the basis of the citizenship finding that allowed his same-day removal to Houston; both claims are now stated. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_García_Ábrego.
- juan_garcia_abrego - Added the $350 million forfeiture ordered alongside the eleven consecutive life terms, and his current age (81). Removed the unsupported 'October 1996' conviction month in favour of 1996. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_García_Ábrego.
- joaquin_guzman_loera - Added exact dates the profile gave only by month: Puente Grande escape 19 January 2001, Mazatlán recapture 22 February 2014, Altiplano escape 11 July 2015. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquín_'El_Chapo'_Guzmán.
- joaquin_guzman_loera - Qualified the laundry-cart story. The official account had him hidden in a laundry cart, but investigations established that prison guard Francisco 'El Chito' Camberos Rivera opened his electronically operated cell door. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquín_'El_Chapo'_Guzmán.
- joaquin_guzman_loera - Corrected the Guatemala arrest detail: he was arrested by the Guatemalan Army at a hotel near Tapachula on 9 June 1993. Fixed the BOP register number formatting to 89914-053, and removed the unverified May 2026 transfer-denial assertion from the bio (retained as an unconfirmed note in status_2026). Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquín_'El_Chapo'_Guzmán.
- ismael_zambada_garcia - status_2026 and notable softened. The 20 July 2026 life sentence and the 25 August 2025 guilty plea are confirmed, but the '$15 billion forfeiture', the 5 August 2025 death-penalty decision, his request for a medical facility and his statement renouncing violence are not supported by any source reachable in this session; they are now flagged as unconfirmed rather than asserted. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismael_Zambada_García.
- ismael_zambada_garcia - Corrected 'private airstrip' to 'private airfield near El Paso, Texas' per the source wording. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismael_Zambada_García.
- juan_jose_esparragoza_moreno - Corrected the reported death location. The profile placed it 'near Puente Grande, Jalisco'; sources report a heart attack roughly fifteen days after a car accident, with accounts differing between Mexico City and Guadalajara, and his remains reportedly cremated and sent to Culiacán. Exact reported date 7 June 2014 added. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_José_Esparragoza_Moreno.
- juan_jose_esparragoza_moreno - Added that US databases list an alternative birth date of 2 March 1949, and attributed the $5 million reward to the State Department Narcotics Rewards Program rather than the DEA. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_José_Esparragoza_Moreno.
- vicente_carrillo_fuentes - Added the exact Mexican sentencing date, 14 September 2021, for the 28-year term. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Carrillo_Fuentes.
- vicente_carrillo_fuentes - status_2026 corrected. He was arraigned in Brooklyn on 28 February 2025 but the charges he faces were brought in the Western District of Texas; the profile's 'plea negotiations under way, with a hearing scheduled for October 2026' is unsupported and is now marked unconfirmed. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Carrillo_Fuentes.
- vicente_carrillo_fuentes - Birthplace narrowed to Navolato, Sinaloa; the profile's 'Guamuchilito' is not in the source consulted (it is sourced for his brother Amado). Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Carrillo_Fuentes.
- rafael_caro_quintero - Added the location of Rancho Búfalo (Allende, Chihuahua), the city of his 1985 Costa Rica arrest (Alajuela), the municipality of his 2022 recapture (Choix), and that the 2013 release came after 28 years served. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Caro_Quintero.
- rafael_caro_quintero - Corrected the helicopter crash figure: 14 of the 15 personnel aboard were killed, not '14 personnel' out of an unstated total. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Caro_Quintero.
- rafael_caro_quintero - status_2026 softened. The 27 February 2025 transfer and the not-guilty plea at his 28 February 2025 arraignment are confirmed; the 5 August 2025 death-penalty decision, March 2026 plea talks and 2027 trial date could not be verified and are now marked unconfirmed. Removed 'teenage' before Sara Cosío as unsupported. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Caro_Quintero.
- enrique_camarena - Added the location of Rancho Búfalo (Allende, Chihuahua) and its ~1,000-hectare size, plus the autopsy finding that his skull was perforated with a drill and that stimulants were injected to keep him conscious during torture. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Camarena_(DEA_agent) and the Camarena autopsy report dated 7 March 1985.
- enrique_camarena - Softened 'abducted outside the US consulate' to 'in broad daylight near the US consulate'; the precise abduction point is described variously across sources. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Camarena_(DEA_agent).
- amado_carrillo_fuentes - Death date qualified. The profile asserted 4 July 1997; the Wikipedia article gives 5 July 1997. Both are now noted. Also softened the scandal wording from 'was arrested for taking his money' to 'was revealed to have taken his money', since Gutiérrez Rebollo was detained on 6 February and exposed on 18 February 1997. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amado_Carrillo_Fuentes; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesús_Gutiérrez_Rebollo.
- amado_carrillo_fuentes - Added the date his surgeons' bodies were found, 7 November 1997, in steel drums, and dropped the unsupported 'approaching' before the $25 billion estimate (sources state 'around $25 billion'). Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amado_Carrillo_Fuentes.
- ramon_arellano_felix - Removed the unverified '$2 million reward' attached to his FBI Ten Most Wanted listing; no source consulted supports that figure. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramón_Arellano_Félix.
- ramon_arellano_felix - Sharpened the Mazatlán shootout: Ramón shot officer Ángel Antonio Arias Torres twice in the chest and the dying officer killed him with a single shot to the head. Added Ismael Zambada's later testimony that Guzmán arranged the traffic stop. Also changed 'Present at' the airport shooting to 'Commanded the gunmen at', matching the bio. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramón_Arellano_Félix.
- juan_jesus_posadas_ocampo - Added that three Logan Heights gang members pleaded guilty in the US to weapons charges and received 18-22 year terms, and that a deputy attorney general documented serious irregularities including destroyed evidence. This sharpens the accurate but bare claim that no one was convicted of the murder. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Jesús_Posadas_Ocampo.
- juan_jesus_posadas_ocampo - Specified he became the eighth Archbishop of Guadalajara in 1987, and removed the profile's precise but unsourced episcopal date ranges for Tijuana (1970-1982) and Cuernavaca (1982-1987). Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Jesús_Posadas_Ocampo.
- pedro_diaz_parada - status_2026 rewritten as uncertain. The claimed October 2024 release from Altiplano and the 2019 home-confinement report could not be verified against any reachable source; there is no English or Spanish Wikipedia article on him. Also removed the unsupported assertion in notable that he escaped 'in 1985 and 1990' as precise years, softened to 'the 1980s and in 1990'.
- pedro_diaz_parada - Coordinates changed from 16.5686, -96.1633 to 16.5586, -96.2933, which corresponds to Santa María Zoquitlán, Oaxaca, his stated birthplace and base. The prior point matched no named settlement associated with him.
- pedro_diaz_parada - Replaced the Wikipedia source URL, which returns HTTP 404 in both English and Spanish, with outlet-level sources (InSight Crime, Milenio, Proceso).
- enedina_arellano_felix - Added her current age (65) and that she graduated in accounting from a private university in Guadalajara and maintained Colombian supplier relationships. Evidence: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enedina_Arellano_Félix.
- FLAG: SESSION LIMITATION: this session's WebSearch budget (200/200 calls) was exhausted before verification began, so all checks were done with direct WebFetch of known URLs. That made Wikipedia the practical backbone of verification, contrary to the instruction to use it only as an index. Every 2024-2026 status below that could not be reached by direct URL should be re-verified against Reuters, AP, DOJ/EDNY or InSight Crime before publication.
- FLAG: miguel_angel_felix_gallardo - HIGHEST-PRIORITY DOUBT. Reuters confirms house arrest was granted on 12 September 2022 with transfer set for 13 September. The profile's claim that it was 'repeatedly suspended and never executed' is plausible and matches my recollection of later reporting, but no source reachable here confirms it, and Wikipedia's infobox says 'Transferred to house arrest'. Whether he is in Puente Grande or at home in September 2026 is genuinely unresolved. Verify before the atlas states either.
- FLAG: ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - Release date is 9 vs 10 April 2025 across sources. Wikipedia's infobox says 10 April. Needs a Mexican prison-authority (OADPRS) or AP/Reuters confirmation.
- FLAG: ernesto_fonseca_carrillo - The profile's claim that he is 'suing the Mexican state for return of assets seized in 1985' was removed as unverifiable. If a Proceso or Milenio report supports it, restore it.
- FLAG: rafael_caro_quintero - Unverified 2025-2026 claims: DOJ declining the death penalty on 5 August 2025; plea negotiations reported March 2026; trial set for 2027. Check DOJ/EDNY press releases and the EDNY docket.
- FLAG: ismael_zambada_garcia - Unverified: the ~$15 billion forfeiture figure, the 5 August 2025 death-penalty declination, his request to serve the sentence in a prison medical facility rather than a supermax, and his reported courtroom renunciation of violence. The 25 August 2025 plea and 20 July 2026 life sentence ARE confirmed. Check the EDNY sentencing documents.
- FLAG: vicente_carrillo_fuentes - Unverified: plea negotiations and an October 2026 hearing. Also note a source conflict: he was arraigned in Brooklyn (EDNY) on 28 February 2025 but his charges are described as brought in the Western District of Texas. Confirm which district holds the case.
- FLAG: hector_palma_salazar - Unverified: the reported April 2026 judicial order for emergency medical care, and his general condition. Also, the English Wikipedia article does not mention the 2023 acquittal at all, though it is widely reported in Mexican media; the acquittal-but-still-held framing should be confirmed against Animal Político, Proceso or El Universal.
- FLAG: joaquin_guzman_loera - Unverified: the reported May 2026 denial of his request for transfer to a Mexican prison. Confirm via DOJ or the EDNY/BOP record.
- FLAG: pedro_diaz_parada - Weakest profile in the set. No Wikipedia article exists in English or Spanish. His birth year, the 33-year sentence, the murdered-judge anecdote, the number and years of his escapes, the 17 January 2007 arrest and the claimed October 2024 release from Altiplano are ALL unverified here. The murdered-judge story in particular has the shape of narco folklore and should be sourced to a named court record or contemporaneous report before publication. Consider dropping the figure if it cannot be sourced.
- FLAG: benjamin_arellano_felix - Unverified: the January 2023 denial of compassionate release and the judge's reported 'ruthless and inhumane' characterisation. Removed from the profile pending a court-docket or news source.
- FLAG: miguel_caro_quintero - Birthplace conflict: Wikipedia says Caborca, Sonora; other accounts place the family, and sometimes him, at La Noria, Badiraguato, Sinaloa. I adopted Caborca and noted the Sinaloan origin. Also, the widely cited capture date of 19 December 2001 is not in the source consulted, which says only December 2001.
- FLAG: amado_carrillo_fuentes - Death date conflict: 4 July 1997 (most contemporaneous press) vs 5 July 1997 (Wikipedia). Both retained in the bio; pick one against the NYT or Proceso reporting of July 1997.
- FLAG: enrique_camarena - DEA joining year conflict: the profile and DEA accounts say 1974; Wikipedia says he joined at the Calexico office in 1973. Kept 1974. Low stakes but worth settling against dea.gov.
- FLAG: amezcua_contreras_brothers - Birth years are 0 (group entry) and the individual fates of Adán, José de Jesús and Luis Ignacio after their sentences are undocumented in any source reached. The claim that the Colima Cartel's remnants ended up 'in the Sinaloa orbit' is from a single Wikipedia line and is a broad-brush characterisation; treat as low confidence.
- FLAG: juan_garcia_abrego - Birthplace remains genuinely contested (La Paloma, Texas vs Matamoros, Tamaulipas). The 'born' field is set to 1944, which is uncontested; the birthplace ambiguity is now stated in the bio rather than resolved.
- FLAG: juan_ramon_matta_ballesteros - The date of the April 1988 Honduran seizure is commonly given as 5 April but is not confirmed in the source consulted, which says only 'April 1988'. Also, Wikipedia categorises him under 'Inmates of ADX Florence' while its body text names only USP Canaan and MCFP Springfield; the ADX period, if any, is unresolved.
- FLAG: rafael_aguilar_guajardo - Born 1950 with no month or day found; his exact birthplace within Chihuahua and his DFS rank and posting history are thinly sourced.
- FLAG: carlos_salinas_de_gortari - The claim that Swiss authorities 'linked his brother's accounts to drug protection payments' is well reported but the exact confiscated amount varies widely across accounts (figures from $74m to $114m appear). I stated the finding without a confiscation figure. Verify before adding one.
- FLAG: enedina_arellano_felix - Her status as current leader of what remains of the Tijuana organization rests on secondary reporting rather than any recent indictment or Treasury action reached here. The 'at large in 2026' claim should be checked against a 2025-2026 OFAC or DOJ document.
- FLAG: juan_nepomuceno_guerra - The 1991 brief detention 'in connection with a killing' is asserted in the profile but was not confirmed in the source consulted, which says only that he never spent more than a few hours in jail.
- FLAG: CROSS-CUTTING - Every figure's lat/lng is a plaza or base-of-operations anchor, not a birthplace or death site. Several people share a single city coordinate (four figures on Guadalajara; three on Culiacán; three on Ciudad Juárez). If the atlas plots them as points they will overlap and will also misrepresent, for example, Matta-Ballesteros, a Honduran assigned to Guadalajara, and Camarena, a victim rather than an operator. Consider adding a separate coordinate-role field.
- FLAG: CROSS-CUTTING - Several 'notable' bullets state contested attributions as settled fact, notably that Amado Carrillo Fuentes ordered the Aguilar Guajardo killing and that Guzmán arranged Ramón Arellano Félix's fatal traffic stop. Both are sourced to reporting and to Zambada's trial testimony respectively, not to convictions. I have hedged these in the bios but the atlas UI should not render them as adjudicated.
- edgar_valdez_villarreal - sentence corrected from '49 years and three months' to '49 years and one month', and sentencing date specified as 11 June 2018 - es.wikipedia Edgar Valdez Villarreal: 'El 11 de junio de 2018 fue dictada la sentencia... una condena de 49 años un mes'
- edgar_valdez_villarreal - status_2026 corrected: he has not been listed in Bureau of Prisons custody since November 2022 (previously at USP Coleman II), so 'Imprisoned in the US' was overstated - en.wikipedia/es.wikipedia both note BOP lists him as no longer in its custody as of Nov 2022
- antonio_ezequiel_cardenas_guillen - casualty figures corrected from 'two marines and a journalist' to the official toll of three marines, one soldier, four gunmen and journalist Carlos Alberto Guajardo Romero - en.wikipedia Antonio Ezequiel Cárdenas Guillén
- antonio_ezequiel_cardenas_guillen - reward attribution clarified as a US State Department reward of up to $5 million - en.wikipedia
- heriberto_lazcano - leadership dates corrected: he did not take command directly on Guzmán Decena's death in Nov 2002; Rogelio González Pizaña (Z-2) led until his October 2004 capture, after which Lazcano became undisputed leader. Role field changed from 'from 2002' to 'from 2004' - en.wikipedia Heriberto Lazcano
- heriberto_lazcano - desertion date specified as 27 March 1998 rather than vague 'late 1990s' - en.wikipedia
- arturo_guzman_decena - wreath detail corrected: the documented floral tribute read that he would always be in their hearts, signed 'from your family, Los Zetas', not that he was 'boss of bosses of the special forces' - en.wikipedia Arturo Guzmán Decena
- arturo_guzman_decena - GAFE service specified as c.1992-1997 with the rank of lieutenant; recruit count tightened from 'roughly thirty to forty' to about 38 - en.wikipedia
- arturo_guzman_decena - Chiapas claim softened from 'served in operations against the Zapatista insurgency in 1994' to deployment in the army's Chiapas operations following the 1994 uprising, since his GAFE service began c.1992 and the specific 1994 deployment is not firmly documented
- miguel_angel_trevino_morales - joining date corrected: he was recruited into the Gulf Cartel orbit by Osiel Cárdenas in the late 1990s and rose within Los Zetas in the early 2000s, not 'joined Los Zetas around 2005' - en.wikipedia Miguel Treviño Morales
- miguel_angel_trevino_morales - capture location refined to near Anáhuac, Nuevo León, outside Nuevo Laredo - en.wikipedia
- miguel_angel_trevino_morales - status_2026 made precise: in US custody since the 27 Feb 2025 transfer with no publicly confirmed guilty plea or sentence; 'awaiting trial' removed as unverified - no US plea/sentencing found in any checked source as of Sept 2026
- omar_trevino_morales - status_2026 same correction: 'awaiting trial' replaced with 'no guilty plea or sentence publicly confirmed as of September 2026' - no source found confirming US proceedings
- omar_trevino_morales - added that he was formally charged in Mexico on 13 March 2015 in Toluca, and that the 2008 DC indictment covered abductions and murders in Nuevo Laredo 2005-2006 - en.wikipedia Omar Treviño Morales
- teodoro_garcia_simental - causality corrected: his April 2008 defection followed the power struggle with Luis Fernando Sánchez Arellano; Eduardo Arellano Félix was not captured until October 2008, so his capture cannot have precipitated the April split - en.wikipedia Teodoro García Simental
- teodoro_garcia_simental - leader's name corrected to Luis Fernando Sánchez Arellano - en.wikipedia
- genaro_garcia_luna - birth date specified as 10 July 1968, Mexico City - en.wikipedia Genaro García Luna
- genaro_garcia_luna - sentence changed from '38 years and one month' to '38 years'; sentencing date specified as 16 October 2024 - en.wikipedia (38 years, release date 19 July 2052)
- genaro_garcia_luna - civil judgment amount and date made precise: about $2.448 billion ordered on 22 May 2025 - en.wikipedia
- genaro_garcia_luna - AFI directorship start specified as November 2001 - en.wikipedia
- enrique_pena_nieto - corrected the claim that he replaced the federal police apparatus with a Gendarmería; the Gendarmería was created in 2014 as a division within the Federal Police, which continued to exist until 2019
- alfredo_beltran_leyva - birthplace refined to La Palma, Badiraguato, Sinaloa; guilty plea date 23 February 2016 and sentencing 5 April 2017 confirmed - en.wikipedia Alfredo Beltrán Leyva
- hector_beltran_leyva - added transfer to Altiplano on 6 October 2014 and the death location, the Adolfo López Mateos medical centre in Toluca; alias 'El General' added to bio - en.wikipedia Héctor Beltrán Leyva
- arturo_beltran_leyva - operation detail corrected: about 200 marines with two Mi-17 helicopters and armoured vehicles; the reprisal against Melquisedec Angulo Córdova's family occurred the day after his 21 December 2009 funeral - en.wikipedia Arturo Beltrán Leyva
- arturo_beltran_leyva - 'eldest of four brothers' softened to 'eldest of the brothers', as sources list five Beltrán Leyva brothers (Marcos Arturo, Carlos, Alfredo, Héctor, Mario Alberto)

Several DOJ press releases and BBC/news URLs returned empty or 404. Primary-source confirmation from InSight Crime, Reuters, AP and DOJ was not obtainable for the 2025-2026 items below.
- FLAG: miguel_angel_trevino_morales / omar_trevino_morales - no source consulted documents any US arraignment, guilty plea, or sentencing after the 27 February 2025 transfer. Wikipedia articles current to July 2026 record nothing beyond the transfer. Status is stated conservatively; a DOJ/EDNY docket check is needed before publication, since a 2025-2026 plea is plausible and would change status_2026.
- FLAG: edgar_valdez_villarreal - his absence from BOP records since November 2022 is consistent with cooperation or witness protection but is not confirmed as such. Do not assert he is 'in prison' without a current BOP or court check.
- FLAG: sergio_villarreal_barragan - whether he has been sentenced in the US, and whether he remains in custody or in witness protection, is not publicly documented. Wikipedia still categorises him as a US federal prisoner while also noting possible protected-witness status. Unresolved.
- FLAG: vicente_zambada_niebla - reports of his release and entry into witness protection are widely repeated but not officially confirmed; his 15-year sentence with credit from 2009 would have expired around 2024. Treat 'whereabouts undisclosed' as the safe formulation.
- FLAG: jose_luis_abarca - the widely circulated claim that he was sentenced in 2019 (figures of 92 years are reported in Mexican media) for the kidnapping and murder of activist Arturo Hernández Cardona could not be confirmed, nor could any later annulment or acquittal. Removed from the profile pending a Proceso/Animal Político/El Universal check.
- FLAG: zhenli_ye_gon - no source consulted reports the outcome of his Mexican prosecution after the October 2016 extradition. Whether he has been convicted, acquitted, or released as of 2026 is unknown; 'no final conviction publicly confirmed' is an inference from silence, not a verified fact.
- FLAG: armando_valencia_cornelio - born year left as 0 (unknown); no source consulted gives a birth year. His reported early release around July 2020 and cancer diagnosis rest on a single thinly sourced line in the Milenio Cartel article and should be corroborated before publication. He may be deceased.
- FLAG: teodoro_garcia_simental - no source consulted documents a Mexican conviction, sentence, or any extradition request after his January 2010 capture. 'Imprisoned in Mexico' rests on the absence of contrary reporting; a Mexican court-record check is advisable.
- FLAG: heriberto_lazcano - the state/coordinate pair (TAM, 27.4761/-99.5164 = Nuevo Laredo) sits oddly with a figure born in Apan, Hidalgo, based largely in Coahuila and Nuevo León, and killed in Progreso, Coahuila. Consider relocating the pin to Coahuila. Left unchanged to avoid unrequested schema churn.
- FLAG: omar_trevino_morales - the Los Zetas overview article gives his arrest as 3 March 2015 while his own article gives 4 March 2015 at about 4 a.m. Retained 4 March 2015, which matches the detailed account and contemporaneous reporting.
- FLAG: arturo_guzman_decena - his death is variously described as occurring in a restaurant in Matamoros or while visiting a mistress; the 21 vs 22 November 2002 date also varies within Wikipedia. Retained 21 November 2002 and dropped the restaurant specificity from the notable list.
- FLAG: genaro_garcia_luna - some reporting gives his sentence as 460 months (38 years 4 months) rather than a flat 38 years. The original '38 years and one month' was not supported; a DOJ judgment check would settle the exact figure.
- FLAG: osiel_cardenas_guillen - the releasing facility (USP Terre Haute) comes from the Spanish Wikipedia article only; some US reporting placed him in a Louisiana facility. Low-confidence detail.
- FLAG: felipe_calderon - the '60,000 to 100,000 drug-related deaths' range is an estimate spanning contested methodologies; it is presented as a range but should be attributed to a specific source (e.g. Justice in Mexico or INEGI) in the published atlas.

- celso_ortega_jimenez - brothers' roles corrected: Jorge Iván Ortega Sánchez 'El Barco' is second-in-command and Antonio Ortega Jiménez 'La Parota' third, replacing the unsupported 'finances' and 'operations' assignments; Tixtla added to the controlled municipalities - insightcrime.org/mexico-organized-crime-news/los-ardillos/
- celso_ortega_jimenez - the father's death detail '26 January 2011 in Tlanicuilulco' softened to murdered in 2011, and the 17 January 2020 Chilapa attack restated as the January 2020 killing of ten indigenous Nahua musicians from Alcozacán - insightcrime.org profile

- hurtado_olascoaga_brothers - the US$8 million reward broken out correctly as US$5 million for Johnny 'El Pez' and US$3 million for José Alfredo 'El Fresa' - es.wikipedia.org/wiki/José_Alfredo_Hurtado_Olascoaga gives US$3 million for El Fresa
- hurtado_olascoaga_brothers - the precise takeover date '1 July 2014' softened to 2014, the '18 March 2021' Coatepec Harinas date softened to March 2021, the '16 April 2025' indictment and '22 April 2025' sanctions dates softened to April 2025, and 'at least 35 municipalities' softened to dozens, as none of these precise figures are corroborated; Morelos added to their area of operation - es.wikipedia.org/wiki/José_Alfredo_Hurtado_Olascoaga
- andres_manuel_lopez_obrador - National Guard creation dated precisely: approved by Congress in February 2019 and formally established on 30 June 2019 - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Andrés_Manuel_López_Obrador

- claudia_sheinbaum - the border deployment is dated 3 February 2025 (announcement) rather than vaguely 'February 2025', and the tariff threat specified as 25 percent - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Claudia_Sheinbaum
- omar_garcia_harfuch - father Javier García Paniagua's fuller record added (PRI president and labour secretary as well as DFS chief) and mother identified as actress María Sorté; Morena membership dated to September 2023 - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_García_Harfuch
- omar_garcia_harfuch - the precise claim that fourteen attackers were arrested and twelve convicted was replaced with 'dozens of participants were later arrested and prosecuted', as the figures are uncorroborated; the 2020 ambush detail 'more than four hundred rounds' added
- omar_garcia_harfuch - 'El Jardinero' capture located near El Mirador, Nayarit, and described as a senior CJNG figure who was mobilising to seize control after El Mencho's death - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_in_Mexico and en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalisco_New_Generation_Cartel

- ruben_rocha_moya - the Zambada letter date '10 August 2024' softened to August 2024, and the pamphlet campaign no longer dated to October 2024, both being uncorroborated precise dates
- carlos_manzo - his prior office made accurate: he was a Morena federal deputy for Michoacán's ninth district 2021-2024 who resigned in February 2024 and won the mayoralty with about 66 percent as an independent - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Manzo
- carlos_manzo - the July 2026 arrest upgraded from 'a suspect' to Ramón Ángel Álvarez Ayala 'R1', alleged mastermind and leader of the CJNG-linked cell Los Rs; added that seven of his eight bodyguards were arrested for negligence - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Manzo

- donald_trump - removed the unverified 24 July 2026 additional 10 percent tariff, the 8 January 2026 threat to strike land targets in Mexico, the May 2026 CNN report of CIA operations inside Mexico, and the reference to NSPM-7, which concerns domestic terrorism rather than cartels; replaced with the general and sourced statement that his administration repeatedly floated unilateral action - en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump

- javier_valdez_cardenas - the precise gunmen sentencing dates 'February 2020' and '17 June 2021' were retained but the day-level precision on the second was dropped to June 2021, and the claim that a third suspect was found murdered in Sonora in September 2017 was removed as uncorroborated
- multiple profiles (nemesio_oseguera_cervantes, juan_jose_farias_alvarez, hurtado_olascoaga_brothers, juan_gerardo_trevino_chavez, donald_trump, claudia_sheinbaum) - the FTO designation date '20 February 2025' softened to 'February 2025' because sources conflict: the Sheinbaum article and State Department listing give 20 February while '2025 in Mexico' gives 19 February for the US action and 20 February for Canada's matching designation
- FLAG: FTO designation date ambiguity: Secretary Rubio signed the designation of the six Mexican cartels on 19 February 2025 and the Federal Register notice took effect 20 February 2025. Wikipedia's '2025 in Mexico' says 19 February (US) and 20 February (Canada); the Sheinbaum presidency article says 20 February for the US. Left as 'February 2025' throughout; pick one convention before publication.
- FLAG: antonio_oseguera_cervantes: the claimed 31 July 2026 guilty plea with a 15-year minimum could not be verified. The supplied CBS News URL is a 404 and no other reachable source mentions it. Treat as unconfirmed; his current legal posture in the US needs a fresh check.
- FLAG: juan_gerardo_trevino_chavez 'El Huevo': the November 2024 guilty plea to all fifteen counts is plausible but unverified here (Border Report source returned 403). Needs confirmation from a DOJ/US Attorney SDTX or WDTX release before publication.
- FLAG: damaso_lopez_serrano 'Mini Lic': the December 2024 re-arrest for attempted fentanyl trafficking and a February 2026 five-year sentence are both unverified. His Wikipedia entries (EN and ES) do not exist, and the Mexico News Daily URL is a 404.
- FLAG: jose_antonio_yepez_ortiz 'El Marro' and roberto_moyado_esparza 'El Betito' both carried an identical claim of 'an additional eight-year federal sentence in April 2025'. The duplication strongly suggests a fabricated or mis-propagated detail. Both removed; verify independently if you want to restore either.
- FLAG: ivan_archivaldo_guzman_salazar: the DEA most-wanted listing (February 2026), the ICE reward campaign (July 2026) and reported surrender negotiations (May 2026) are all unverified. His Wikipedia article carries no updates past August 2024.
- FLAG: aureliano_guzman_loera: the US$5 million reward is well attested but the announcement date of 8 November 2021 could not be confirmed (El País and Vice sources unreachable). Also unverified: capture operations against him in December 2025 and April 2026 in which ~10 of his men were detained.
- FLAG: rosalinda_gonzalez_valencia: birth year is genuinely disputed. Sources give 1962, 1963, 1964 or 1965; InSight Crime says 1963, which is retained. Her post-February-2026 whereabouts are unreported rather than confirmed 'unknown'.
- FLAG: juan_carlos_valencia_gonzalez alias conflict: InSight Crime gives 'El 03'; the CJNG Wikipedia article gives 'El Pelón'. Both retained. His succession as CJNG leader rests on WSJ (18 March 2026) and El País (6 April 2026) reporting and is contested by the Audias Flores Silva faction; not a settled fact.
- FLAG: hernan_bermudez_requena: Paraguay arrest date conflicts between sources (12 September per his own article, 14 September per '2025 in Mexico'). The 17 September 2025 expulsion and Altiplano transfer are consistent across sources.
- FLAG: rodrigo_paez_quintero: the Caborca Cartel Wikipedia article names the 30 April 2023 Guadalajara arrestee as 'Rodrigo Omar, alias El R' without explicitly confirming he is Rodrigo Páez Quintero. The identification and the mid-July 2024 extradition to Arizona both need a primary source (DOJ District of Arizona).
- FLAG: jesus_alfredo_guzman_salazar: the Kingpin Act designation year is genuinely unclear. The original profile said 2012; his Wikipedia article dates US sanctions on both brothers to 2025. The year was removed rather than guessed. Verify against OFAC's designation list.
- FLAG: andres_manuel_lopez_obrador: the ~190,000 homicide total for his six-year term is a widely repeated approximation, not an official figure; INEGI and SESNSP counts differ (roughly 189,000-199,000 depending on source and cutoff). Retained as 'roughly'.
- FLAG: ismael_zambada_sicairos: birth year ~1982 is approximate ('born around 1982' per InSight Crime). The 9 September 2024 start date for the civil war is the conventional date but was not confirmed in the sources reachable here; softened to September 2024.
- FLAG: donald_trump: the 'state' field is set to 'US', which is not an ISO 3166-2:MX code. This is intentional for a non-Mexican figure but will need special handling in the atlas's state filter.
- FLAG: Web search quota for this session was exhausted before verification began; all checks were done with direct page fetches. Several outlets (FBI.gov, State.gov, El País English, Tucson Sentinel, Border Report) returned 403 or were unreachable, so a number of 2025-2026 items rest on Wikipedia and InSight Crime alone. A second pass with working search and access to DOJ, Treasury/OFAC and Reuters primary sources is recommended before publication.
